# Session 1 {{float:"right" 2025-01-26}}
{wide}

## 1 - Road to Greenest
_~18:00, 22 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. Eguzkilore is camped at a roadside fire, cooking a roe deer he hunted on a spit. The smell carries.
2. Drawn by the cooking smell, the rest of the party arrives in turn:
   - Nuberroca approaches first, hungry; Eguzkilore shares the food.
   - Sinsong arrives next. At some point during the gathering he casts a spell — his bald head glows brightly with the casting. Nuberroca, struck by the light, nicknames him *"Cabezabombilla"* (lightbulb-head) on the spot — the name sticks.
   - Nyx and Amidamaru arrive together — they had crossed paths earlier on the road and were already traveling as a pair.
   - Mialee arrives last, alone.
3. Over the meal, Nuberroca shares his mission: his alpine tribe's elders sent him out to investigate the rise of the undead in the pass. In response, Sinsong gives Nuberroca a vial of holy water, telling him it may help against the undead. Nuberroca pockets it to show the elders later — he doesn't know what it is, but takes Sinsong's word for its use.
4. The group decides to head to Greenest together. Approaching at dusk, they see the town under attack — fires across the rooftops, smoke rising, raiders in the streets. A **blue dragon** is visible in the sky, throwing lightning.
5. The whole party is *awestruck* by the sight of the dragon — its size and majesty, even as it kills.

## 2 - The looted house

1. The party moves into the town streets, looking to help where they can, and finds a townhouse being looted by a mixed group of kobolds and cultists, some of the cultists wearing Greenest guard livery.
2. Counting roughly half a dozen looters, the party sets up a stealth approach — the plan is to take them before any can call out and bring more raiders down on the street.
3. Sinsong casts a spell to prepare for the rush; his bald head flares with the casting and the light spills out into the street, giving away the party's position before they can move. **Stealth blown.**
4. Nuberroca charges first, sprinting through the doorway before the raiders can rally. Despite the lost stealth, the rest of the party still catches the looters by surprise — the goliath crashing in pins their attention long enough for the others to close.
5. The fight is short. The kobolds break and scatter, picked off by precise arrows as they run. The liveried cultists are cut down at the doorway. The house is cleared in under a minute, the looters dead.

## 3 - A desperate family
_~19:00, 22 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. In the town streets, while trying to help where they can, the party comes upon a family pinned by a kobold pack: a wounded mother holding the kobolds off to buy time, while her badly wounded husband herds their three children toward safety.
2. The mother is overwhelmed and killed before the party can close the distance — the kobolds drag her down while the heroes are still moving in.
3. The party engages, with the priority of keeping the father and the children alive.
4. Eguzkilore and Mialee loose arrows from cover, dropping kobolds before they can reach the children.
5. Nuberroca crashes into the front of the pack, axe swinging. Amidamaru pushes in behind him with his sword, finishing off the kobolds the goliath's charge knocks aside.
6. A kobold breaks through to the father; the badly wounded husband manages to fend it off himself.
7. Nyx rushes to help him and puts herself between the man and the kobold.
8. Sinsong swings his mace to help — and fumbles, the strike landing on Nyx instead of the kobold.
9. The kobolds are swiftly defeated, but the party comes out of it bloodied. The children and the husband huddle around the dead woman, weeping. The party gathers around them, offering what comfort they can while quietly acknowledging that they cannot linger here — the streets are still hostile, and the keep is the only safe place left.
10. The husband composes himself enough to introduce himself as **Cuth**, then echoes the urgency: they must make for the keep without delay. The party falls in with Cuth and the three children and presses on.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 1}}
\page

## 4 - River-bank skirmish

1. The most direct route to the keep is clogged with raiders — too many to push through with **Cuth** and his three children in tow. The party detours toward the river, hoping to skirt the worst of the fighting along the bank.
2. The detour brings them in sight of a patrol crossing the river to flank the keep approach: a band of kobolds with two large drakes wading the shallows alongside them.
3. The party tucks Cuth and the three children behind cover and moves to ambush the patrol before it can spot them.
4. Mialee takes an elevated vantage on the river bank and opens with her bow from cover.
5. Eguzkilore looses arrows alongside her, dropping the leading kobolds before they understand they're being shot at.
6. Nuberroca and Amidamaru break for the drakes — the most dangerous threats in the patrol — and meet them in the river itself. Nuberroca splashes into the shallows, water sloshing around his thighs, and brings one drake down in two heavy axe strokes. Amidamaru, more at home in water than on land, slips alongside the second with his sword, working the joints below the waterline, and finishes it as it tries to turn on him.
7. Nyx slips through the bushes along the shoreline, working around the patrol's flank, and drops a kobold from behind as it tries to bring its sling up.
8. With the drakes down and the flank broken, the patrol shatters: most kobolds turn to flee across the river, while two scrabble up the near bank to get at Mialee.
9. Mialee drops one of the climbers before the other reaches her vantage and wounds her at close range.
10. Sinsong moves up the bank and, with a heavy mace strike to the back, drops the kobold pressing Mialee.
11. The remaining kobolds break and run for the far bank, vanishing into the smoke.
12. The party retrieves Cuth and the children from cover and presses on toward the keep, the family in tow.

\column

## 5 - Inside the keep
_~20:00, 22 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. The party reaches the keep gates with Cuth and his three children. The guards admit them, take charge of the family, and direct the party on to the governor.
2. Before heading inside, one of the heroes looks back from the wall: the raiders have closed a ring around the keep. The way they came in has shut behind them. The party is *locked in*.
3. **Tarbaw Nighthill**, governor of Greenest, receives them in worse shape than the title suggests — a man in his sixties with half his face wrapped in bandages, his right arm in a sling, and his pale blue tunic stained dark down one side. He has clearly had no time for more than the roughest first aid. He thanks the party for the rescue and gets straight to the situation.
4. Tarbaw lays out the situation plainly: the keep is ringed, and they need a way out — for messengers, for any defensive sortie, and for civilians still trying to reach the keep from outside. He mentions one option: an old tunnel under the keep that leads beyond the raider line. It has not been used in years and is reportedly infested with vermin; it has not been cleared.
5. At Tarbaw's call, his castellan steps forward: **Escobert the Red**, a shield dwarf whose tangled mop of bright red hair has earned him his name, an enormous ring of iron and brass keys jangling at his belt — he holds every lock in the keep. Escobert leads the party down to the tunnel entrance and hands them the keys.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 1}}
\page

## 6 - The Old Tunnel

1. The tunnel was built generations ago as a covert way to fetch water from the river during sieges — a covered passage running from the keep cellars down to the riverbank. Along its length, three small storage rooms are carved into the wall; at the far end, a collapsed section of stone opens into a small cave the original masons never sealed off.
2. The party descends with **Escobert**'s keys. The air is *dark, thick, and stale*, and the tunnel is alive with vermin: giant rats scurrying underfoot, and spider swarms boiling out of every corner as the lanterns reach them.
3. The party works through the tunnel. Eguzkilore and Mialee pick off the giant rats with arrows from a few paces back; Nuberroca crushes the rest at close range. When the spider swarms boil too close, Amidamaru drives them back with fire from his hand — bolts hissing into the dark, the swarms recoiling rather than pressing. The party clears most of the tunnel this way.
4. The fight is ugly even with the strategy — close work in a confined passage, weapons fouling on each other. Sinsong swings at a rat in the press and clips Mialee in the head with his mace instead.
5. They push into the first of the storage rooms. As the door opens, giant spiders drop from the ceiling — the room is a *long-undisturbed nest*, and the giants pour out in numbers the party did not expect.
6. Sinsong casts Sleep on the lead giant spider. The spell lands; the giant folds where it stands. The rest of the party closes and kills it in melee while it is helpless — a clean victory on the first of them.
7. The fight in the room turns into a brawl. Eguzkilore peppers one of the giants with arrows from the doorway until it is bristling with shafts, slowed and bleeding; Nuberroca cleaves the wounded creature down with his axe. Amidamaru's fire keeps the smaller spiders at bay so the others can work.
8. Another giant spider lands a bite on Nyx, and she goes down under the venom — when the others can next glance her way, she is breathing shallowly but holding, no longer bleeding out.
9. Mialee, still reeling from the earlier mace blow, takes a bite of her own and goes down beside Nyx, bleeding hard, the press of spiders giving no one room to reach her.
10. ***[EPIC]*** The fight bleeds back out into the tunnel. As Sinsong shifts to support the line, a spider swarm boils over him in a rush — fangs and venom finding every gap — and he is *dead before anyone can reach him*.
11. The party breaks for the keep, leaving the storage room behind. Four still on their feet, every one of them heavily wounded — Nuberroca and Amidamaru both a single bad hit from going down themselves:
    - Nuberroca scoops up Sinsong's body and Mialee, one slung over each shoulder — Mialee's bleeding has stopped at some point in the chaos, no one quite sure when.
    - Amidamaru picks up Nyx and falls in behind him.
    - Eguzkilore retreats last, alone, arrows nocked, covering the others as they go.
12. As the party clears the tunnel mouth, the last of the giant spiders withdraw deeper into the dark — both sides disengage. The lanterns' light pulls back; the tunnel goes quiet behind them.

## 7 - Aftermath
_~21:00, 22 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. The party stumbles back into the keep with their dead and wounded. Sinsong's body is laid out in the hall; the others triage the living — Mialee and Nyx still unconscious, Nuberroca and Amidamaru on their feet but bleeding from a dozen lesser wounds.
2. Bringing Sinsong back becomes the only thing that matters. The party fans out through the keep, asking every guard, healer, and refugee they pass — is there a cleric here, someone with the power to bring a dead man back?
3. They find one. A cleric stationed at the keep hears them out and agrees to perform the rite — for **600 gp**.
4. The party does not have the gold. They argue, plead, empty their pouches in front of him — it isn't even close. After a long look at Sinsong's body and at the exhausted handful still standing over it, the cleric agrees to perform the rite *on credit*.
5. The rite is performed. Sinsong jolts upright with a strangled scream, clawing at his face and arms as if dragging spiders off them, shouting about the dark and the legs and the bites. It takes the party a long minute to bring him back to his senses.
6. With Sinsong breathing again and the wounded patched up, the party finds a quiet corner of the keep and rests for a while. They eat what little they can, see to their wounds, and talk over what just happened — how badly the tunnel went, what they could have done differently, what they need to be sharper at next time. By the end of the hour the change is unmistakable — their hands are steadier than the rest alone could explain, their wounds are closing visibly, and the lessons of the disaster are pressed into them.

{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 1}}

# Session 2 {{float:"right" 2025-02-02}}
{wide}

## 1 - Finishing the tunnel
_~22:00, 22 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. The heroes are at the keep, picking up after the tunnel disaster and Sinsong's resurrection. The night has not let up; the assault is still pressing on Greenest from every side.
2. **Tarbaw** briefs them: civilians are sheltering in the temple of Chauntea; the church is under attack and needs relief. Watching the party still bandaged from the tunnel disaster, he presses three of the keep's last healing potions on them before sending them out.
3. The party returns to the lower tunnel and finishes clearing it of the residual giant rats and stray spiders left from the disastrous spider-nest fight.
4. The tunnel is now fully cleared and rehabilitated as a covert way in and out, beyond the raider line.
5. Sinsong, still recovering, remains at the keep helping the wounded. Eguzkilore takes the battlements, pinning raiders to cover his friends' sortie.
6. The party emerges from the cleared tunnel and runs straight into a small kobold patrol.
7. The fight is short. Nuberroca subdues one kobold *alive* — pinning rather than killing — and questions it on the spot.
8. ***[EPIC]*** The kobold is collaborative but renders barely any useful information. Rather than dispatch it, Nuberroca restrains the kobold and keeps it as a *"pet"*, strapped to his chest like a baby.
9. Approaching the temple, Nyx and Mialee scout ahead in good stealth; Nuberroca trails slightly behind with Amidamaru and the restrained kobold. The goliath's bulk forces him to hang back rather than lead, and even there his heavy footfalls keep threatening to give them away.
10. The two scouts carry the party past further patrols unseen.

## 2 - Sanctuary rescue
_~23:00, 22 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. The temple of Chauntea is surrounded by an external wall, already breached at the front by the time the party arrives. From the outside, they can see a loose patrol circling the perimeter — three cultists, ten kobolds, and two ambush drakes, dispersed.
2. The party closes on the wall as soon as the patrol leaves the area. Mialee climbs onto Nuberroca to peer into the temple compound, and from the elevated angle she spots two more enemy groups:
   - At the back door, four cultists and four kobolds — clustered tightly — are trying to burn it down, failing miserably and producing a prodigious smoke cloud that obscures the area.
   - At the front door across the compound, a menacing-looking cultist leader directs two cultists working a battering ram against the temple door, with six kobolds standing guard around them.
3. The rhythmic thuds of the ram already echo through the temple — the party can hear them clearly even before Mialee gets eyes on the front-door group.
4. The party scales the perimeter wall at the rear, falls on the arson detail, and kills them swiftly under cover of the cultists' own smoke. The ram keeps battering the main door.
5. The cuts the cultists landed on Nuberroca start closing with a *blue glow* while Mialee strips the robes off two of the dead cultists — one for herself and a spare for a partner — before pushing in.
6. Inside the temple, the civilians are panicked; calming them takes several minutes, and the heroes succeed only with the help of **Eadyan Falconmoon**, the half-elf priest in charge of the temple. Through the whole dialogue, the front-door ram keeps beating, each strike echoing through the sanctuary — a constant reminder that they are still in danger.
7. As the group is finishing the extraction through the back door, the main door finally gives way in a shower of splinters — the cultist leader's ram team is breaking through. At almost the same moment, outside, the loose patrol has drifted dangerously close to the back door.
8. Mialee creates a magical illusion at the main entry, pulling the attention of both cultist groups back toward the temple's entrance. The party uses the gap to slip out the back of the temple.
9. At the perimeter wall, Amidamaru and Nuberroca lift and steady the civilians across between them, one by one — slow but unobserved. Up close, the kobold strapped to Nuberroca's chest sets off fresh panic in some of the civilians as the goliath hoists them up.
10. Reaching the river with all civilians unharmed, the group heads back along the stream toward the keep.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 2}}
\page

## 3 - Ambush in the river
_~00:00, 23 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. The way back follows the stream in the dead of night. Frogs croak in the shallows. The only visible lights are the distant fires and a luminous stone Amidamaru gave Nuberroca to keep them from stumbling in the dark.
2. Almost back at the tunnel mouth, the stone gives them away: a cultist patrol — five kobolds and three cultists directed by an acolyte — spots the light and ambushes the party.
3. The opening is brutal: kobolds fire their slings from cover along the bank while cultists rush in from concealed positions among the trees. The party is caught flat-footed.
4. Panic takes the civilians — some plunge into the shallows, others crash into the underbrush along the bank. **Eadyan** tries to hold them together, but the chaos outruns him; within breaths, **half the group is lost in the dark**.
5. Nuberroca answers with a counter-charge, axe up, the cloth-strapped kobold on his chest squealing as the goliath crashes into the lead cultists. Nyx moves closer to the goliath, shooting arrows as she goes.
6. The patrol's first blows land hard on the charging goliath, but a *blue glow* runs across each fresh cut and closes it partway.
7. The barbarian's charge halts when the acolyte commands him to retreat. With his *body betraying him*, the goliath falls back, leaving Nyx exposed.
8. In Nuberroca's absence the patrol surges around Nyx — kobolds and cultists pile on the small ranger, landing hits before she can give ground.
9. Nyx drops the lead kobold with a precise thrust of her shortsword, but the rest keep coming; she holds, bloodied and pressed.
10. Amidamaru intervenes to save Nyx, wading in beside her. He cuts down two kobolds in the surge before a cultist drives a spear into him and drops him.
11. Nuberroca recovers his senses, downing the acolyte with a precise axe throw as an act of revenge.
12. Hidden along the bank, Mialee picks her moment and subdues one of the remaining cultists with a clean knockout. With the acolyte dead and one of theirs taken, the rest of the patrol breaks for the trees.
13. Nuberroca hurls a hand-axe after one of the fleeing cultists; the throw buries itself between the shoulder blades and drops him short of the treeline.
14. With the fight done, the goliath turns to Amidamaru — the artificer's bleeding has stopped at some point in the chaos, breath shallow but holding.
\column
15. Sizing up Amidamaru's deadweight, the goliath decides he can't carry both the artificer and the kobold strapped to his chest. He rips the bundled kobold off and hurls it up into the high branches of a nearby tree — out of reach, out of earshot, unable to alert anyone.
16. Only as he stoops to lift Amidamaru does the goliath see the obvious: the stream will carry the weight for him. He ties a rope around the artificer and drags him through the shallows, the current bearing what his shoulders no longer have to — the kobold-throw, in hindsight, unnecessary.
17. The party rounds up the scattered civilians, returning to the keep with the rescued people, the captured cultist, and the dragged Amidamaru.

## 4 - Recovery at the keep

1. Inside the keep the air is loud with the night's wounded — soldiers and townsfolk alike. **Eadyan** and the rescued civilians are taken in; healers move to those who need them most.
2. Sinsong, helping at the keep tonight rather than in the field, meets the party as they bring the cultist in and takes the prisoner's robe — disguise material for whatever the night still has.
3. The cultist is then handed to **Escobert the Red**, who sets to interrogation in one of the cellars below the great hall.
4. The party finds a quiet corner and takes a short rest. This one is *unusually restorative*: aches fade faster than they should, lesser cuts close on their own, and old wounds feel lighter by the end of the hour. The party comes out of it cleaner than anticipated.
5. Once rested, they take the time to restock and reset. Keep quartermasters refill ammunition; Nuberroca gets a fresh pair of hand-axes; the smiths patch dented armour and re-edge chipped weapons; the party tends to the wounds the night left them.
6. Refreshed and rearmed, the party climbs to the keep's battlements to find Tarbaw and learn what the night still wants of them.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 2}}
\page

## 5 - Dragon attack
_~01:30, 23 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. The party reaches **Tarbaw** at the parapet, mid-conversation about the night's run of missions, when one of the watch guards shouts the warning — *the dragon is coming* — his voice fraying with terror as he points to the sky.
2. The blue dragon arrives fast, opening from above with a *lightning breath* that sweeps the rooftop in a single arc, leaving a scorched line in the stone and the remains of a guard smoldering.
3. The dragon dives in for its first close pass, sweeping through the battlements: it lashes with its claws, snatches one guard from the parapet and devours him without slowing, raking Tarbaw in passing — face cuts re-opened, blood pouring.
4. By the end of that first pass two of the keep's guards are dead and several more wounded; the parapet is slick with blood.
5. Mialee, frightened beyond reason, presses herself behind cover and runs down the stairs, choosing her safety over the fight.
6. Nuberroca answers with axe and rage, charging into the open to challenge the dragon directly. Nyx and Amidamaru engage from the parapet with everything they have, slowly overcoming their fears.
7. Challenged, the dragon pulls back to range. Most of its attacks come at distance now — the lightning breath sweeping the rooftop again and again.
8. Between passes the party keeps firing on it: Amidamaru looses arrow after arrow, Nuberroca hurls hand-axes when its arcs bring it within reach, and the keep guards work their crossbows from the wall. Most of the volley shatters against the dragon's powerful scales — arrows snapping, bolts skittering off into the dark, hand-axes chiming like struck anvils.
9. ***[EPIC]*** Nyx, sighting between the dragon's passes, threads a longbow shot into one of its eyes — a *precise arrow* that scores where the scales don't shield. The dragon pulls higher with a roar of rage, blood streaking the side of its head.
10. On the next close pass, the dragon dives in to rake Nuberroca's flank with its claws before pulling up; the goliath's retaliating axe-stroke opens a cut along its foreleg as it banks away.
11. After a lightning discharge that only wounds one guard, the dragon dives in again, snatching another guard from the parapet — a snapping bite, and the man is gone.
12. On its next pass, the dragon slashes Nuberroca again and lifts a guard off the wall, hurling him over the parapet — the man falls to his death on the ground far below.
13. With the parapet near-empty around them and another claw-rake bearing in, the surviving party retreats off the rooftop into the stairwell down to the keep — the doorway narrow enough to deny the dragon's bulk.
14. The dragon presses against the stair opening from above, forcing a foreleg through, claws raking blindly for the heroes as they crouch below the threshold.
15. Nuberroca, axe braced under the lintel, lands a clean stroke on the reaching claw — not deep, but enough to draw blood and sting.
16. ***[EPIC]*** Bleeding from the eye-shot, the foreleg cut, and now the wound on its claw, the dragon pulls its leg back out of the stairwell, looks down at the heroes saying *"esperaba a un grupo de héroes, no me han pagado para esto"*, and turns away from the village.
17. The party watches it vanish into the night, the keep still standing.
18. They climb back up onto the rooftop and turn to the wreckage — wounded guards strewn across the parapet, the dead among them, and Tarbaw unconscious in his own blood — and set to tending whoever can still be saved.
19. As the party works through the parapet's wounded, Tarbaw recovers quickly — by the time they lift him clear, his bleeding has stopped and his eyes are flickering open.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 2}}

# Session 3 {{float:"right" 2025-02-09}}
{wide}

## 1 - Rooftop briefing
_~02:00, 23 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. The party is on the rooftop of the Greenest keep after the dragon's retreat. **Tarbaw Nighthill** is in poor shape from the dragon attack: the half-tended cuts on his face have torn open and are weeping blood through hastily wrapped bandages, his arm hangs in a hastily re-tied sling, and he sways on his feet, visibly fighting to stay upright as he speaks. Every sentence comes shorter than the last.
2. A keep guard arrives with a low-voiced report: cultists are burning the mill, Greenest's main grain stockpile and bakery. Guards will need 15–20 minutes to muster a force. Tarbaw asks the party if they can move faster.
3. Mialee voices the strategic case: *"si esta gente pierde el molino, se va a morir la mitad del pueblo de hambre."* Nuberroca asks for healing supplies before they go; Tarbaw replies that he already gave them three healing potions and that the rest are needed for the wounded from the dragon attack.
4. The party decides to leave by the rehabilitated lower tunnel rather than the front gate, which is less secure. Sinsong and Eguzkilore each put on a cultist robe as disguise.

## 2 - Approach to the mill

1. Party leaves through the tunnel and works southwest along the river toward the mill. It is the dead of night; some fires elsewhere in town are dying.
2. From cover, the party spots another armed group moving among the houses to the west; they choose to skirt them rather than engage and press on toward the mill.
3. Mialee notices the smoke from the mill is *white*, not the dark colour expected from burning grain or wood. Nyx seconds the observation a moment later. The party files it as suspicious and presses on.
4. Sinsong casts Guidance on Mialee so she can sneak ahead and scout. Mialee creeps in close enough to identify the patrol's composition without being seen: a guard drake, several kobolds, and one leading cultist.
5. As the rest of the party closes in, the drake catches their scent and moves toward them.
6. ***[EPIC]*** Nyx opens with a longbow shot, a critical hit that drops the guard drake in a single arrow. The patrol's dangerous element is gone before the rest can react.
7. Mialee stealthily attacks from cover, killing a kobold in a single hit.
8. Eguzkilore looses an arrow that ends another kobold.
9. Sinsong casts Fire Bolt, ending a distracted kobold before he can react.
10. Amidamaru casts Fire Bolt at the leading cultist, a direct hit that kills him outright.
11. The surviving kobolds break and flee.

## 3 - The mill ambush

1. From behind a nearby tree, Sinsong uses his magical hand to open the front door while the rest of the party holds ready in case anyone shoots from inside. Nothing happens.
2. Through the open door they see the ground floor: scattered grain sacks, the millstone still grinding, and a pulley system rising to a second level. The mill looks empty; the smoke is coming from upstairs.
3. Eguzkilore enters first; Mia, Nyx, and Amidamaru follow him in quickly, spreading across the ground floor. Sinsong and Nuberroca stay by the doorway.
4. With the party committed inside, the ambush is revealed: a volley of spears and arrows comes down from concealed attackers in the rafters. Two of the throws crit on Eguzkilore, dropping him unconscious. Nyx is hit and badly hurt.
5. Immediately after, ten cultists drop from the rafters into melee, five wearing keep-guard livery.
6. A livery cultist drives a spear under Nyx's armpit, a heavy hit, but she holds.
7. One of the cultists in keep-guard livery steals Eguzkilore's short sword off his unconscious body.
8. Amidamaru casts a cone of flame from his mouth that engulfs four cultists, being careful to spare Nyx from the conflagration. Three cultists die outright in the fire.
9. Mialee shoots from behind a crate, fails the attack, and retreats to safety outdoors.
10. Nuberroca rages forward and engages two livery cultists at the doorway, missing with both attacks. As the cultists land their hits, a *blue glow* runs across each cut and closes it partway.
11. Nyx stabilizes Eguzkilore and downs a healing potion to recover. She's back in fighting shape.
12. Sinsong tries to cast Fire Bolt at one of the cultists, but the spell fumbles, fizzling and sparking in his hand without ever leaving.
13. Recovering quickly from his disappointment, Sinsong leans into his cultist robe disguise: he hugs Amidamaru as if *"arresting"* him, selling the moment to the nearby ambushers. The deception lands hard, and none of the nearby cultists break through it.
14. A livery cultist hits Nyx with his spear, dropping her unconscious. Amidamaru, unaware of his downed friend, attacks the cultist beside him with knives.
15. Sinsong uses his magic to stabilize the halfling.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 3}}
\page

16. Mid-fight, one of the surviving cultists in keep-guard livery shouts in frustration: *"Mondath no nos dijo que eran tan peligrosos."*
17. Nuberroca finishes a large cultist with a critical Hand Axe blow, sending him flying into the wall.
18. The livery cultist who stole Eguzkilore's short sword breaks for the door and escapes outside the mill. Nuberroca hurls a hand-axe after him at a long distance; the throw hits but does not kill, and the axe lodges in his back as he flees with both the stolen sword and the axe still embedded.
19. Eguzkilore stays down for the rest of the fight, breathing only thanks to Nyx's earlier stabilization, never regaining consciousness on his own.
20. With the last cultist down, the mill falls quiet. The fight inside took just a few minutes.

## 4 - Looting the cultists

1. The *"fire"* upstairs was a montage: wet straw smoldering, deliberately smoking. The mill itself was never seriously burning. The cultists set the smoke to lure responders into the rafter ambush.
2. Nuberroca claims a piece of green cotton from a cultist's bundle and ties it on as a scarf, praising it as soft as a newborn's skin — he keeps it through the rest of the night.
3. The goliath pockets what he calls a fly in a jar, prizing the unfamiliar curio. Sinsong corrects him — it is a firefly — and Nyx makes the connection out loud, calling it a *lightbulb-head bug*.
4. Nuberroca seizes the framing and presents the jar to Sinsong: *"¡Es un bicho Cabezabombilla como tú!"*
5. The scavenging barbarian scoops up other oddments — sugar from a pouch, a live chicken for the eggs, a dried piece of guano he abandons on sight. Sinsong tries to redirect him to a cow, but the cows are already gone, dead in the streets.
6. A livery cultist's pouch contains a whistle. Nuberroca hands it to Sinsong as a gag, telling him to blow it whenever he wants attention. Sinsong blows it theatrically while discreetly tapping unconscious Eguzkilore with his Healing Staff. His bald head glows from the casting and Eguzkilore wakes; Sinsong sells the whole thing as the whistle's doing.
7. Beyond a few low-quality weapons, the bodies yield two gold bracelets and several art objects worth keeping; Nuberroca also peels a small unfamiliar insignia off one of the corpses and pockets it.
8. Eguzkilore checks the second floor: flour bags and grain sacks, plus the millstone's clutch, which they disengage to stop the grinding.
9. With the mill secured in the hands of the recently arrived guards, the party heads back to the keep through the tunnel.

\column

## 5 - Keep under attack
_~03:00, 23 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. The lower tunnel is unusually silent. Combat noise from deeper in the keep reveals enemies have entered through another route.
2. Party emerges into the keep's inner courtyard: the sally port gate has been breached. Cultists, kobolds, and drakes are fighting the guards across the courtyard; six attackers head for the heroes on sight.
3. Sinsong removes the cultist robe and elbows Eguzkilore to do the same before joining the fight.
4. Sinsong takes cover behind the well; Mia crouches behind a wagon. Nuberroca, ignoring cover entirely, charges straight in.
5. Eguzkilore drops a cultist with a clean longbow shot to the eye.
6. Sinsong casts Fire Bolt repeatedly from behind the well, ducking after each cast. One cultist's shield deflects the first bolt entirely.
7. Mialee hides and shoots each round; she remains effectively undetected through the entire fight.
8. Nuberroca grapples a livery cultist and **throws him head-first down the well**, the cultist's head bouncing audibly off the stone walls all the way down.
9. As cultists land hits on Nuberroca during the courtyard melee, a *blue glow* runs across each cut and closes the wound.
10. Two livery cultists trip over each other trying to flank Nuberroca and are hacked down. The remaining cultists try to escape; only one makes it through the sally port, the rest fall.
11. Among the felled, one kobold lies stunned but breathing. Nyx calls to keep it alive. Nuberroca binds and gags it on the spot and stows it against a courtyard wall for later questioning.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 3}}
\page

## 6 - The sally port room

1. Mialee splits off to scout the sally port room, vanishing from the group's sight; from the other side of the door the rest of the party hears the unmistakable rhythm of a four-volley salvo — the cultists inside the room overwhelm her in a brutal assault, leaving her unconscious on the chamber floor.
2. Sinsong opens the chamber door from afar using Mage Hand while Nyx, Eguzkilore, and Nuberroca ready weapons.
3. Through the open door the party gets its first look inside: a small chamber holding two cultist acolytes, several kobolds, and another large drake — with Mialee's unconscious body on the floor in the middle of them.
4. Nuberroca, seeing the unconscious little Mia on the floor, charges in Raging with a hand-axe.
5. An acolyte casts Command on Nuberroca; Nuberroca shrugs it off. The other acolyte buffs himself with a defensive ward.
6. Nyx, briefly dissuaded by the acolyte's defensive ward, shakes it off and lands a chest-shot on the caster, dropping him.
7. Sinsong casts Spare the Dying on Mialee from across the room, stabilizing her.
8. The lead acolyte Commands Nuberroca to disarm; the goliath drops his weapons. The drake and a kobold attack together; the kobold critically wounds the barbarian, dropping him unconscious.
9. Sinsong tries to throw a Fire Bolt at a nearby enemy; the spell fizzles in his hand and he ducks back.
10. Unable to cast more Commands, the acolyte falls back on Sacred Flame and starts sweeping the room turn after turn — Sinsong takes the first flare and resists, his bald head briefly glowing; Eguzkilore weathers the next without yielding.
11. Sinsong moves close to Nuberroca and uses his magic to stabilize him.
12. The drake rounds on Sinsong, connecting a bite that drops him *beside Nuberroca*.
13. Eguzkilore takes another Sacred Flame, then digs deep and pushes through the worst of the damage, staying on his feet. Nyx flanks the drake and stabs it until it stops moving.
14. Eguzkilore reaches Sinsong and patches the worst of his wounds, stabilizing the cleric.
15. Amidamaru kills the last kobold with a Fire Bolt.
16. The acolyte tries Sacred Flame again, aimed at Nyx; it fizzles harmlessly. With his colleague dead, the kobolds gone, and the rest of the party closing on him, he turns and flees out the broken sally port.
17. With the raiders repelled, the heroes focus on healing their wounded: Mialee, Nuberroca, and Sinsong are unconscious but stable; the rest of the party is heavily wounded.

\column

## 7 - Sally port repair
_~04:00, 23 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. Nyx rummages Nuberroca's pack, finds his unused healing potions among the plethora of items collected, and force-feeds one to him and one to Sinsong; both revive instantly, feeling much better.
2. Sinsong stands up and blows the whistle theatrically while discreetly tapping Mialee with his Healing Staff, his bald head glowing as Mialee wakes. The whistle gag holds.
3. Mia wakes recounting a nightmare, disoriented; the trauma from the sally-port room sits heavy on her without being named aloud.
4. Nuberroca crouches over the bound courtyard kobold and presses it for intel. Through fear and broken Common, the only useful thing it gives up: a *"dragon woman"* gave their orders — raid Greenest and the surrounding villages and take as much loot as they can carry. The kobold's untreated wounds catch up with it, and it bleeds out where it sits before they can press it further.
5. Amidamaru repairs the sally port door using his Artificer tools to put the broken structure back together over ~10 minutes, while Eguzkilore arranges a barricade of furniture as backup.
6. The keep guards confirm the assault is breaking. Most attackers are leaving Greenest. The party tries to sleep, but soldiers are still mopping up the last attackers across town and another wave cannot be ruled out, so they simply rest a bit.
7. During the short rest the party studies a recovered cult map. They see Greenest clearly labeled, with several other locations also highlighted; most notably an X southeast of Greenest.
8. The hour proves *unexpectedly restorative*. The party comes out healed and refreshed, ready to continue their adventure.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 3}}
\page

## 8 - The challenge
_~05:00, 23 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. A large, hooded figure (~7 ft) stands at the gate with 10 kobolds and three civilian prisoners: a woman and two boys, knives at their throats. The figure's voice is deep and dragonborn.
2. The figure addresses the keep:
   > *"Defensores de Greenest, esta ha sido una noche exitosa y me siento generoso. ¿Veis estos tres vergonzosos e inútiles prisioneros? No los necesitamos, así que acepto devolvéroslos. Mandadnos a vuestro mejor guerrero a luchar contra mí y podréis tener a estos tres en respuesta."*
3. The three prisoners are recognised as the family of a keep guard: his sister and two nephews, taken from the town during the assault and held hostage.
4. The guard is **Sergeant Markguth**, who steps forward at the wall when he sees them, recognising his family.
5. **Tarbaw**, beside Nuberroca, quietly notes that none of his soldiers would stand a chance and asks whether one of the party would go in their place.
6. Nyx asks if the figure is acting in good faith, and the figure swears on his honour.
7. Nuberroca accepts the challenge and asks the figure what becomes of the prisoners.
8. The figure responds with his terms, declaring his word worth more than their lives, and lays out the duel: whoever dies, the prisoners go free; the outcome of the combat does not matter to him, only the fun of the fight.
9. Nuberroca requests healing before stepping out. A guard hands him one of the last full healing potions. Sinsong runs the whistle gag, blowing it theatrically while tapping Nuberroca with the Healing Staff. The potion goes down with unusual force, the brew turns more potent than usual. Nuberroca steps out reinvigorated.
10. ***[EPIC]*** Nuberroca removes the green cotton scarf, folds it carefully, sets it on the ground, then stomps once and announces:
    > *"Soy Nuberroca Quebrantacielos, guardián del paso sagrado y escudo del árbol del mundo. No lucho por gloria ni oro, sino por el deber. Si buscas desafiarme, que los espíritus de la montaña sean testigos de tu destino."*
11. The figure pulls back its hood and cloak: a blue dragonborn with greatsword, lance and the build of a champion. He responds:
    > *"Yo soy **Langdedrosa Cyanwrath**, y estoy aquí para grabar mi nombre en la historia de este pueblo."*
12. On Langdedrosa's signal, the two boys are released; they run to the keep gate and are taken inside. The mother remains in kobold custody, knife at throat.

\column

## 9 - The duel

1. Both fighters stand 50 feet apart on either side of the open gate. Mialee, Sinsong, Eguzkilore, Nyx, and the keep watchers line the wall, weapons sheathed by **Langdedrosa**'s terms.
2. Nuberroca opens by hurling a hand-axe; the throw goes wide at the distance. Langdedrosa surges forward and hurls his lance at the barbarian, hitting hard.
3. Nuberroca lets the fury take him. He closes and attacks twice with two hand-axes; both land cleanly. The *blue glow* flares again across his older cuts.
4. Langdedrosa plants his greatsword in the ground and exhales a lightning breath in a line. Nuberroca dives aside and catches the brunt at a glancing angle, but the lightning still takes a heavy toll.
5. Nuberroca closes again, pushes through with hand-axes, both hit. Langdedrosa is visibly bloodied and stops smiling.
6. Langdedrosa raises his greatsword and swings four times in the same breath. The first hits Nuberroca and leaves him *barely standing*. The second misses. The third misses. The fourth lands cleanly. Nuberroca falls unconscious.
7. Langdedrosa stalks forward, plunges his greatsword into Nuberroca's abdomen and twists. He withdraws and turns to leave; Nuberroca is unconscious, his life slowly fading away.
8. Sinsong, near the wall, raises his hands to cast Fire Bolt at the retreating Langdedrosa. Eguzkilore and the others restrain Sinsong physically, warning that any retaliation would mean Nuberroca died for nothing; Sinsong relents and instead runs to cast Spare the Dying on the unconscious goliath, keeping him alive on the ground.
9. Langdedrosa keeps his word. As he withdraws, he walks into his own kobold ranks, frees the woman from her captor, and disappears with the rest of his force into the night.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 3}}
\page

## 10 - Aftermath

1. The raiding army is leaving, victorious; the assault is over.
2. Nuberroca, stabilized by the party cleric, is back on his feet shortly after.
3. **Tarbaw** offers the party two more healing potions and 60 gp total, 10 for each of them, for the night's defence and for rescuing the guard's family.
4. The party shows Tarbaw the items they recovered from the raiders: two gold bracelets and several art objects. They identify these as belongings of Greenest townsfolk and return them to the keep for the families. Nuberroca leads this, framing it as paying back their humble hosts before they leave.
5. Tarbaw praises the party's honourable acts: defending Greenest and returning the recovered townsfolk goods rather than keeping them.
6. The party pools their gold to clear Sinsong's resurrection debt. The cleric forgives 100 gp as a token of gratitude; the guards contribute another 100 gp; the party covers the remaining 400 gp with their combined funds.
7. Nuberroca produces the cultist insignia he pocketed back at the mill. The party recognises it through general lore: it is the symbol of **Tiamat**, the five-headed dragon goddess. The raiders are confirmed to be the *Cult of the Dragon*.
8. The party retires to the barracks to rest, sleeping until well past the morning.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 3}}

\page

# Session 4 {{float:"right" 2025-02-16}}
{wide}

## 1 - Briefing at the keep
_~14:00, 23 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. After roughly ten hours of sleep, the party is awake at the Greenest keep in the early afternoon. The town is in the daylight clean-up: surviving guards bandaged and overworked, dead bodies being gathered to be cremated that night to avoid disease. The party's purses are empty.
2. **Tarbaw Nighthill** and **Escobert the Red** gather the party in a side room. The castellan reports the captured cultist did not survive his interrogation — Escobert's knuckles are bloodied and split. One of the party murmurs that Escobert's hand clearly slipped.
3. Tarbaw lays out what the keep needs: motivations of the raiders, leaders, numbers, and plans, so the next likely target can be warned. As a bonus, recover any of the looted treasure if possible. He offers **200 gp per head** if the party can bring back this intel.
4. The party is hailed as the heroes of Greenest; soldiers salute them in the corridors. Mialee asks Eguzkilore in a side conversation whether they should show Tarbaw the cult map; the party agrees, and Eguzkilore produces it.
5. Tarbaw studies the map. The X marked south of Greenfields corresponds to a hilly area roughly 12 miles south, about four hours' march. The terrain transitions from rolling farmland into rougher hills with caves, sparse herd-pasture and no settled villages. A plausible cult hideout.
6. The party discusses tactics: not an assault, a *reconnaissance*. Approach, observe numbers and disposition, withdraw with intel.
7. Mialee broaches the leadership question. Nyx assumes Langdedrosa Cyanwrath is the leader; Tarbaw corrects her — survivors describe a human who was directing operations, and Langdedrosa is too exposed to be the actual head.
8. Nyx then asks whether Langdedrosa might in fact be the *"Mujer Dragón"* the kobolds had named the night before. Tarbaw corrects the conflation: Langdedrosa is male, and a Half-Dragon rather than a true dragon.
9. Sinsong asks whether any of the keep guards could come along. Tarbaw answers carefully: the keep cannot spare anyone, but the guards are free citizens — the party can try recruiting one from the wounded. Tarbaw walks them to the infirmary.

\column

## 2 - Recruiting help

1. The infirmary is full of wounded guards in varying states. Sinsong announces, half in earnest and half theatrical, that he wants to hire a guard for the mission. Nuberroca quips that the more dying, the cheaper.
2. ***[EPIC]*** Sinsong delivers a recruitment speech to the room: *"Si alguien está dispuesto a venir con nosotros a una lucha que desde luego marcará el futuro de Greenest y de la gente que vive en ella, no descansando desde una cama sino luchando espada contra espada, que levante la mano si es que le queda."* The speech lands on the room because the party's reputation precedes them; even **Tarbaw** quietly acknowledges that no one in the keep is in shape to come.
3. One badly wounded guard half-rises and says he would go, but only if the party can guarantee his family's wellbeing should he not return. He names a figure: roughly fifty gold pieces. The party's pockets are empty — between the six of them, two platinum and five gold, which the guard cannot accept.
4. Sinsong improvises: he tries to pawn his own mace as a sanctified relic of the heroes of Greenest — the guard appreciates the gesture but says a mace cannot feed his children.
5. The party pivots: is there a wounded guard with no family attached? Someone with nothing to lose. Mialee suggests, only half-joking, taking an unconscious one and not waking him until they are far down the road.
6. Sinsong walks back through the cots and finds an unconscious guard with no family. He casts Healing Word on him — barely enough to wake him. The man stirs, dazed, head ringing. Sinsong's bald head glows during the cast; the man notices but is too weakened to ask about it.
7. Nyx pulls a bottle from her own kit, opens it, and starts pouring drinks for the surrounding wounded while the guard wakes — *"tomad, tomad, que tenéis que recuperaros."*
8. The man names himself **Miguel**. The party explains the mission and asks him to come along. Miguel is reluctant — he is barely standing, the keep needs him, the wounded need him — but agrees in the end on one condition: he comes as a guide and lookout, not as a fighter, and the party does not put him in harm's way. The party accepts.
9. Miguel tells them he needs a moment to ready himself and will catch up at the gate.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 4}}
\page

## 3 - A monk's apprentice

1. As the party readies to depart, a young half-elf approaches, limping. He introduces himself as **Nesim Waladra**, the apprentice of a monk named **Leosin Erlanthar**. Leosin and his companions have been investigating the *Cult of the Dragon* for months — the cult's activity has increased dramatically — and were tracking the raiders into the area when they were intercepted last night outside Greenest.
2. The apprentice and the others escaped; Leosin did not return. They cannot tell whether he was killed, captured, or chose to infiltrate. The latter would not surprise them — Leosin is reckless that way.
3. He shows the party Leosin's pendant: a leather cord with a silver fitting in the shape of a silver dragon. Nesim names the symbol as Bahamut, the dragon god; Mialee recognizes it — counterpart to the Tiamat insignia the party already carries. The leather is torn at the back — not removed by Leosin, *ripped from him*.
4. The apprentice tells them the Cult of the Dragon is enormous — several thousand, possibly hundreds of thousands, of members across the Sword Coast. He cannot guess at numbers locally. One of their fellow monks has already departed to Berdusk seeking reinforcements; help, if it comes, is days away.
5. He asks the party to look for Leosin and bring him, or any news, back. Leosin is described as a half-elf with no particular distinguishing features, his only distinctive carry being his now-broken staff and the pendant the apprentice still holds.
6. The party agrees and Nesim leaves, limping, towards the infirmary.

## 4 - Tracking the raiders
_~14:30, 23 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. The party leaves Greenest with Miguel as guide, headed south. Outside town the trail is plain: a wide swath of trampled grass through cultivated fields, the path of several hundred attackers retreating toward their hideout.
2. Nyx and Mialee read the prints — booted humans, clawed kobold feet, and a third set of larger four-legged tracks: the drakes seen in the assault on Greenest, used here as pack beasts.
3. Crucially, both inbound and return prints are visible, and the drakes' return prints are noticeably deeper than the inbound ones — laden coming back, with looted goods. No cart wheels: everything they took, they put on the drakes.
4. The estimated raider strength is not 100, nor 200, it's closer to **400 or half a thousand**, possibly more.
5. The terrain turns slowly — first farmland, then scattered low knolls, then progressively steeper hills. The party advances cautiously, watching for stragglers, ambushes, or rearguards.

## 5 - The stragglers fire
_~16:30, 23 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. Roughly two hours into the trek, a thin column of smoke catches the party's eye, rising between rocks ahead — a small cooking fire, not a signal fire.
2. Mia and Nyx scout in close. Eguzkilore and Nuberroca close in covered by the rocks, but unable to see the cultists. Miguel hides with the party's gear at the rear.
3. From cover Mialee makes out the camp: four human cultists eating rabbit they have cooked, weapons stacked together against a single rock. Apart from them, around ten kobolds eating their own scraps, *ostracized* — taking insults from the humans and visibly enduring them rather than answering. The kobolds wear their weapons on their belts; the humans do not.
4. Nyx sends Eguzkilore a Message cantrip describing what they see and asking for his read. Eguzkilore likes the idea of stealing the swords — disarmed, the cultists might even turn the blame on the kobolds and start a fight.
5. The party regroups and the scouts brief them in cover. Mialee proposes attacking, but only after stealing the four swords first. Nyx remains uneasy — they are five against fourteen at best, and yesterday smaller groups gave them serious trouble.
6. ***[EPIC]*** Mialee creeps in. With the cultists' backs turned and arguing about the cook's handling of the rabbit, she slips behind their rock. Once in position, she casts Minor Illusion behind the cultists — the rustle of something snake-like in the brush. The nearest cultist startles, the rest crane around to look, and Mialee uses the diversion to lift all four swords cleanly off the rock and slip back to cover.
7. With the swords secure, Nyx whispers across to Mia asking if any cultist is watching her direction; the answer is no, they are still focused on the rabbit. Nyx Messages Nuberroca, identifying herself and giving the green light to attack. Hearing the disembodied voice in his head, Nuberroca panics briefly and asks Eguzkilore if he heard it too — Eguzkilore tells him it is in his head and to move.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 4}}
\page

## 6 - A swift ambush

1. Nyx fires from cover; the arrow strikes hard but the cultist holds, dropping his food in shock. One of the others jokes that what hurts him most is losing the food.
2. ***[EPIC]*** Nuberroca leaps from the rocks above with a war-cry, reaching the cultist and landing an axe-blow that takes a cultist's head off cleanly, then a second slash that splits another cultist *almost in half*. Pieces of cultist and pieces of cooked rabbit land together on the camp.
3. Eguzkilore runs down behind Nuberroca, takes cover behind a tree, and ends another cultist with a longbow shot.
4. Two winged kobolds break from the camp into the air; the rest of the kobolds dive into cover behind rocks and start hurling stones with slings. The kobolds' positioning behind the rocks is good enough that the party's return arrows mostly clatter off stone.
5. A winged kobold dives over Nuberroca and drops a stone from above; the throw lands hard, catching the goliath in the head for a severe hit.
6. The kobolds' stone volleys keep landing brutally one after the other. Mialee takes a stone in the kidney from one of them, badly wounded.
7. Mia uses her action to drink a healing potion, then ducks fully out of sight behind her rock.
8. A faint *blue glow* flickers along the gash above Nuberroca's brow; the cut closes enough for him to wipe the blood out of his eye and keep moving.
9. The rogue asks the party to keep the last cultist alive. Nuberroca gets the message. He moves up, grapples the surviving cultist, and clocks him into unconsciousness with a single open-handed blow rather than the axe, then carries the limp body back tucked under one arm.
10. Nyx fires on the wounded winged kobold; the arrow clips its wing, dropping it grounded but alive. It slips off into the rocks.
11. The other winged kobold flies away over the cliffs to the north, taking a final volley from Eguzkilore that misses. Several of the ground kobolds break and flee in the same direction — at least six retreating uphill. The remaining few are killed.
12. Eguzkilore tracks the wounded winged kobold by its blood trail; it dies a few yards on. He searches the body and finds a stone pendant on a cord around its neck — mundane junk, but he keeps it. He returns to the party and gives it to Nuberroca as a gift after Nuberroca shares the rum bottle he has just looted from a cultist's pouch.
13. Nuberroca takes a swig from the rum, beams, and offers the bottle to Eguzkilore: *"esto está increíble, es como el azúcar ese, está buenísimo esto."* The elf takes a small sip, only enough to be polite. They are still standing among the bodies of the cultists when the toast happens.

## 7 - Interrogation

1. The captured cultist is bound with Eguzkilore's rope. Nuberroca pees on him in lieu of water to wake him; Mialee searches him while he is out. He carries a bell, a hooded lantern, leather armor — nothing of obvious value.
2. The cultist comes to. Mialee tries to lead the interrogation but botches her opening — Nuberroca steps in, then breaks one of the cultist's fingers when threats fail. The cultist gasps but does not yield.
3. Mialee dangles ten gold pieces in front of him. The cultist refuses with *"el destino solo lo decide Tiamat. Estoy preparado para morir."*
4. Nuberroca cycles through tactics — *"¿quieres que te vuelva a orinar en la boca?"*, then he begins fileting one of the dead cultists in front of him as additional intimidation. The prisoner responds with cult dogma: *"el mundo debe ser dirigido por los mejores, que son los dragones"*.
5. Asked whether he knows **Langdedrosa Cyanwrath**, the cultist answers only that his lips are sealed.
6. Nuberroca raises the axe over the prisoner's neck and gives the rest of the party one last chance to call it off. Eguzkilore, audibly, bounces the call back to Nuberroca and tells him to decide for himself. Mialee hesitates, then proposes **Miguel** take the prisoner back to Greenest so **Escobert** can try with proper tools — better than executing a defenseless man.
7. Eguzkilore searches the prisoner one last time and confirms he carries no hidden blades. Miguel agrees to escort the prisoner back; the party hands him the rope and tells him to keep his spear at the prisoner's back. The guard leaves north toward Greenest with the bound cultist.
8. Nuberroca grumbles aloud that he does not understand letting a defeated combatant survive to be enslaved, but the party moves on. The path ahead is plain even without Miguel as guide.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 4}}
\page

## 8 - The defile pass
_~18:00, 23 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. About one and a half hours further south, the party enters a narrowing defile: large rocks, the path serpentining between cliffs. Most of the surrounding hills are sheer and unscalable; only a few ridges, eroded enough for a person to climb, give access overhead.
2. The footprints in the dust grow distinctly fresher. Eguzkilore notes there are no horse tracks — only the drakes seen earlier — meaning whatever defenders are ahead, no cavalry has reinforced them.
3. Roughly half an hour from the X marked on the map, the party is well into the rocky stretch when stones begin to rumble overhead. Several boulders come down on them: Nuberroca and Nyx are surprised and get slammed; Mialee and Eguzkilore dodge the worst but still take a glancing hit.
4. A *soft blue glow* ripples briefly along the bruise the boulder left on Nuberroca's ribs; the worst of the ache eases before he can plant his feet.
5. From the ridges above, the party makes out the ambush party of cultists in matching black leather livery — different from yesterday's mob — plus two acolytes and one veteran clearly better-armored and better-armed than the rest. The veteran trains his crossbow on Nuberroca, marking the goliath as his target.
6. The party is exhausted from the camp fight, outnumbered, in the lower ground, and freshly wounded. Mialee is the first to call it — they can still flee — and Eguzkilore agrees: get out before the rest commit. Nuberroca scoops Mialee and Nyx, one under each arm, and dashes back the way they came. Eguzkilore dashes after them.
7. The veteran above raises his heavy crossbow and looses at the retreating barbarian — the bolt buries itself in the dirt at his feet. Spears and javelins fall short at that range.
8. As the party flees, an alarm-call goes up from the defile — a horn note. One cultist breaks south at a sprint along the path toward the cult camp ahead. *Whoever is at the X knows the party exists now.*
9. About a mile back from the defile, in country open enough to choose a hide and rough enough to break their tracks in, the party stops. They erase their trail and go to ground in cover.
10. Half an hour later, no pursuit reaches them. The party debates options — wait until nightfall to push back in under cover of dark, or pull all the way back to Greenest.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 4}}

\page

# Session 5 {{float:"right" 2025-03-02}}
{wide}

## 1 - The mountain detour
_~18:30, 23 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. The party takes a short rest in cover. Sinsong leans on Guidance liberally to help Nyx scout an alternate route south.
2. The valley floor where the ambush waits is the only easy road. Eguzkilore argues that the cultists will hold the chokepoint with at least a couple of lookouts even after the rest move on, and Mialee agrees the original ambushers have probably repositioned. The party climbs up over the rocky shoulder of the hill rather than push through the defile again.
3. Nyx leads them along a narrow goat-track up the slope, the rest in single file behind her. The going is slow and the night is closing in.
4. Eguzkilore's foot rolls on a loose stone partway up; the rock skitters down the slope and starts a small avalanche of pebbles, loud enough to carry. Voices begin reacting from below.
5. ***[EPIC]*** Sinsong scrambles into his cultist robe. Nyx ducks into cover. Amidamaru shapes a magical illusion of a *forest deer* on the slope ahead of them, complete enough that the species and even the season's coat are convincing — the illusion startles when the cultist voices approach and bounds off into the brush. Two cultists below point at the retreating deer, exchange a few words and turn back.
6. Another hour of climb across the ridge takes them past the worst of the rough country. As they crest, they finally have line of sight on the cult camp ahead.
7. The path dies at a small ravine. To press on means a careful climb down; to detour around it means more time in the shadows with three of them blind in the dark. Eguzkilore points out that night-blind movement compounds; they take the descent.
8. Nuberroca goes down first, then turns to catch the rest. Nyx loses footing partway down and clings until the goliath reaches up and steadies her. The descent costs them time but nobody is hurt.
9. Amidamaru and Nyx are still hauling themselves over the worst of the climb when Mialee and Nuberroca have already cleared it. Between Nuberroca's reach and Sinsong's casting, the group makes it down without injury, though it costs them a full hour and dawn is far off.

## 2 - Through the gate

1. The camp sits in a horseshoe of high hills, two terraces wrapped around a lower bowl. A makeshift gate parapet at the entrance holds two crossbowmen behind cover and four guards on the path itself. A handful of stunted watchtowers and a single proper tower at the bowl's heart break the skyline. Tents and torch-fires are scattered across the floor.
2. Stragglers from the Greenest raid are still trickling in past the gate — wounded, laden, slow. The watch barely glances at them.
3. The heroes measure the gate against themselves: six of them against several hundred. Even Nuberroca, who would rather charge, is talked down. Mialee lays out three options — observe from outside the bowl; bluff in through the gate as cultists; or sneak in unseen.
4. Two of them are already dressed in cultists' robes from the previous day's loot; only Sinsong and Eguzkilore can pass unchallenged. Nuberroca and Amidamaru are too distinctive to disguise — they would draw eyes the moment they reached the gate. The party splits.
5. Sinsong walks in calmly through the gate; Eguzkilore follows a few paces behind. Amidamaru lays a Guidance on Sinsong before he goes, telling him with a hand on the shoulder that his charisma will carry him through. The cultists at the gate barely look at them; the gate-stragglers are constant and disorganised.
6. The first ring of the camp is a triage zone — rough cots, makeshift tents, walking wounded. Most of the wounds are *superficial*; the keep's defenders did not score deeply.
7. Past the triage they catch sight of giant lizards being led inwards: the drakes used as pack beasts, hauling looted goods deeper into the camp. They drift along behind a group, keeping the lizards at the comfortable middle distance.
8. They reach a tier of more permanent structures — bone-and-hide shelters daubed with crude five-headed dragon symbols, and a stunted tower around which kobolds cluster. The kobolds keep their distance from the human cultists; the human cultists return the courtesy.
9. A ranked cultist stops them. *"¿Quién era el dragonclaw a cargo de vuestra operación?"* Sinsong stalls — he is bad with names — and improvises that they have to go back outside, that they forgot a piece of plunder on the road and his idiot companion is going to pay for leaving it. Eguzkilore catches up just in time, plays along, smacks Sinsong in the back of the head to sell the bit, and hauls him out.
10. The cultist lets them go. They walk back to the gate at the same unhurried pace they came in at and slip back out into the dark.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 5}}
\page

## 3 - On the high plateau

1. The other four trace the camp's outer wall along the high plateau, climbing the steeper escarpment to the ridge that overlooks the bowl. The ascent is rough — Mialee and Nuberroca top it cleanly; Nyx and Amidamaru both struggle with the night-blind climb until Nuberroca reaches down and hauls them up. The whole climb costs them about an hour.
2. From the ridge they can see the bowl in full. The high terrace runs along the rim; the bowl interior is dotted with cooking fires and the mobile lights of inner patrols. There is *no patrol at all* on the high plateau — whether by lazy oversight or by design, they cannot tell.
3. Nyx works a rough sketch of the camp into a scrap of parchment by torchlight, ink from her own kit, a quill cobbled together from one of Mialee's arrow-flights.
4. Searching for tracks on the high terrace, Nyx finds nothing suspicious — no kobold, no drake, no human. Only animal sign. The cultists either do not patrol up here, or they prefer not to be seen doing so.
5. Nyx Messages Sinsong to check whether they got through cleanly. Sinsong answers that they came out without a problem at all — the cultists swallowed the story whole, smooth as anything. Mialee proposes the two halves rejoin by the inside group circling around to the high plateau.
6. Sinsong and Eguzkilore work their way around to where the climb starts; following the rest of the party's tracks through the dark, the two of them only need an hour to cover what the first group spent two on. They rejoin near the rim of the camp.
7. The party is exhausted and intends a long rest. The high terrace has a generous supply of small caves; many are half-buried by old rockfall and brush, more than enough cover for a halfling-led party to disappear into.
8. Before sleeping Nyx prepares a fistful of magical berries — the party's emergency food and healing — and presses one into each companion's hand.
9. They sleep the rest of the night with no disturbance, waking up refreshed.

\column

## 4 - The morning camp
_~08:00, 24 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. They wake to the bowl in full daylight. The trickle of stragglers at the gate has all but stopped; the wounded are mostly back on their feet, bandaged but functional. Four small parties of hunters — three or four men each, armed with traps and crossbows — leave the gate and disperse out into the surrounding country to forage. The party guesses the camp itself has stripped the local game, and they have not seen any animal sign worth mentioning on the trail in.
2. Sinsong sets a magical sensor floating out and downwards over the camp, granting them a bird's-eye sweep without exposing themselves on the rim.
3. Through the sensor they pick out the *prisoner detail* — eight wretches in chains being broken into two groups of four, set to cleaning latrines. Mialee asks whether any of them might be a half-elf monk, but the sensor cannot tell from above.
4. Cultists in heavy packs are streaming inward toward the camp's east end and coming back without their loads. The route ends at a heavily-guarded cave at the far east of the bowl. Whatever the cult is gathering, the cave is where it is gathering it.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 5}}
\page

## 5 - Finding Leosin

1. Working closer along the rim, the party spots a man tied to a stake against the bowl wall — bloodied, malnourished, slumped. From his bearing and his clothes Mialee makes him for a monk.
2. Nyx sends a mental message asking whether he is Leosin, telling him to stay calm — they are friends, sent by his apprentice, and he can answer her in his mind if he prefers. The man's head lifts as if hearing a voice from nowhere. He answers that his name is **Leosin Erlanthar**; he does not know whether he is the one they are looking for.
3. Asked how long he has been there, Leosin says since the start of the assault on Greenest — about two days. Asked how he was caught, he answers without flinching: he let himself be captured; he needed to *infiltrate*. Nyx asks dryly whether it has been working out for him; he says he has gathered a great deal already, and that he has his own ways out.
4. The party offers help anyway. Leosin accepts the offer of a free hand: if they can untie him without drawing attention, he will manage the rest.
5. ***[EPIC]*** Mialee, from cover on the rim, conjures her invisible arcane hand and walks it down the cliff and across the camp floor, arriving at the stake without a single cultist seeing her. The hand picks loose the knots one by one. Leosin nearly stumbles when the cord goes slack and recovers, miming bound shoulders so cleanly that nothing in his posture gives him away.
6. Sinsong, from the rim, calls a quiet healing on the monk. The effect *runs noticeably further than the spell ought to carry* — Leosin's worst cuts close, the slump leaves his shoulders, and the bruise and hunger of two days lift off him in a single beat.
7. Eguzkilore, who has watched the whole rescue, asks Nyx to ask Leosin whether they should set a meeting place outside the camp; before Leosin can answer, the monk takes one careful look around for inattentive cultists and slips into a tent. He is gone before the question lands.
8. The party decides to create a distraction to help the monk escape. Mialee proposes a fire on the rim itself, drawing eyes up and away from the prisoner stakes.
9. Nuberroca asks Sinsong whether he can burn one of the tents. The dwarf, after gauging the distance, agrees it is just within range; he hurls a bolt of fire down at the cluster of inner tents at the eastern end. The tent ignites; black smoke rises immediately. Nuberroca claps Sinsong on the shoulder — well done, about time.
10. Below, a horn-call goes up from the central tower. The fire spreads along the canvas. Cultists run to put it out; others look upward to find the source. Two of them notice the party silhouetted on the rim and start pointing.
11. One of the cultists begins to climb the cliff face — a slow scramble. Sinsong, watching him come up, levels another bolt of flame and drops the man before he reaches the top.

## 6 - Four from the tent

1. The horn brings out the camp's leadership. Out of the largest tent — a permanent-looking structure ringed by drake-handlers — emerge **four figures**. Eguzkilore takes a moment to place the first; the party recognises him from Greenest: **Langdedrosa Cyanwrath**, the blue half-dragon.
2. Beside Langdedrosa stands a much larger figure: a *black half-dragon*, female by build, clearly senior to the rest.
3. The third is a tall human in red robes, his head shaved and tattooed in a style none of the party recognises — visibly a *mage*, clearly not from this region.
4. The fourth is another human, robed half as a soldier and half as a scholar, also reading as a caster.
5. The red-robed mage looks up at the rim, makes a small gesture, and a sword of *pale blue light* blooms into existence beside Sinsong on the cliff edge. Before Sinsong can react it cuts him cleanly across the side. Nuberroca lunges to grab it, but his hand passes through the blade as through smoke.
6. Nuberroca points Sinsong toward the central tent — just within his range — but Sinsong commits the cast against the red mage instead, and a bolt of fire goes back down the rim. The mage scarcely shifts, makes another small gesture, and the worst of the heat passes him by. The conjured sword stays beside Sinsong, swinging at him.
7. *Why don't we just leave*, Nyx says, and that decides it. The party breaks east along the high plateau, away from the climbable face of the cliff and toward open country.
8. The veteran guards ringing the central tent loose three bolts upward at the rim. All three break against the rocks — the party's height advantage on the cliff defeats the angle.
9. The conjured sword paces Sinsong as he runs; it is faster than a man's walk but slower than a sprint. Nuberroca scoops Sinsong under one arm and Dashes east, taking the goliath's full stride at speed; the sword cannot keep up and dissolves several breaths behind them. The gash the sword left along Sinsong's side has stopped bleeding by the time they reach broken country.
10. About a mile back from the rim, the party drops into broken country and Nyx erases their trail behind them — quartering a path, doubling back over rock, picking the line a halfling raised on tracker's craft can pick.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 5}}
\page

## 7 - The wounded deer

1. The party reviews what they have. **Tarbaw** asked for four things: the cult's motivations, leaders, numbers, and plans. They have the first three. The motivation they can guess at — *gathering treasure for the glory of Tiamat* — but their plan is unknown.
2. Amidamaru and Eguzkilore both vote to fall back to Greenest. Nuberroca wants to lay an ambush and start wearing the camp down patrol by patrol — pick them off and string the bodies up in the surrounding woods to break their morale. Mialee tells him that would just bring the whole camp out in force. The vote falls on returning. They begin to walk west.
3. Half a mile on, a wounded deer crashes through the brush ahead of them and collapses on the rocks. The deer is fresh-bleeding and broken; whatever scared it is close behind.
4. Eguzkilore draws his bow and crouches behind cover; the rest take whatever shelter they can find on the broken ground. Amidamaru works himself into a fissure. Mialee notches an arrow and waits.
5. Two drakes burst through the brush on the deer's tail, the four hunters trailing behind them. The drakes are faster than their handlers and reach the open ground first. Mialee's arrow hits the lead drake before any of the hunters has registered the party.
6. Eguzkilore puts a second shot on the same drake. Nuberroca, hidden behind a tree on the high ground, swings his greataxe at the closest drake and misses badly — the axe buries itself in the trunk and refuses to come free. The lead drake's first snap catches him in the side as he hauls at the haft; a *blue glow* runs across the bite and closes it partway. He spends the next round wrestling the weapon out of the wood.
7. The hunters have crossbows out before the drakes are halfway across the open ground. They take cover behind whatever they can find — a tree, a rock, the carcass of one of their own drakes once Mialee drops it. The shooting is steady and hard.
8. The drakes converge on Nuberroca, who is the largest threat in sight. They take him at the tree, biting and tail-whipping; a *blue glow* runs across each fresh tear as it opens, but the damage outpaces it and the goliath drops unconscious in seconds. Eguzkilore breaks cover, crosses the open and stabilises Nuberroca on the ground.
9. Sinsong calls down a blessing on three of his companions and pours a healing potion on Nuberroca — the goliath sits up still bleeding but conscious.
10. Two of the hunters close to knife range against the party at the tree line while the other two hold the rocks with their crossbows. Amidamaru flanks one of the knife-fighters from behind and lays in two daggers between the ribs; the man takes the hits but stays upright. Mialee's follow-up sneak shot punches through and drops him dead in the dust.
11. The second knife-fighter shifts to Eguzkilore and lands a critical strike that nearly puts him down — the gash along his ribs *mends to a slow ooze* before he can press a hand to it. Before the man can finish the job, Amidamaru circles in behind him and lays in two more daggers, this time held flat for non-lethal blows; the hunter crumples unconscious, breathing but barely. The party will keep this one for questioning.
12. The drake's bite-and-tail-whip catches Eguzkilore in the open and drops him; Sinsong moves up and stabilises him swiftly.
13. Mialee hits the second drake; Nyx turns her Hunter's Mark onto it. Between the two of them the drake falls dead off the rock.
14. The two crossbow hunters at the rocks fire one last volley at Amidamaru — both miss — and break for the broken country behind their position. Mialee's sneak shot wings the trailing one in mid-retreat and leaves him reeling but on his feet. He hesitates a beat, then breaks into a sprint.
15. Nuberroca's first lunge after the runner falls short by a few yards. The goliath *dissolves into cloud* and reappears beside the runner a single step later, bringing the greataxe down. The man does not get back up. The fourth hunter slips away into the broken country before anyone can catch him.
16. With the field clear, Amidamaru darts to Eguzkilore and presses one of Nyx's berries between his lips; the elf opens his eyes, alive but barely. Total tally: two drakes dead, two hunters dead, one alive and bound, one escaped into the country. The party catches the deer, dresses it for travel and saves the meat.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 5}}
\page

## 8 - The interrogation

1. The bound hunter wakes feet-up against the rock-face, four armed strangers around him and a deer being butchered nearby. Nyx tries the gentle line first: *"buenos días, majo. Has tenido mala suerte. Queremos que nos digas un par de cositas y te dejamos ir."* The cultist's reply is cold — he will never tell them anything.
2. Nuberroca steps in still slick with the last hunter's blood, the greataxe across his shoulder, and Nyx says, *"mira, este es mi amigo. Si no nos dices lo que queremos saber, va a hacer lo suyo."* The cultist breaks.
3. He names his commander first — **Rezmir**, a black half-dragon, female, the chief of this camp. He confirms **Langdedrosa Cyanwrath** when Nuberroca asks for him by name: a blue half-dragon, rigid honour — not someone you would want to anger.
4. Pressed on the line of command, the cultist offers a third name on his own — **Frulam Mondath**. Langdedrosa, he says, is Frulam's right hand; Frulam in turn answers to Rezmir.
5. Asked about the red-robed mage, the cultist says he does not know the man. Nyx watches him as he says it and judges he is *lying* — there is something about the red-robed figure he is hiding.
6. Asked for numbers, the cultist puffs himself up — *"somos legión. Somos más de los que podréis jamás derrotar y somos más de los que podréis contar. Y cada día somos más."* — and lands on five thousand. Pressed harder he concedes that this camp itself is fewer than that, and that he and the dead hunters were not even formal members of the cult, just hired scouts feeding the camp.
