Nuberroca scoops Mialee and Eguzkilore up onto his shoulders — the elves are slight against the goliath's bulk — and climbs the rope back up the shaft to **Mondath's quarters** above, his clothes still aglow from Sinsong's *light* cantrip. Eguzkilore's body dangles on his back, the broken arm jutting wrong under the rope, blood gone tacky on Nuberroca's shirt where the elf presses.
2. Nyx, still in her kobold-disguise, follows behind, slipping a *berry* into Mialee's mouth as they walk — *"en mi tribu me enseñaron que no se deja ningún hombre atrás"*. Mialee comes around against Nuberroca's shoulder.
3. Inside the room, the goliath drags one of the low shelves across the doorway as a *barricade*. Mialee calls **Echo** — her bat familiar — but the bat is dead, killed when Mialee activated the acid trap in the statue chamber.
4. Sinsong, meanwhile, had been exploring alone — the party had run too far ahead for him to follow. He descended the ramped corridor past the remains of the *purple fungi*, reached a high-ceilinged cave *alive with bats*, the floor *thick with guano*, scattered with *desiccated bat corpses*. Past it he heard heavy footfalls and a growling deeper down; he called for the others without answer and turned back the way he came.
5. Coming back towards the cave's entrance Sinsong sees a kobold — *Nyx, still in disguise, searching for him* — and lulls it into slumber, mistaking her for an enemy. As he reaches in to check the kobold's body he notices something off — *the body doesn't match what his hands are telling him*. He grapples her and shakes the slumber loose. *"Sinsong, cabrón. Que soy yo. Soy Nyx"*. *"¡Hostia, Nyx!"*. He apologises and lets her go.
6. The party settles in to catch their breath behind the barricade — an hour of quiet. **Munin**, Amidamaru's summoned owl, takes the watch. Nyx and Mia pull off their kobold-form at last and the halfling steps over to slip a *berry* into Eguzkilore's *pocket*.
7. Nyx finally reads the papers she grabbed from Mondath's desktop. *A map* of the Greenfields — *arrows from the towns the cult has hit, all bending west toward Beregost and then north*. A side note: *all goods to be freighted north to Naerytar*. The other papers: Rezmir — *the wirmspeaker* — has *permitted* them to keep some pearls and small valuables; dry camp logistics of no use to the party — *watch rotations, ration counts*; and, *a handful of bad dragon poems*.
8. After resting, Sinsong approaches Nuberroca weaving a prayer of healing through his palm, and the goliath, recovered, thanks him.

\column

## 4 - The last egg

1. Sinsong wants the *third dragon egg* — the one Mialee set on fire — destroyed before they leave. *Smash the egg, then revive him*, he proposes. Nuberroca agrees on the first part.
2. The party descends back to the egg chamber. Sinsong opens with a *bolt of light* at the shell — it strikes home but the egg holds. He follows with a *fire bolt*; still no crack.
3. Nuberroca steps up and brings his *greataxe* down on the shell — a small crack opens. He follows with the *greatsword*, driving the blade through the breach. *Inside, the dragonling cries out in agony, then falls silent*. Acid washes over the blade as he draws it free — *the blade holds, but the acid scores marks into the steel*.
4. Nuberroca then takes some straw and uses the acid to *etch the rune he wears on his belt* into both the greatsword's blade and the axe-head. *There — that's my writing.*

## 5 - Strange offerings

1. *"¿Volvemos por la ruta pintoresca o la corta?"* — Nuberroca lays out what he knows of the cave's north end from his previous exploration. Past the bat cave is a chamber with *troglodytes*, and beside it *another chamber with drake-sound from inside*. Mialee and Nyx push to leave — they have what they came for, Eguzkilore needs the *cleric in Greenest*, the cave's western route they know is clear.
2. Nuberroca wants to check the *kobold quarters* — the burrow the reinforcements had streamed in from — and heads in ahead of the group, Eguzkilore's body left lying on the ground behind him. Nyx starts to follow for support, but the ambush comes first — *two kobolds drop from the ceiling*, surprising him, one scoring a hit as it lands. The kobolds scuttle out through a wall-gap before he can chase. Nuberroca picks his way around the empty chamber: *no traps*, and at the back, a strange offering pile.
3. *88 small piles of 8 copper coins each*, surrounded by *scattered gold and silver* — *that smells ritual*, Nyx says quietly. Mounded against the wall is a heap of *dragon-amulets* — most are shoddy, whittled wooden things, but one is heavier, *worked in gold*. Nuberroca takes the gold amulet and scoops the coin-piles together.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 10}}
\page

## 6 - The drake nursery

1. They follow Nuberroca's route — north out through the cave-in he had triggered on his way out the previous trip. Sinsong walks across first, the rocks above holding. *Then a fresh fall comes down* — most of it crashes around Nuberroca, opening a fresh gash across his shoulder and a cut over his temple; *a soft blue glow flickers across him and the wounds close* as he walks on. A chip of stone catches Mialee on the shoulder right behind him.
2. Sinsong, *impatient*, runs ahead. He slips down into the floor of *the next chamber* before the others can call him back. The room is large and *deeper than the corridor* — a pit ringed by climbing walls, with *broken humanoid mannequins* against the back wall, *bite-marks worn through them*. *Three drakes* jump on him — smaller than the others they have seen, but together they pose a serious threat.
3. Sinsong scrambles for the lip of the pit, scaling the rough side as the drakes lunge after him. One catches him as he climbs — *claw across the back*. The party closes in from the corridor mouth. Nuberroca finds the pit's edge thanks to his magic light and opens the iron gate at the top. Amidamaru runs through into the dark behind him. *Nyx climbs onto the lip from the side*, scrambling up the rough wall hand-over-foot without slowing.
4. Mialee, perched above the pit on the corridor lip, threads an arrow straight into the lead drake's *eye* — the shot lands true from above. *The beast reels*, blood streaming where the arrow stands.
5. Sinsong's body lights up with *celestial radiance*, light pouring from him to fill the pit, his bald head glowing. He calls a *prayer of healing* on himself as the drakes claw at him, his wounds emitting a soft red glow as they knit together. The drakes flinch from the radiance as it begins to *scorch them*.
6. Munin the owl drifts over the pit, perching on the dwarf's shoulder to *deliver a healing spell* — the worst of the claw-wounds seals shut. Mialee sends a second shaft into the staggering lead drake from above; Nuberroca's axe-swing at the same beast goes wide.
7. Nyx, with her last arrow, fires at the reeling drake she had been tracking; the drake falls. Her eye is on the next before her arm has lowered.
8. Sinsong steps in with a *necrotic touch* on the closest drake — the attack lands, while the radiance *scorches it*, but it stays on its feet. *"De nada, Sinsong, por curarte"* — mutters Amidamaru.
9. Nuberroca brings the greataxe down on it — the blow lands clean, shredding the drake through, and the beast falls.
10. Amidamaru, from the upper lip, snaps a *fire bolt* at the last surviving drake.
11. ***[EPIC]*** Nyx pulls two arrows from a *fallen drake's carcass*, sets one on the bow, and threads the shot into the last drake. As the arrow strikes, a single swing of Nuberroca's *greatsword* finishes the work.

## 7 - The cold storage
_~12:00, 26 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. A short corridor links the drake nursery to the bat cave. Crossing it, Sinsong steps on a *soft, leathery tile that gives under his weight* — a needle bites up through his sandal into his sole, leaving him a little *light-headed*. On the cave's east side, a curtain of leather strips hangs across an opening.
2. Mialee scans the floor ahead and finds more of the same — *soft, leathery tiles* in the floor beneath; the leather gives under weight and lets the needles, coated in some greenish fluid, bite up through the sole. With Nyx steadying her, she packs sand and dirt into the space beneath each tile until the leather can no longer give.
3. Past the bat cave they reach the leather-strip curtain. Several of the strips are *threaded with fishhooks dipped in some darker liquid*. Mialee calls up her *unseen hand* and lifts the strips clear from above so the party can walk through unsnagged; Amidamaru raises a second hand to mirror her.
4. The room beyond *stinks of rot* and is colder than the rest of the cave. *Chains and meat-hooks* hang between the pillars; some carry *animal carcasses, several fresh, some long past*. The cult's *cold storage*. *Nothing of use* among the tools. They go back and continue to the exit.
5. The party clears the cave's outer mouth in silence. The walk back to Greenest is *four hours by foot*; with Eguzkilore's body on Nuberroca's back the count comes to *five hours*. The road is uneventful.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 10}}
\page

## 8 - Briefing at the Keep
_~17:00, 26 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. Inside Greenest keep, Nyx lays the *map and notes* on **Tarbaw Nighthill**'s table. He reads the *Greenfields chart* with the arrows bending west — *this is genuinely important* — and confirms they should carry the find to **Leosin** at **Elturel**, where Leosin is bound. He promises to send *the same warning* to every contact the keep can reach, especially along *Beregost*. Tarbaw counts out *50 gold pieces* from his own purse as a thank-you.
2. Mialee shows Tarbaw the *ring* she lifted from the cult cave the night before — a gold band with a worked stone. Tarbaw recognises it: *the Dolenza Zirrine* of Greenfields, *one of the richer families in town*, *robbed in the raid*. He arranges its return — *250 gold pieces* as the family's thanks. *"Estoy orgulloso de ti, Mialee"* — says Nuberroca, his hand on her shoulder.
3. Nuberroca asks for a final favour — *the cleric* who revived Sinsong, *can he raise their fallen?* Tarbaw writes them a letter of recommendation asking whoever reads it to *help them save their companion*. The cleric will be at the *Temple of Chauntea* if not at *the dragon's mansion* north of the keep.
4. **Escobert the Red** receives Cyanwrath's head a moment later — *Nuberroca brought it on his belt*. Escobert grins and asks if he may *mount it on the castle gate*. Nuberroca agrees, on one condition: *"me gustaría que se recordase que el último golpe se le di yo"*. *We'll put your name on a plaque*, Escobert grins back. Later, Nyx pulls Nuberroca aside — *it was actually one of her arrows that landed the kill* — but she lets the story stand for Escobert's sake.
5. Tarbaw also presses the party for *resupply* — arrows, axes, rations, all the gear they had used up; Nyx asks for arrows above all. He lends them *nine horses* — one each for the six of them, plus **Miguel** as escort and *two guards* for local support, with Miguel and the guards to return the horses to Greenest afterward. **Leosin** has separately left fresh mounts and provisions prepaid at the stables — those are the horses the party will ride north to Elturel.

\column

## 9 - The diamond
_~18:00?, 26 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. The cleric — *the same one who had brought Sinsong back* — greets them at the temple. He looks at Eguzkilore's body and asks the only question that matters: *How long has it been?*. The party debates aloud — *the night's full rest at the bat cave fell before Eguzkilore died, not after* — and the count comes out to *seven hours since the death*. *Raise Dead* is on the table.
2. The cost: a *pure diamond, at least five hundred gold pieces*, burned into the rite itself; *2,500 gold pieces* for the casting, knocked down to *1,700 on Tarbaw's letter* and further to *1,500* in the final negotiation. The party has neither the diamond nor that much coin.
3. They cross the keep to the only *jeweller's* in Greenest — **Bryert**, *a smug, well-dressed man* with the bearing of money. Sinsong, who had laid *guidance* over Nyx for the bargaining, betrays the spell mid-pitch — the quiet syllables and finger-traces no jeweller's eye would miss. The pitch goes flat anyway: *If you want to buy things, this is a shop. The prices are clearly marked*.
4. ***[EPIC]*** Mialee, watching the count, lays five *worked gems* she had been holding on the counter — *roughly five hundred gold pieces' worth* between them — and asks plainly to trade them straight across for one of his diamonds. The pitch lands cleaner than Sinsong's ever did — *Bryert agrees: the gems for one of his diamonds*. Mialee also picks up *a small 75-gp diamond for Amidamaru* before they leave.

## 10 - Eguzkilore restored
_~19:00?, 26 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. Back at the temple the party pools every coin they carry. The total stops at *1,334 gold* — still short. *Cero*, Nuberroca says of his own purse. He turns Eguzkilore over and tips out the elf's coin too. *If money's what brings him back — damn the cost, take it*.
2. The cleric calls the rite over the diamond. *Eguzkilore comes back barely conscious, unable to speak*. The party gathers in close. *At last, at last, brother* — Nuberroca, taking the elf in his arms. Sinsong follows up with a *healing* to lift him further from the edge.
3. The cleric warns Eguzkilore that he will move *slower than usual* for the next several days — his every effort dulled until the resurrection settles. The party walks the elf the short way back to the keep.
4. Inside, they share a quiet dinner together and, wrung out from the day, retire to their rooms — the night carries them.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 10}}

\page

# Session 11 {{float:"right" 2025-04-27}}
{wide}

## 1 - The path to Elturel
_~10:00, 27 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. The night's long rest at the keep runs late — the party is wrung out enough that they only rise mid-morning. Down in the common room of the keep the party finds it thick with the garrison and with villagers from the nearby settlements come in to help with the reconstruction, materials moving in and out at pace. They pick at the cold food left over from breakfast.
2. **Tarbaw Nighthill** greets them warmly — glad they are back and in better shape than the last time. He can't share their morning — the reconstruction has him stretched — but he reminds them that **Leosin** is expecting them at Elturel.
3. Tarbaw lays out the road. **Leosin** left them fresh horses and provisions prepaid at the stables — the Greenest mounts and Miguel's escort go back with Tarbaw, and at Nyx's suggestion a small cart is worked into the loadout for the heavier gear. The route runs northeast to the Trade Way, following the river through Berdusk and Scornubel, then west to Elturel — safer, though a small detour. The alternative, cross-country, cuts the distance but there are no patrols, and the horses will suffer. Amidamaru votes for the safer road — he is tired of monsters, and tired of fighting. Eguzkilore agrees, Nyx concurs, and Mialee falls in. Six days by road.
4. Amidamaru asks after a courier for a letter to *Westgate*, his home city. Tarbaw's guidance: no local messenger reaches that far — the letter is best carried by the party themselves as far as **Berdusk**, where east-bound merchants will pick it up.
5. Before leaving, Eguzkilore restocks arrows at the keep. Mialee buys twenty pieces of incense at a gold piece each — the ritual components she will need to summon a new familiar. Eguzkilore also exchanges his coin at the keep.

\column

## 2 - Open countryside
_~14:00, 27 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. Once on the road the day passes quietly — no soul crossed all day, despite the good weather. To the south, grain-plains and vineyards; to the north, cattle-pastures dotted with small groves, where herds of cows and sheep graze.
2. On the ride, Sinsong pulls Amidamaru aside and starts telling him stories from his cave days — a rambling account of a colleague named Juan who fell into a hole and had to be pulled out by the leg. Amidamaru, only half-listening, works a small tinker-craft over his hood so it starts emitting a constant sound of sea-waves and wind, and leaves the trick running. The rest of the party, further along the line, can hear the sound of the sea over Sinsong's voice, gulls and all, even though the river is closer than the sea.
3. By dusk the party has covered a good stretch of country but the Chionthar is still far ahead — they pull off the road into the shelter of one of the small groves and make camp for the night: horses picketed at the tree-line, saddles pulled and stacked, a low fire kept small against the autumn chill, and watches shared out around it. Beside the fire Mialee performs the ritual to summon a new familiar — she calls up **Echo** the bat again, then folds it back into rest to travel with her come morning.

## 3 - The Chionthar ford
_~09:00, 28 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. In the morning the party breaks camp and continues northeast, reaching the Chionthar at noon — a broad, slow river; they take the horses through the ford in file. The road picks up on the far bank and holds the same quiet countryside as before.
2. The crossing takes the rest of the afternoon, and by the time all six are on the far bank the light is already thinning. Rather than push on, they pitch camp shortly past the ford on open ground beside the Trade Way — broader and better graded than the Greenest track they'd come in on — no grove in reach. Horses picketed close, saddles for pillows, a smaller fire than the night before against a colder wind out of the north; watches shared in shorter turns and no one straying far from the light.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 11}}
\page

## 4 - Reaching Berdusk
_~09:00, 29 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. Dawn breaks over the camp; the party is back on the Trade Way by first light. Four hours of steady march, then an hour's rest at midday to eat and let the horses breathe. Five more hours in the saddle after — the last of them a forced march as the light thins, riders and mounts alike beginning to labour, to gain Berdusk before dusk.
2. Approaching the walls, Amidamaru shifts his shape — the tortle vanishes into the appearance of a heavy-built, muscled elf so that the party's shape does not catch the town's eye. Once inside, the crowd looks Nuberroca over, but between his size and the disguised tortle beside him, no one presses.
3. Amidamaru asks around the trade-quarter for a merchant heading east and settles on one whose feeling is right; the courier fee comes to five gold, and Amidamaru pays six to speed the letter on.
4. Sinsong asks after the wounded — anyone missing an arm or a leg from the recent raids. The town has plenty of maimed folk still living — but Sinsong has no means to restore lost limbs, and lets it lie.
5. The party takes the night at a tavern for two gold per head — dinner, the room, and next morning's breakfast included.

\column

## 5 - Sinsong's confessions
_~09:00?, 30 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. On the road again, Sinsong turns his stories on Nyx. He explains — in the language of *warlocks*, which he thinks apply to him now — the fate of his tribe: a black stone found by his master; whoever touched it began petrifying over the course of about fourteen days; the chief touched it, then most of the tribe, half of them ended in stone. Sinsong is the only one still whole, and searches for a cure — that is why he left the cave. Nyx presses on where the stone is now: *"You're not carrying it on you, are you?"*. Sinsong dodges the answer; Nyx spurs her horse and puts a little distance between them.
2. Amidamaru moves alongside Nuberroca and offers to touch the goliath's talisman and make it glow — he had promised as much. Nuberroca thanks him and asks whether the same trick could also serve on *another* object he wears: a tribal piece the size of a bracelet, worn at the base of the pelvis since his coming of age — the goblin-sized belt he keeps under his clothing. Amidamaru misreads Nuberroca's request for a small illumination and lays an *invisibility* on him instead.
3. Nuberroca, invisible, keeps riding — his horse plodding on with no rider on view. The rest of the party sees the horse of Nuberroca advancing alongside Amidamaru's with no one atop it, and cannot tell where the goliath has gone. Nyx guesses Nuberroca has fallen off.
4. Nyx, watching Sinsong's rambling from a distance, quietly pulls a bottle of vodka from her pack — *"por si quiero beber después de la conversación que he escuchado"*.
5. Eventually Nuberroca finds Amidamaru, taps his shoulder, and asks him to fix the spell that came out wrong — offering the bracelet itself into his hand to try again. Amidamaru reminds Nuberroca that the talisman is already glowing — he'd made that first. For the second piece — Sinsong will need to help; a ruby of fifty gold in dust would let him mend it properly. Nuberroca pockets the talisman inside his neckerchief and rides on.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 11}}
\page

## 6 - The light of Elturel
_~19:00?, 30 Marpenoth 1489 DR_

1. Forcing the horses again, the party pushes to reach Scornubel that same day rather than camp on the road again. As they close on the city and the sun starts to set in the west, a strange thing happens — the sun's usual red fade holds, but the light beyond it does not go out. Where twilight should have taken over, a white light lingers from the west, brighter than lamplight.
2. Inside the walls, Mialee stops the first townsman she finds and asks about the light. He picks them for southerners — they must be new here. It is *the light of Elturel*, he tells them; it protects the city and the whole region. He knows nothing more of how it works — he has never been to Elturel; the light has always been there. Sinsong presses: it has been shining twenty-five years and the man has never walked up to look at it — who set it there? The townsman only shrugs.
3. Scornubel itself is a walled town but not a serious fortification — a low stone wall, the river running straight through with no grille to stop the passage of boats, arches only. The main street cuts north from the southern entry across the river; the road west toward Elturel that the party will take next is a poorer, less-travelled trade route by comparison.

\column

## 7 - Scornubel

1. The party takes rooms at a tavern for the night — Amidamaru pays half the going rate to share the room with Sinsong. Amidamaru re-casts his *disguise* before going down to gather information; Sinsong lays a *silver-tongue* blessing on him, quickening his tongue for the questions to come.
2. Amidamaru works the merchants for word of dragons across the region. Most talk of the raids near Greenfields — the same beat that came through Greenest. But one man's account carries weight: a village to the north was hit alongside the bandits by a red dragon. Nyx notes what the party already knows — the dragon they met was blue. Amidamaru returns to the tavern chilled by the two colours.
3. Mialee, meanwhile, questions merchants about a large caravan with carts drawn by giant lizards or draconids. None have crossed one — they know the giant lizards are used by lizardfolk in the northern marshes, but no honest trader has ever seen them working the Trade Way. *Not around here, that's for sure*.
4. Sinsong, back at the tavern, presses Eguzkilore for detail on the family arms he searches for — a dagger, a sword, and a bow, lost across generations. Eguzkilore has never seen them and has no lead — *he knows they exist*.
5. Sinsong fashions small trinkets — painted stones with a single eye on each, tokens of friendship for the party. He offers one to Nyx and one to Nuberroca and starts crafting more for the others. Nyx accepts hers, mildly amused.
6. As they turn in for the night the party discusses their sleeping arrangements — light armour off, heavy armour off, Amidamaru locked in his shell with a chair braced against the door, Eguzkilore keeping his mail and only four hours of trance.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 11}}
\page

## 8 - Four figures
_~11:30, 1 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The party leaves Scornubel late in the morning and rides out onto a poorer, less-travelled road that runs through an old, dry valley, mountains rising on one side, the river paralleling on the other. The Light of Elturel stays with them from the west — a small sun above the city — and with the morning sun in the east, the party cast no shadows.
2. Amidamaru and Mialee send their familiars up to scout the road ahead — **Munin** the owl on the wing above, **Echo** the bat flitting between the rocks — the party rides on behind them.
3. About fifty or sixty feet ahead, four figures stand across the road, one larger than the others. Nyx asks if she can spot anyone hidden further on; too far to tell from where they sit. The river cuts them off to the left; the valley walls to the right could be climbed but not by horse without risk.
4. Nuberroca dismisses the pause — worse than four men have failed to stop them; on they ride. *"¿Quién dijo miedo?"*. He advances alone on horseback, then dismounts and takes up the pike and shield.

## 9 - The ambush turned
_~17:30, 1 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The leader — an older man, calm, moving with the ease of someone who has done this many times — hails them and offers the party his group's protection in exchange for a modest fee, warning of the road's monsters and stray bandits. Nuberroca answers in the language he understands: *"Perfecto, ¿dónde hay bandidos? Tengo nuevas armas que quiero probar"*. He steps another pace closer.
2. From behind, Amidamaru shapes a small illusion that places a whispered voice at Nuberroca's ear — *enemies on the left*. Nuberroca glances left, then turns back to the older man: *"Insecto, si no quieres perder tu vida ni la de los que tienes escondidos alrededor, déjanos pasar y pasaremos esta afrenta de largo. Es tu última oportunidad"*. He squares up, ready to loose the blood-fury the moment steel comes out.
3. The old man's face changes — not fear, only a small note of resignation: *if not the easy way, then the hard one*. In one fluid, practised motion he draws his crossbow and steps backward; the three companions with him ready weapons of their own and the fight is on.
4. The first bolt sails wide of Nuberroca; the shooter fumbles his crossbow to the ground with the recoil. Nyx, holding her shot for the leader, releases in the same breath — the arrow threads clean into him where he stands.
5. Nuberroca, furious, answers with the pike, splitting the bandit nearest him and lifting the body clean off the polearm's tip; the follow-up haft-strike cracks the second bandit down to reeling.
6. Mialee's arrow slips out of her hand as she draws with nerves; she picks it up, moves, and casts a bank of white fog across the middle of the field, heavily obscuring the ledges' line of fire. The bandits shift to cover — one bolt lands wide between Eguzkilore and Sinsong, and another finds Amidamaru with savage force.
7. Sinsong, riding into the middle, calls a blessing over Nuberroca, Amidamaru and Eguzkilore and then — for one still moment — asks the leader across the battle to stop: *"please, we can end this slaughter. No one has to suffer"*. Amidamaru remarks aloud, half in wonder, that this is the first time Sinsong has not opened combat with an attack.
8. Amidamaru, dismounting against the valley wall, calls up a *shadow blade* — he grips the hilt at his belt and a black-edged blade seems to grow from it — and lands a first cut on the captain.
9. Eguzkilore catches his breath, takes a first shot at the leader, then looses a second in the same instant — the two arrows together open the man deep, more damage in that one turn than anyone else has landed on him. 
10. Sinsong folds an *emboldening bond* across the party, then picks one of the two bandits crouched atop the cliff and threads a *fire bolt* up at him — the strike catches and the man falls from the ledge.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 11}}
\page

## 10 - The last runner

1. With the leader bleeding out on the ground, Nuberroca closes on him and pins him under his weight — the captain cannot break free and is bound where he lies. Amidamaru finishes it: pommel-strikes from the *shadow blade*'s hilt to the nape and the captain slumps unconscious. Between the arrows Eguzkilore left in him and the beating, he is a hair from death.
2. One bandit is still standing on the ledge above the road; he turns and runs, scrambling up the rocks toward the ridge line. Sinsong rides up beneath him and casts a spell of slumber onto the climbing man — the spell takes, and the bandit crashes down the rocks, badly battered by the fall.
3. Sinsong calls after the sleeping man — *wait, we can still be friends*. Nuberroca will have none of it and urges Nyx to shoot.
4. ***[EPIC]*** Nyx lowers her bow: *"no le voy a disparar. Se va a contar la historia de cómo hemos aniquilado a una banda de bandidos"*. She stays her hand, and turns instead to recovering the arrows she'd fired.
5. Nuberroca draws his crossbow and shoots the man cleanly. *"Cuando entras en la batalla, no puedes huir como un cobarde"*.
6. Amidamaru searches the captain for cult iconography or a talisman — nothing along those lines, but he does find a small stash of precious stones. He passes them to Nuberroca for his collection; Nuberroca thanks him warmly — *"Muchas gracias, hombre-tortuga"*.
7. Sinsong asks Nuberroca to borrow the small whistle Nyx had given him; Nuberroca agrees but complains his wounds hurt, and Amidamaru quietly casts a healing touch on him from behind. Sinsong claims the credit, but the goliath doesn't buy it and turns to thank Amidamaru.

\column

## 11 - Taifoz's story
_~18:00, 1 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The party ropes the captain into the cart and rides on; an hour of the road passes before he stirs. When he finally tries to move against the ropes, his wounds pull sharp and he goes still with a low pained sound. He speaks — *up until a few hours ago he worked for himself; he is unemployed now. If they need a guard, or a guide, he is good at both*.
2. He calls the whole encounter a terrible misunderstanding — his party had been offering their trade in escort along the caravan road, and Nuberroca had come in charging with a spear. Nuberroca answers — *they are bandits, and they had tried to rob the party of their goods*. Nyx cuts in: *"te empezamos a entender mal cuando sacaste la ballesta y nos apuntaste"*.
3. The man offers, without shame, to serve them — twenty gold, then eighteen — as guide and guard as far north as Waterdeep. Nyx sees him for what he is; Mialee wants him delivered to Elturel's authorities; Sinsong finds him convincing; Nuberroca wants a duel once he has healed. Amidamaru and Nyx both read him closely — he is *convinced of what he is saying*, which is not the same thing as telling the truth.
4. He gives his name — **Taifoz** — and names three sons, two at Elturel, one at Baldur's Gate. On the light of Elturel he speaks with confidence: an ancestral device the town's rulers set in the sky to keep the hordes of the dead away from the region. He mentions **Ontharr Frume** of the Order of the Gauntlet as *a great friend of his*. The party recognises the name from Leosin's briefing.
5. The party settles: they will take him to the gates of Elturel and let the guards handle him — Mialee rides in the cart with him, ready to move if he tries anything; Nyx trails behind with her eyes fixed on him; Sinsong rides alongside to keep him talking.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 11}}
\page

## 12 - Push to Elturel
_~19:00, 1 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Rather than camping on the road, the party decides to force the pace to make Elturel that same night — an extra three hours of hard riding beyond the day's march.
2. The ride wears them down — horses labour, riders go sore, the party hollows out hour by hour; only Eguzkilore's soldier's discipline keeps him steady. When four of the six horses are spent, Nuberroca and Nyx give up their mounts to Sinsong and Mialee; Nuberroca takes over the exhausted horse to lead, and Nyx dismounts to walk.
3. In the changeover Mialee misjudges her fresh mount, slips out of the saddle and thumps to the ground — the fall knocks the wind and the last of her strength out at once, to a laughing groan from the party. She crawls into the cart and sleeps the rest of the ride.
4. Sinsong, spent and out of patience with Taifoz's constant pitching for release, takes a swing at his head to shut him up — *and misses, exhausted as he is*. As the walls of Elturel come clear ahead, Taifoz tries one more pitch from the cart-bed: *you should seriously consider letting me go*.

## 13 - The escape
_~22:00, 1 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. About two or three hundred metres from the gate, the walls of Elturel visible ahead and the guards atop them, Nyx, Nuberroca and Eguzkilore scan the surrounding — no one sees anything odd.
2. Then, in one motion, **Taifoz is up from the cart** — Mialee's scimitar in his hand: *you should have hired me*. He slashes at Nuberroca as he leaps free and turns to run for the woods.
3. ***[EPIC]*** Nuberroca kicks his horse into a gallop and runs the escaping man down before he can clear the road. He drops off the horse on top of Taifoz in a wrestling tackle, wrapping him under his weight. He holds back the killing blow this time — the goliath knocks Taifoz unconscious with the impact and the fists that follow.
4. The guards on the wall watched it all and had crossbows ready — but they let it play out, seeing the direction of it. Nuberroca hoists the unconscious captain across his back and calls up to the gatehouse: *"Perdonad, buenos señores — traemos un bandido y hemos tenido un pequeño incidente"*.

\column

## 14 - The Black Antler

1. One of the wall-guards comes down to meet them; when he sees Taifoz's face, he starts to laugh, calls his companion over — *look who's here* — and asks whether they have come to collect the bounty. He counts out **fifty gold pieces** into the party's cart and takes the body away. Taifoz had been raiding the region for months, always slipping the patrols — by morning his head will likely be on the gate.
2. Nuberroca tells the tale of the ambush to the guard, spinning it a touch in the party's favour; the guard takes it happily — *always good to have warriors who put an end to bandits*.
3. The guard points them to a tavern for the night — the **A Pair of Black Antlers**, a stag's head with both antlers painted black hung above the door, on the main street from the gate.
4. He tells them the place is the favoured hangout of the **Order of the Gauntlet** — a military order that supports the city guard, better-equipped than most because they have money behind them; noisy people, fond of tournaments of strength and skill, but good guys. Their local leader is **Ontharr Frume**, a paladin the guard names as one of the best warriors alive.
5. Amidamaru asks about other outstanding bounties on the road. The guard: no, most bandit trouble is on the main Trade Way and belongs to the trails further out; most travellers arrive by boat down the Chionthar because it is much faster and safer than the road.
6. Wrung out to the last — legs heavy in the stirrups, hands slack on the reins, more than one of them half-asleep in the saddle — the party turns at last toward the Antler for the night.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 11}}

\page

# Session 12 {{float:"right" 2025-05-25}}
{wide}

## 1 - Under the second sun
_~22:30, 1 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. It is late in the autumn evening when the party rides up to the walls of Elturel. Full night should be on them, but the Light of Elturel hangs over the city like a small sun of its own — Nyx notes it sits directly above them, catching them from overhead the way the true sun would at noon, and the party remarks that it must be miserable to actually live under a light that never dims.
2. Inside the walls Elturel shows itself as civilised in a way the party has not seen for weeks — wide, well-guarded, clean streets kept lit by the constant sun overhead. The river Chionthar runs straight through the city, busy in both directions — barges downstream with the current, mule-teams towing barges back upstream along tow-paths on either bank.
3. Amidamaru and Nuberroca ride in still fresh, Nyx and Eguzkilore a shade footsore. They make straight for the tavern the Elturel gate-guard had named — **A Pair of Black Antlers** — with no complication reaching it.

\column

## 2 - The paladin of Torm

1. At the back of the common room the party spots **Leosin** at once — laughing and talking with a big, plate-armoured human whose breastplate carries the golden hand of **Torm**, and one gauntlet of his armour painted blue as a personal token of his faith. Around them a knot of other armoured folk are arm-wrestling, throwing an odd gauntlet-wrestling match, generally cutting loose.
2. Nyx orders a cup of wine, Nuberroca a full gallon of beer, Eguzkilore his own jar, Amidamaru waves off drink for now. The tavern's two-gold rate covers a room, dinner and next morning's breakfast — a shared board of bread, cheese and small plates is a silver a head on the side, and Nyx pays for it.
3. Nyx walks up to Leosin's table with the ease of a lifelong friend and drops into a seat. **Ontharr Frume** — the plate-armoured man — sizes her up but lets her stay; then Leosin calls them *"the heroes of Greenest,"* granting that thanks to them he made it out of the cultists' hands alive, though he adds a wry qualifier that they hadn't done all that much for the escape itself.
4. Their briefers ask whether they gathered any intelligence at the cultists' lair. Nyx spreads Mondath's papers on the table — the map of the Greenfields region, the notes ordering everything the raiders steal to be carried north to **Naerytar**, **Rezmir's** allowance of some of the pearls, and a sheaf of dragon-poems the two men wave off as uninteresting.
5. Ontharr and Leosin trace the route on the map with the party — from Greenfields the cult marches west, then north up the Trade Way through Baldur's Gate and on toward a place they call *Naerytar*, which no one recognises. Between them the party sizes the cult's lead at about six days; Ontharr reckons the cultists on foot are about a week from Baldur's Gate at that pace.
6. Ontharr adds a grim wrinkle — the order's scouts have been watching the whole Sword Coast and see no sign of them, no army-movement, nothing hostile, *"as if they had vanished."* The party suggests the cult may be moving as many small parties rather than one column; Ontharr agrees that fits the silence.
7. Ontharr sees the party is running on nothing, notes Sinsong and Mialee already nodding into the table, and offers to save the substance of it for the morning — first hour after sun-up, in the tavern's private room. Meanwhile, if any of them want a game, his companions will oblige.


{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 12}}
\page

## 3 - Ontharr's duel

1. Nuberroca walks up to Ontharr, looks him over, and tells him he looks strong. Ontharr reads it right — is this a challenge? Nuberroca, with a small shrug, allows that if Ontharr will have it he came only to say so, but *"nos podemos pegar unas hostias si quieres"*. Ontharr's people take him at his word and the whole common room opens a ring around them.
2. Nyx clambers onto a table to open the pool — two gold on the goliath. One of the civilians edges forward to take her bet against his boss; Nuberroca stops the pool with a soldier's line — this isn't right, this is just a warmup between warriors.
3. Ontharr strips down slowly — his plates take a helper to unlace — then a padded gambeson comes off to reveal a builder's mass of muscle. Without armour or weapons he sets himself and waves Nuberroca in. With Nuberroca's kill-joy line still in the air, Nyx starts to pocket the money — until Leosin arrives at her elbow and lays five gold on Ontharr; Nyx takes the bet, five gold on Nuberroca. Nuberroca starts his pec-flexing routine for the crowd.
4. Reading the pec-flexing as readiness, Ontharr opens first — and catches only air, as Nuberroca ducks low to remove his pants. Ontharr calls him off at once: pants stay on, as he has kept his own.
5. ***[EPIC]*** Nuberroca comes back all-in, no guard, and his first punch lands so hard Ontharr coughs up a smear of blood — the room reads it: the paladin is stronger but slow, and the goliath has more than a puncher's chance. Two rounds later Nuberroca sees Ontharr fighting confident and open, hits him again with the same full commitment; the paladin's answering swing goes past him miserably, badly out of practice fighting without steel around him. The room erupts and starts clapping the goliath; Leosin hands Nyx her five gold, well-earned.
6. Ontharr accepts the loss cleanly. He gathers Nuberroca into a wide grin and allows that if all the goliath's companions are half as good, tearing down the cult of the dragon will be a walk in the park, then starts strapping his plates back on with a wry note that he is out of practice fighting bare. Nuberroca puts out a hand, calls him a knight of honour, and slides the barrel of beer over for him to share; Nyx offers him her wine too.

\column

## 4 - Rooms and rest

1. Before turning in, Amidamaru asks the tavern-keeper about couriers to **Westgate** and hands over a sealed letter. Two gold buys it first-caravan carriage east in the morning — around forty to fifty days on the road.
2. The party takes six individual rooms at two gold apiece — no need to share this time. Amidamaru asks for breakfast brought to his door for five copper; Nyx laughs and undercuts the tavern-keeper by a piece, offering to bring it up herself for four. Amidamaru pays the tavern-keeper anyway.
3. Amidamaru announces his plan for the night's off-hours before heading up — some tinkering with his equipment — refining the small enchanted focuses he uses to shape spells. Nyx, deadpan, observes that the whole thing sounds suspiciously like a euphemism.

## 5 - Late to the meeting
_~08:00, 2 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Morning arrives with a knock — eight in the morning. Amidamaru asks for the tray to be left at the door, opens a crack, hooks it in, and shuts himself back up. The night's rest bleeds the worst of the exhaustion off the party.
2. Amidamaru stays upstairs. Nuberroca, Nyx, and Eguzkilore drag themselves down to the common room — and find Ontharr openly angry. They are nearly two hours late; the sun came up at half past six and they were supposed to meet in the private room then. Nuberroca apologises with a grin: they came in dead-tired, and the beating Ontharr gave him didn't help him rise. Ontharr breaks into a smile.
3. Eguzkilore heads back up to knock on Amidamaru's door — for the turtle... well, for the elf — he corrects himself mid-word, mindful the disguise is on. Amidamaru pulls himself together in ten minutes and comes down. Mialee and Sinsong are left to sleep.
4. The private room is small — a table, four chairs, no windows, some of the order's storage stacked against the walls, and one of the Gauntlet on guard at the door. Nyx settles on the floor rather than press one of the standing party to the wall.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 12}}
\page

## 6 - Several dragons

1. Ontharr opens flat: the situation is complicated — the cult of the dragon is organising something large and they don't yet know what. What the party's own intelligence has confirmed is that they are hoarding treasure and moving it. The order has agents trying to trace the destination and no answer yet.
2. From the papers the party brought they now know the caravan is bound for Baldur's Gate along the trade route — that is the point to intercept and infiltrate, and the order is counting on the party to be the ones who do it.
3. Ontharr names the wider alliance for them: he speaks for the **Order of the Gauntlet**, Leosin for the **Harpers**; also with them are the **Lords' Alliance** and the **Emerald Enclave**, all working to break the cult.
4. Ontharr closes the pitch on why the party is needed — the future of the civilised world hangs on understanding what *the dragon* is doing. Amidamaru raises a hand tentatively — Ontharr said *the* dragon, singular, but there is more than one. Yes, Ontharr answers — many.
5. Amidamaru presses on with his voice starting to tremble — *how many, and what does the order know of them?*. Ontharr answers first with the name: the cult calls itself for THE dragon — **Tiamat**, goddess of chromatic dragons. *And that is bad*, Amidamaru offers back; yes, quite bad, Ontharr answers. Cells of the cult have historically turned dragons into dracoliches, one at a time — never before now a coordinated operation at this scale.
6. Amidamaru asks the count. At least five confirmed, Ontharr answers: the blue the party saw at Greenest; a red that has worked with it in the north, plus either that same red also striking other cities, or two more reds beyond it; and a white.
7. Nuberroca asks whether such creatures can be beaten. Individually, Ontharr allows, even without joining forces it should not be too hard; the problem is not the single dragon — it is *why they are gathering together at all*. That has no precedent Ontharr knows.
8. Asked for their help, the party assents — Nuberroca first, plainly. Eguzkilore nods behind him. Amidamaru rounds it out: *within our means, and trying to fight as little as possible, infiltration sounds a good plan*.
9. Nuberroca sets the table with a second thread of his own — far to the north his tribe is holding back a slow tide of undead they cannot trace, and his elders sent him south for allies. Ontharr cannot spare force now, but he vows the order will help once the cult is put down. Leosin, more concretely, offers to put a few Harpers agents on the trail and to try to trace the source. Ontharr adds his own guess — if Nuberroca's home is near the border with **Thay**, the **Red Wizards** are stirring again, and undead would fit their pattern.

\column

## 7 - Factions and gear

1. Amidamaru brings the meeting back to logistics — the party needs healing potions and coin, and asks Leosin for any work in town in the meantime. Leosin waves it off: time is not on their side. The order will get them a boat downriver — four or five days with the current, no fee. They keep the horses to Baldur's Gate, then hand them to the Harpers waiting there — mounts can't cross the city. The order can spare twenty gold and a healing potion each — no more.
2. Leosin turns the offer over — would either of them care to join? Nyx asks for more first. The Harpers work to end tyranny and keep no single group too powerful; they were nearly wiped out once for being too open, and now work in secret, cell-by-cell, gathering intelligence for the general good.
3. Ontharr makes a buzz-kill wave behind Leosin's back — cowards, he calls them; the Gauntlet does the same work but marches out openly and puts the enemy down. Sign with the Gauntlet and they'll have equipment, backing, and quarters.
4. Nuberroca answers Ontharr on the spot. Nyx, more careful, asks whether joining is for life; there's a probationary period as a rule, though given the circumstances they'd waive it. Leosin, plainly, has been eyeing Nyx; Ontharr, Nuberroca.
5. Ontharr takes Nuberroca first. The goliath boasts of noble blood — a descendant of the titans — and tosses a gold piece to the table for effect: they may prize the metal, but where he comes from this is worth nothing. Ontharr asks his rank and steers toward the order's principles: what he wants of Nuberroca back north is that he be a bulwark of them — *"el orden, la ley y el bien, defender a los inocentes y perseguir a los culpables"*. Nuberroca says his people already do that. Then it is done — he signs to the Gauntlet.
6. Nyx works Leosin quietly: she has a family to return to eventually. No problem — the Harpers work in independent cells, called in only when needed. She signs. Leosin gives her a Harpers pendant — a leather cord with a harp pierced by a sword, matching his own.
7. Ontharr equips Nuberroca at once — scale mail with the sigil of Torm on the breast (a step up from bare, at the cost of moving less quietly), a shield with the order's mark, and a halberd. No helmet on offer.
8. Amidamaru asks after some order that traffics in gems and diamonds — pearls and diamonds specifically, to be of more use to the party. The nearest fit is the Lords' Alliance, but the alliance takes nobles or a city's representative; the party's claim as *saviours of Greenest* falls short because Greenest is a village, not a city. Amidamaru shrugs it off.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 12}}
\page

## 8 - The last errands

1. The party sits to the porridge left out for them from the morning — cold enough by now to have set like a cast. The tavern-keeper offers to warm it; Nuberroca waves him off — cold is fine, let's eat — and a jug of beer helps it down.
2. Before they leave, Amidamaru circles back to the tavern-keeper to ask after his first letter — sent that morning with the eastbound caravan, he's told. He hands over a second sealed letter for the same destination; the tavern-keeper warns this one will take longer to get through.
3. When asked about a jeweller, the tavern-keeper points them toward the merchants' quarter down by the river — the shop is close. Amidamaru heads out; Nyx asks the tavern-keeper's leave to bring her breakfast plate along, promising to return it, then follows; Nuberroca shovels his own porridge down whole and comes along; Eguzkilore falls in.

## 9 - Merchants' quarter
_~09:30, 2 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The party walks along the Chionthar to the merchants' quarter — a busy river-port with warehouses, dockhands, and heavy guards. The first thing to catch the eye is the crowd of ragged street-children working the crowd for pockets. 
2. Nuberroca pockets his gold and slings the pouch on a cord around his neck like a pendant. Amidamaru ensures that anything worth stealing on him is inside his shell. Nyx just rests a hand on her pouch.
3. Within minutes Amidamaru is brushed by a small child — the child breaks off and runs, and Nyx spots the attempt. Amidamaru pats himself down; nothing missing. Nyx guesses the shell rebuffed the child — and adds that the child now knows there is something strange about him.
4. Nuberroca warns the party he'll slap away anything that gets within reach.
5. Soon after, the same child steps back out of a side-alley and points Amidamaru out to six men following him — armed, though the weapons are poorly hidden under long coats. As the men close, the street-crowd around them peels back without needing to be told.
6. Nuberroca does not want to be surrounded in a crowd; he tells the party to get into the shop — *"vámonos, o me pongo violento"*. Amidamaru plays it flat and walks on to the jeweller's door.

\column

## 10 - The gnome's shop

1. Inside the shop Amidamaru shifts his shape again — the elf gives way to a semi-orc. The jeweller — a gnome — goes pale, unsettled by the magic in his own shop and beginning to press something under the counter with one hand.
2. Amidamaru tells the jeweller that bandits outside mean to rob them all, then drops fifty gold on the counter and asks for a diamond, urgently. Nuberroca arrives a beat later, half-inside and half-planted in the doorframe as a wall so the bandits cannot follow him in, and repeats the word — urgently!. The jeweller doesn't move on it, and Amidamaru quietly pockets the fifty gold again.
3. The jeweller's answer is flat — he doesn't like his clients changing appearance, it raises suspicions, and suspicions aren't good for business. Nyx presses him — they are being followed, they wanted the change so as to lose them; he answers that the party's trouble is theirs to keep out of his shop. Nuberroca sharpens the pressure — he'll have that trouble either way if he refuses to help. Nyx asks after the city guard; on the way, the jeweller replies. He then reads the whole exchange as a threat.
4. The jeweller pulls a lever. In the blink of an eye, iron plates come down from the ceiling and slam the shop shut — the counter is cut off, the displays are cut off, only the outer door still stands. Then mechanical sounds start above their heads — something is moving in the ceiling; Nuberroca smells a trap. Amidamaru glances up: from each corner, tubes are lowering, widening at their ends, and after a beat's swing they pivot as if to aim.
5. Amidamaru shouts to run and the party bolts back out through the door, closing it behind them as something *whooshes* against it from the inside and the door starts running warm.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 12}}
\page

## 11 - The street fight

1. The bandits are waiting where the party left them, and the leader looks them over, asking where the elf is. Amidamaru answers first: he stayed inside, the fire got him. Nyx echoes the story. The leader is not buying — the cover falls flat.
2. The leader closes on the party and reads them one by one — he's seen Nuberroca before, he's seen the halfling before too, and Nyx eases back to keep the distance; but the semi-orc who wasn't with them coming in is the one he wants to talk to, alone.
3. Amidamaru takes the singling-out as his cue — he calmly sneaks out of the leader's field; the moment he's clear he slips a spell of *invisibility* onto himself.
4. Rage takes Nuberroca and he closes the gap in a single stride, lunging for the grapple, demanding *"¿a quién coño te estás acercando tú?"*. The leader slips clean out of it and warns him back — *"las manitas quietas"* — it's not in the party's interest to get involved with them.
5. ***[EPIC]*** Nuberroca doesn't stand down — he drives the halberd in once, a punishing cut, and the killer's touch in him sinks it deeper still. Nuberroca stays swinging in the same motion — the polearm's butt-end comes over onto the adjacent bandit, cracking his head and leaving him dead on the stones.
6. The two bandits furthest back break — they turn on their heels and vanish into the crowd; the wounded leader flees alongside them. The remaining two draw and charge Nuberroca.
7. Nuberroca meets the nearest with a swing as he comes into reach; the bandit's answering strike catches him too, but a moment later a blue glow rises over the cut and the flesh knits back closed.
8. Nyx strikes with the pommel of her weapon and drops the bandit facing her unconscious. Eguzkilore swings his own scimitar and fumbles it. Amidamaru, still invisible, uses the moment to slip further away, walking with as much stealth as he can manage.
9. The last bandit still on his feet sees his fellow put down alive rather than killed, throws his weapons to the ground, and flattens himself onto the stones.
10. Nuberroca wants the wounded leader — badly hurt and bleeding hard from the halberd cut — and tears off down the crowded street to run him down, banking on the visible blood-trail. The crowd swallows the man before Nuberroca can pick him up; he comes back empty-handed.
11. Nyx and Nuberroca question the bandit on his knees: what did he want with the elf? He doesn't know — Arthax told them to interrogate the elf and they came. Nuberroca grapples him and ropes his arms to march him off with them.

\column

## 12 - The guards arrive

1. Barely ten seconds pass between the fight ending and the whistles — the crowd has already circled up around the dead man in the street, and the party has been seen.
2. Eguzkilore refuses to run this time; he wants to explain. He sheathes his weapons and waits. Nyx follows him: sword back into scabbard, hands up in plain sight.
3. The guards arrive at pace. They fix on Nuberroca first — he is the tallest man there, the crowd is pointing him out, and the armour with the sigil of Torm is easily recognisable. They ask what happened.
4. Nyx and Eguzkilore speak first — the party was attacked, they defended themselves; but the guards answer that the crowd is telling them the party attacked without provocation. Nuberroca ropes the tied bandit closer and prompts him to repeat what he'd told them on their walk over — that Arthax had sent his group after the elf.
5. Instead the tied man, terrified, rewrites the story to save his own skin: they were minding their business trying to walk on with the elf; the giant animal turned on his companion for no reason, killed one of them who never even had his weapons out — and the guards search the dead man and confirm his dagger and scimitar are still sheathed.
6. Nuberroca cracks the bandit at his side with an intimidation, and the confession comes out through the fear: Arthax had told them to kidnap the elf, they couldn't find him, and then a semi-orc appeared who wasn't there before — some magic mixed up in it. Amidamaru, still invisible, drops the spell and steps in from behind, calling to the guards that the bandits wanted to rob him, he had to hide, and asking what's going on. The bandit stares at him — that, that's the elf.
7. The guard-sergeant gives the party a chance to sway the crowd — they all try in turn; nothing lands. The crowd testifies against them — the bandits were bad people, everyone knows, but they weren't hurting anyone now, and the party had attacked them cold.
8. Nuberroca protests — the man just confessed a kidnap plan. Attempted kidnap, the guard answers, is not the same crime as kidnap; the party is not walking away from a dead man in the street this easily. Nuberroca, Nyx, and Eguzkilore are led off in custody — a fast hearing rather than a full trial. Amidamaru is not taken. Mialee and Sinsong sleep through the whole thing at the tavern.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 12}}

\page

# Session 13 {{float:"right" 2025-06-01}}
{wide}

## 1 - The diamond, at last
_~10:00, 2 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Amidamaru comes back to the gnome's jeweller's shop wearing a fresh elf face — a different one from the last encounter. The shop is spotless: no scorch marks, no sign that anything trapped or fired at all — the gnome tells him someone tried to rob him earlier and things had gotten worrying, but he heard noise while he was locked up and doesn't know what happened.
2. Amidamaru asks for a fifty-gold diamond, specifically for a spell. The gnome checks his stock — his price ledger wasn't laid out for every value, but a fifty-gold diamond can be produced.
3. Amidamaru pockets the diamond and heads back to the tavern.

## 2 - In the cells
_~10:30, 2 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Nuberroca, Nyx and Eguzkilore are held in adjacent, poorly-lit cells; their weapons and armour have been taken and only their clothes are left on them. In one nearby cell the tied-up bandit from the street fight lies silent — unconscious, asleep or simply mute, they can't tell. From another cell, someone else is present without speaking, only making small sounds.
2. Nuberroca calls across to Nyx to check on her, then asks whether they should be here at all, offering to leave. Nyx pushes back — this is his first city and cities have rules; you can't roam killing people on the street. Nuberroca insists he *has* adapted: bandits came with knives and weapons, so he adapted; the sergeant who took their weapons was not killed, was he? Nyx concedes he has restraint, and thanks him for not killing the guard.
3. Nuberroca says he's beginning to grow impatient; he doesn't like being shut in without a way out.
4. Nuberroca goes back to first principles for Nyx: his tribe's mandate is to help the weak and stamp out evil, and there was evil in this city — he cannot simply ignore it because the party would prefer he did. Nyx concedes the mandate but reframes it — evil can also be cleared from a place by pulling it off the streets and locking it up, not only by killing it. Yes, Nuberroca allows, with a rifle-butt to the head; Nyx sighs and asks him for a softer one next time.
5. From the cell opposite, a cracked old voice half-laughs at the two of them talking about clearing evil from the streets. Nuberroca calls across — who dares laugh?. The old man answers that *he* is here for denouncing exactly that — that the evil was never eradicated from the city, and the man who thinks he knows evil doesn't know what evil is in *this* city. Elturel, he says, was plagued and ruled by vampires until the second sun appeared — the gift of **Amaunator**, the sun-god, to be rid of them. He has been in this cell for years for saying that aloud.
6. Nyx pushes for detail: how long ago? Forty-seven years since **the Companion** appeared, five since he was jailed. Nuberroca asks who put him here — the Order of the Companion, together with the Order of the Hellriders, who have run the city since a few years after the Companion arose. The old man's charge is *religious heresy*: it is illegal to attribute the Companion — the light of Elturel — to any specific god, and he has been saying aloud that it was **Amaunator** who made it.
7. A guard appears at the bars, raps the metal for silence, tells them to keep the noise down, and walks off. The old voice fades to a quiet mutter; Nuberroca gives up on him and starts pacing.
8. Nyx checks on the bandit next door; Nuberroca reminds her it's the one who was still conscious and had confessed the kidnap plan on the walk over — the other, more badly wounded one, has since died of his wounds. The confessed bandit responds barely — *yes* — that the dead man was his friend; when Nyx offers condolence with a note about her friend being pressed to fight, he answers he owes them nothing. Nuberroca growls across at him — calling him a vile rat, he can start earning their way out of here — and the bandit refuses.
9. Hours later Nyx tells Nuberroca to be patient — they'll be judged or told what's happening, and no one is going to leave them here forever.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 13}}
\page

## 3 - The tavern reunion
_~12:00, 2 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Around midday Amidamaru walks into the common room of *A Pair of Black Antlers* wearing the elf face and greets Mialee and Sinsong as if nothing were different. Neither recognises him at first: Mialee reaches out with a small casting of her own and reads illusion-school magic layered over him.
2. Mialee runs the check she trusts most: reaches around behind him — under the elf outline — and taps shell. Amidamaru drops the disguise-check act; the party accepts he is Amidamaru.
3. Amidamaru delivers the news: Nuberroca lost his temper in the market, killed one bandit and knocked another unconscious, and the guards took him and the others away. Sinsong groans that he can't even get three days' rest — he can't sit through a cup of milk without it turning into another mess.
4. Amidamaru waves Sinsong off — let the grown-ups talk — and turns to Mialee: they will need her to get the others out.
5. Mialee corners Amidamaru with the practical question: where were the others taken? Amidamaru says he doesn't know, because he kept his distance so he could get back and tell them.

## 4 - Ontharr intercedes
_~13:00, 2 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The three cross to **Ontharr** at the back of the tavern. He is already unhappy that they are still in Elturel and hears the news with visible irritation — the party's own comrade has killed a man in the market. He tells them plainly that killing here is not looked on well; multiple killings warrant execution, and single ones are answered with fines, jails and exile. He walks off at once to speak with the tavern-keeper — every word audible across the room, no attempt to whisper — to arrange another river-barge out of the city for the following midday, because time is the party's real enemy.
2. Amidamaru slips to the privy first, refreshes the elf disguise so he stays the same face people saw on the street, and rejoins the others; he can stand witness for the hour the spell lasts. Ontharr has already told them the prisoners will be at the fortress of the Order of the Companion — that's where the city holds criminals.
3. Twenty minutes later they walk to a heavily fortified compound. Its banner carries a circle with a smaller sun-and-rays at the upper-left — the same emblem worn by the city watch and by a good number of knights across the streets, in two different sets of livery: two orders sharing one sigil.
4. At the gate Ontharr kneels to the door-guards in a gesture of respect and asks — in a level, almost honeyed voice — for audience over a killing committed that morning. The guards' faces are enough of an answer: they know exactly which killing he means, they discuss it briefly among themselves, and one steps inside.
5. Four or five minutes later a large armoured captain in the same two-suns emblem on his breastplate comes out and introduces himself as **Recaredo**. He recognises Ontharr from an earlier occasion and greets him with a small barb — *the murderer is one of yours, isn't he — I thought your order had better standards*. Ontharr only nods and hands the meeting over to the party. Sinsong takes the moment to sweep the captain for aberrations, celestials, elementals, fey, fiends and undead — nothing of those shows; the only magical thing he catches is Amidamaru's own illusion, which he already knew.
6. Amidamaru speaks first and at length. He tells the captain there were nearly ten bandits circling a small group of shoppers; they showed weapons on their belts and pressed in aggressively. Nuberroca, unused to a city's manners, reacted from fear — one bandit died, the second was captured — and no one was killed for killing's sake. When the captain replies that the witnesses have described the party striking first, Amidamaru pivots to a *psychological attack* — that fear of visible weapons and being circled by eight men is the true first blow. He closes with an appeal to necessity: the party is heading west to hunt dragons and needs Nuberroca back for that fight — take his return to the road as the goliath's penance.
7. The captain looks over at Ontharr, who gives a small honest nod that the story is true. He says he understands, turns without another word, and goes back inside.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 13}}
\page

## 5 - Recaredo's lesson

1. Deep in the cell block the same captain who had come out to Ontharr descends with a torch and two guards. The torchlight catches the old man's face — a small, thin, obviously old man — and he shrinks back into a corner and curls up on the ground.
2. Recaredo steps up to Nuberroca's cell: your companions have come to intercede, you're being released shortly. Before that, he says, a lesson.
3. Nyx shakes the bars and shouts, *what are you doing?*, uselessly. The three of them — Recaredo and the two guards — set on Nuberroca all at once with bare fists and pommels. There is no metal in the beating; the blows do not draw blood; they simply do not stop.
4. ***[EPIC]*** Recaredo draws his own greatsword last and lands a blow on Nuberroca, the goliath goes down, but remains with a thread of life. The knights hand him to his feet, and give him a pair of slaps for good measure. The captain's line as he pulls Nuberroca upright is that there are ways to bring an enemy down without killing him — learn non-lethal damage.
5. He then opens Nyx's cell and Eguzkilore's; on the bandits, he says, they will stand judgement, but the penalty for theft is not death — stealing does not justify killing.
6. Nyx crosses to Nuberroca and props him up so he can move his legs; she takes a hard look at all three of the men who beat him, wanting to keep the faces.

\column

## 6 - Release

1. The party is walked to a storage room to collect their gear. Nuberroca re-armours himself as he goes and turns back to the captain — *I will not forget this lesson. What is your name?*.
2. The captain answers: **Recaredo**, Captain of the Order of the Companion. Nyx notes the name mentally.
3. At the entrance, Ontharr looks at Nuberroca with plain disappointment — he expected better — turns and starts to leave, telling them the barge for tomorrow midday is arranged.
4. Nuberroca catches him on the way out. He wipes blood from himself, drops it deliberately on the stones between them and says: is this not brutality *to you*? — it's clear I need to calibrate mine better. Ontharr answers that there is no place in the world with worse brutalities than the remotest villages; Nuberroca allows he has restrained himself, and Ontharr knows it. Ontharr walks away.
5. Sinsong comes up to Nuberroca and lays a hand on his shoulder, murmuring *no killing, no killing, remember?*. As the touch lands, Nuberroca sees the dwarf's bald head glow, feels the wound knit, and lifts Sinsong bodily off his feet with a grin: *those were the words I needed*.
6. Nuberroca announces that this repulsive city does not deserve any more of their time, but he is curious about the sun — evil does not rest. Amidamaru, Mialee and Sinsong argue for finding a boat before losing the day.
7. Before they go, Nuberroca presses Amidamaru — are you sure no one is chasing you?. Amidamaru insists he doesn't know why the bandits singled him out; if the appearance is not his own, it wasn't the elf face they were after. Nyx presses: the child touched him and looked startled — that was what marked him. Amidamaru allows there was rough living in **Westgate** but says no law and no bandit is on his trail there. Nyx accepts the answer for now; Sinsong mutters he doesn't believe a word of it.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 13}}
\page

## 7 - The wrong boat
_~14:30, 2 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. At the port they find a merchant barge finishing its load. About halfway across the docks Amidamaru turns and heads back to the inn; he is done with the day and needs sleep to have his magic back.
2. Nyx walks up first and asks whether the barge is taking travellers — the answer is yes, a hundred silver per hundred miles of route, so twelve gold each for the six of them, rounded to seventy-five gold in the captain's favour.
3. Nyx offers services in exchange: Sinsong as a healer, Nuberroca for guard-work and cargo, herself for the galley. The captain shrugs off the medic — a quiet run — but under Nyx's hard bargaining knocks the fare to sixty gold, then to fifty. They pool the money — Nyx roughs the split at eight-and-change each and drops her share; Nuberroca and Mialee both pay in.
4. During the wait for the barge, Nuberroca hunts down a wise old man in the town — the type of elder who sits in a circle of grey-haired locals and knows the city's stories — and asks about the sun.
5. The elder gives him the version that isn't officially heresy: the second sun appeared when the city was near-lost to a plague of vampires and undead; the Hellriders alone were fighting them and losing ground. When the sun rose in the middle of the night, most of the vampires still in the streets — including the old governor — burned on the spot. The Hellriders finished the rest, the pilgrims flooded in from across the region, and the Order of the Companion was founded around the light.
6. Attributing the sun to any single god is illegal, the elder adds — a rule made after mass pilgrimage arrivals to keep the city off a civil war over gods. Popular tradition ties the sun's coming to **Thavius Kreeg**, the current mayor, because the sun appeared as he was rising in power; officially, though, the sun is called *the gift of Amaunator*, and Amaunator is who most people quietly believe made it.
7. Also during the wait, Nyx buys a fresh quiver of arrows and an off-hand quiver, at about a gold each. Mialee stops to buy sticks of incense for her familiar rituals — ten pieces of gold.
8. When it comes to boarding, the horses Ontharr had told them to keep through to Baldur's Gate become a problem — the barge is loaded past the gunwales and the captain never agreed to horses on the run. Nuberroca offers two of the animals as extra fare; the captain declines. Mialee cuts through it: why do they need horses at all if the boat is supposed to drop them at Baldur's Gate? The party agrees to leave them behind — Mialee proposes handing them back to Leosin — and boards without them.
9. The barge unmoors, the mule-teams brace along the tow-paths, and it begins to move. Within a minute or two the party notices that the direction is wrong — the barge is heading *upstream*, away from Baldur's Gate.
10. *You seriously hired a boat that doesn't go to Baldur's Gate?*, Mialee groans, and Sinsong is already talking about jumping into the water. Sinsong half-proposes hijacking — have him order the captain overboard and take the barge — Amidamaru shuts him down: enough, his ideas never work. They ask for their fare back; the captain won't return it, the cabins are held.
11. They walk off the barge with no refund. The barge, they will learn afterwards, was bound for Scornubel — a hundred miles upstream — while Baldur's Gate is a hundred and fifty and downstream.
12. Mialee walks straight back to Ontharr and asks him — plainly — for the *name* of the barge he had arranged, so they cannot make the same mistake again. Ontharr gives it: **La Sirena de agua dulce**, departing tomorrow around midday.

## 8 - At the town hall
_~17:00, 2 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Mialee finds the barge at the dock, tells the captain she is one of the passengers due to board tomorrow, and asks to move into her cabin now — she is tired. The captain will not hand over the cabins early, but for five silver a head he will let them sleep on board overnight. Mialee pays; Sinsong and Nyx take the same deal.
2. Nuberroca then plants himself at the town hall door. Two guards in the two-suns emblem — one Companion, one Hellrider — stand at the gate. Nuberroca asks for audience with the mayor to talk about the sun and about the undead plague he faces at home. The guards ask who he is; he lists his credentials — one of the party from Greenest that faced the dragon, an envoy from the far north, and recently admitted to the Order of the Gauntlet, its emblem on his chest. That name gets him a check inside — the mayor is not in.
3. The goliath leaves a message that Nuberroca Quebrantacielos was here inquiring about the sun and a possible alliance against the northern plague. The Hellrider at the gate offers his order's sincere apologies for the party's cause but is plain that the Hellriders' operations reach only the highlands around Elturel; he cannot promise force at long range. The order's method against the undead is faith — no single god required — and Nuberroca strikes him as a man of action, not meditation. Nuberroca thanks him and walks off to the tavern.
4. For the rest of the afternoon, Nuberroca settles at the tavern, pays a couple of copper for a beer, and waits out the hours.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 13}}
\page

## 9 - Sirena de agua dulce
_~08:00, 3 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Morning of the following day. Nuberroca wakes at eight and walks to the dock; the barge is there, its name matches — *La Sirena de agua dulce*. The tavern kept no breakfast that morning, so the party arrives at the barge on empty stomachs; Nyx pulls out a handful of the berries she prepared the day before and hands them round.
2. Around the midday hour, the barge is loaded to the gunwales; there is barely room to walk on deck, though a passenger can step out for air. The captain sets expectations: two to three days downriver, no intermediate stops, and the autumn rains should carry them fast. The barge unmoors.
3. Amidamaru does not come up on deck — hidden away in his cabin, coming out only for meals, then in an elf's shape. Their contact in Baldur's Gate, per Ontharr, is a merchant named **Ackyn Selebon**, who works in the north of the city — the party will need to make their own approach to him after landing.
4. The barge slips into the current; the mule-teams take the tow-paths on both banks. A full day of travel passes without event.
5. The mid-river country reads back to them as the day wears on: the south bank is greener, the north bank drier and browner; when the light thins, distant large figures can be sighted moving inland — trolls, in a stretch famously more dangerous than the rest of the river — and occasional mounted patrols pass on the roads to keep the routes secure.

## 10 - The hippo raid
_~14:00, 4 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. About six hours into the second day of the trip, they hear a hard knock in the hull. The barge is heavy enough that it does not roll, but a solid thump comes through the boards; a second one follows.
2. On deck the crew are trying to fend off animals — four large forms in the water, coming in on both sides of the barge. Hippopotamuses. Aggressive enough that the hull is already losing wood on one side.
3. The party opens fast. Nyx puts an arrow into the nearest one over the low gunwale; Sinsong lays a drowsing hum across the water and puts another under. Mialee brings up a tiny illusion between the two on the far side — a short, piercing squeal placed right between them — and both wheel around at the sound and swim off. Sinsong then turns to the sleeping one, points, and orders it to run; the word takes and the animal wakes into a flight upstream.
4. Nuberroca picks up his lance and moves down to the barge's rail. A thrust lands on the animal hitting the hull, and the shaft's force puts it onto its back in the water.
5. Before that one can right itself, another hippo climbs the gunwale and charges Nuberroca; he catches it hard as it comes in, but the beast's rush takes a chunk out of him on the way through — and then a soft blue glow rises over the wound and the flesh knits back closed.
6. ***[EPIC]*** With a yell, the barbarian drives a thrust clean through the hippo — no guard, all commitment; the beast falls beaten at the barge's side and rolls once as it dies, tearing off a piece of the plank it had been smashing.
7. Sinsong turns to the deck-climber and orders it to flee; the word takes and it runs, at least for the moment. But another hippo climbs up in short order and clamps its bite on Nyx, almost tearing her apart. Nuberroca answers with the same all-in commitment — the lance goes clean through, and the follow-up puts the hippo flat on the deck.
8. Nuberroca crows: tonight they dine on hippo.
9. Even as the fight runs, the barge is already holed and shipping water in more than one place; Mialee slips a small-repair charm across the worst of the damage as the crew frantically bails around her. Once the last hippo is off, Sinsong turns his own repair magic to the planks and together with Mialee they finish piece by piece; the captain accepts it as good enough to finish the run.

## 11 - Baldur's Gate
_~12:00, 5 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The rest of the second day passes without incident. The following morning the mountain that sits above **Baldur's Gate** rises in the distance; by midday the barge is at the docks.
2. The city is bigger than any of them — save Amidamaru — has seen: a proper port, packed and noisy.
3. Their Baldur's Gate contact, per Ontharr's brief, is at the northern gate — the **Black Dragon Gate**.
