4. The captain gives the party a warm hand off the boat, thanks them for the help with the hippos and the repairs, and promises to send word back to both Leosin and Ontharr.
5. Nuberroca stays on the dock to help the crew unload — they are hard-pressed to make time and money back, and his strength is worth a couple of hours — and tells the others to go ahead.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 13}}

\page

# Session 14 {{float:"right" 2025-06-22}}
{wide}

## 1 - Baldur's Gate
_~12:30, 5 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The barge ties up at the Baldur's Gate docks on the third day out from Elturel. The captain thanks the party for the hippo defence and river repairs, then walks the hull to a shipwright — she holds water but will not do another run without work. The city rules are clear at the pier: no draught animals, no carts inside the walls; cargo moves on porters' backs.
2. The scale of the port lands hard on those who have never seen a real city — stone quays, four-storey slate-roofed houses along the wharf, crowd noise thick enough to swallow a shouted conversation ten feet away. Amidamaru, veteran of Westgate, is the only one not startled by it.
3. The tortle pulls on a fresh elf-face illusion twice over — one casting for the meal, a second for the walk into the city.
4. Nyx warns him this is going to be the biggest city they will ever pass through: if he cannot walk this one, he cannot walk any. Amidamaru answers that big cities do not treat *different* kindly, and until a fight forces him out he will stay hidden.
5. After the meal on the docks the party spends the afternoon working the streets on foot — Amidamaru quietly taking the measure of the crowd; the rest learning the shape of the city.
6. By dusk they pick out a nearby tavern by its sign — a mermaid raising a jug — and head over: the *Blushing Mermaid*.

\column

## 2 - Blushing Mermaid
_~18:00, 5 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Inside, the room is packed and loud; each pays three gold for a room and full board — Amidamaru is nearly out of coin and Nyx covers his share. Nyx drops onto an open couch between two sailors on shore-leave; the loudest is a veteran whaler who introduces himself as **Hipocampus**.
2. The whalers invite her into dice at a silver a hand — five dice, highest sum wins. Nyx puts in a silver, rolls low, steps away — not enough coin to keep playing.
3. Nyx starts fishing for news: has anyone unusual moved through, anyone in strange robes? Hipocampus only knows what any sailor knows — trade from the south is thick, rumours of attacks in the green fields including a place called *Greenest*, and *"nobody has ever seen a dragon around here"*.
4. Mialee shows the whaler the cult's dragonclaw insignia — has he seen it? A pause; no. Amidamaru quietly confirms he was not lying.
5. Nyx presses him — a merchant contact, north gate, a name they were half-forgetting. Hipocampus knows it: **Ackyn Selebon**, a northern-gate merchant who outfits caravans; his shop is about an hour's walk north. The party parks the errand for the morning.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 14}}
\page

## 3 - Stranger at the back

1. Amidamaru has been noting the man alone at the back from the moment they walked in — hooded, huge, armoured under the cloak, taking up a whole table for one glass of wine. The party drifts over.
2. Only sharp eyes catch it at first, in candle-light and at close range: under the hood, more snout than face, and red. Eguzkilore offers a jug of beer as a peace-offering; Nyx raises her wine and toasts him.
3. Amidamaru sees a dragon face and pulls his sword back, drops into guard. *A dragon* — the party comes half up out of the seat with him. Nyx tries to hold the room: yes, he looks like Langdedrosa, but this one seems peaceful.
4. The tavern's two bouncers step in with steel out. Nyx intercepts — *sorry, misunderstanding, my friend was just showing his sword to a friend* — while Nuberroca slides behind the nearer bouncer and murmurs that this is a private conversation. The bouncer, half fear and half duty: *calm down, don't get so close, please behave*. Amidamaru sheathes; the bouncers step back, one lingering to watch.
5. Amidamaru pulls out the dragonclaw amulet and drops it on the table between them, watching for the reaction. Kaelthar looks up: *You are wearing a symbol that hangs like a sentence. Do you tell me you are guilty, or you are a martyr? Do you dare display that insignia, or do you seek provocation?*.
6. Amidamaru asks him to speak plainly, in a language everyone at the table can follow. The stranger names himself: *Kaelthar, warrior, paladin, more lethal than I look*. Kaelthar then names his hunt: anyone bearing that insignia.

\column

## 4 - Fists in the street

1. Nuberroca puts a proposition on the table: no shields, no weapons, fists only. Kaelthar takes it; the pair walk out onto the port-side avenue rather than break the tavern. Nyx catches the bouncer on the way past: *they are only playing*.
2. Kaelthar draws a small ward of protection onto himself before the first exchange. Nuberroca throws the first punch with no guard up — it goes wide. Kaelthar's return lands solid to the ribs; Nuberroca tips into the roaring open-throated fury he keeps for the worst moments.
3. Kaelthar returns fire through the opening Nuberroca leaves him — a clean crack across the jaw; as the fist connects a small light flares along his knuckles, a divine burn folded into the strike, and Nuberroca spits blood. Nyx: *look, his fist is glowing*.
4. Two more exchanges. Nuberroca goes back reckless — one punch entering, one answered under a second flare of divine light — and by the third round he is bloodied and wobbling, a state none at the table have ever seen him in.
5. ***[EPIC]*** Kaelthar drops one last divine-burning strike and Nuberroca finally loses a fistfight for the first time in memory: still on his feet but battered, holding on rather than winning. Nuberroca concedes outright — *you have beaten me. My whole body hurts. How do you make your fists glow?*. Kaelthar answers that he swore an oath — a vengeance oath, against the very insignia the party had shown him at the table.
6. Amidamaru lays a hand on each and closes the worst of the cuts with a small casting — Nuberroca's wounds knit under a soft blue glow, Kaelthar's under a red one; the blood stays on the skin. Nyx offers Kaelthar her wine. Both men are still tender walking back in, and the bouncers watch them come in with a very different look than they had going out.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 14}}
\page

## 5 - The private room

1. The party pays a gold to close off the tavern's private back room for the evening; Amidamaru sweeps it for concealed listeners on principle. Walls thick enough that the tavern noise drops to a murmur; no windows.
2. Nyx asks the direct question: *how did you come to hate these people?*. Kaelthar answers as a paladin of the vengeance oath — a soldier of a clan of red dragonborn. In his own telling: a prophecy at his birth named him *the edge of the final fire*; a ceremony was arranged to unite his red clan and a white glacial clan against the cult of Tiamat; during the rite the glacial queen — a secret Tiamat priestess — killed the red-clan leaders and consumed their souls into a fragment of the goddess-dragon; Kaelthar survived three days buried under the sanctuary's rubble and cinders and eventually crawled out; he stood alone, marked by fury, and swore that no temple of Tiamat would stand, no traitor would go without judgement, no captive soul without redemption or destruction.
3. The party pays the story back with a compressed version — Greenest sacked by dragons, Langdedrosa Cyanwrath the half-dragon general beheaded (his head still hanging in Greenest); trouble in Elturel that ended in friendship with the Order of the Gauntlet and the Harpers; a contact they were sent to find north of Baldur's Gate.
4. Kaelthar names his own faction — *the Order of the Gauntlet*. Nuberroca peels back the skin over his coat, shows the matching insignia, and Kaelthar returns the sign in kind. Then, because it is *him*, Nuberroca tries to lift the paladin off his feet in a grip-hug to see how heavy he is. He passes on the reference to Ontharr — *the captain of the order. I beat him with my fists, you should fight him too*.
5. Kaelthar names his own errand: he is here to wait for a group of adventurers, escort them north to **Ackyn Selebon**, and infiltrate with them to uncover the plans of the Cult of the Dragon. Nuberroca, half-way out the door, doubles back — *that's the name I couldn't remember*.
6. Nuberroca announces Kaelthar is in as far as he is concerned, tells the others they can confirm in the morning, and takes himself off to bed. Amidamaru stays behind to press Kaelthar for anything more on their business — has he heard of a tortoise in his travels? — and the paladin admits he has not been looking for that. The rest drift out to eat, taking supper up to the room; before Nyx sleeps she readies a fresh set of Goodberries for the next day.

\column

## 6 - Under the hood
_~08:00, 6 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The party is down at eight for breakfast: cold meat, hard bread, toasted pieces, beer — Mialee asks for a cup of milk and gets one more curd than milk, dairy does not travel well.
2. Amidamaru, having drawn the wrong eyes yesterday even with the elf-face, tries a different tack: a walking cloak deep over the shell so nothing of his shape reads clearly. The attempt goes badly — the hood makes him more conspicuous, and by the time the party is on the street heads are turning, guards are looking longer, the crowd is stepping around him. Kaelthar picks it out at once — a heavily burdened person under a very odd cloak, something moving underneath — and asks him plainly, *who are you?*.
3. Amidamaru drops the cloak just enough for Kaelthar to see: *this is my real form — the same problem you have, hard to show in public. I don't usually show it in big cities. I'm taking a risk, and I don't like it*. Kaelthar answers as one outsider to another: *that is what it feels to be a stranger among many*.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 14}}
\page

## 7 - Black Dragon Gate

1. Following the flow of porters through the crowded street is enough to reach the north gate without directions. Amidamaru and Nuberroca are the first to catch what the others miss — several faces among the porters at the gate register from a past encounter, back at Greenest or the cult's camp or on the raiders' road.
2. Amidamaru summons his owl. **Munin** wheels down onto the rooftops and settles into a long circle overhead — birds common enough here that no one looks up. Amidamaru watches through Munin's eyes and his own at once.
3. Nuberroca picks a porter he half-remembers — *hello, what are you doing here?* — the porter tries to shake him off. Nuberroca sidles closer and, on the excuse of helping with the bundle, hooks a finger under and lifts it. Something metallic clinks inside the sack the porter has just insisted is grain. Nuberroca, quieter: *this does not sound like grain — if you want to keep talking straight, talk*.
4. Mialee slips an invisible magic hand in beside the covered palanquin the same group is escorting, and — as if a small gust had caught it — lifts a corner of the cover. Amidamaru, at oblique distance, sees under and turns away without a word: inside sits a black dragonborn. He and Nuberroca pull back into the group.
5. Nyx pings each of the others with a short mind-to-mind whisper — *come back, distance, we do not want them tying you to the rest of us*. Nuberroca lets the porter go and pulls back; half a dozen furtive glances follow him.
6. At a middle distance, they compare notes: black dragonborn in the palanquin, cultists' faces among the porters, ten or twelve moving as a unit. Nyx puts the question — infiltrate as planned, but Nuberroca has already been marked — how do we play this?. The party keep their spacing and follow the palanquin to a warehouse-inn used by merchants waiting for a caravan; it shuts behind them. Munin passes overhead but finds nothing to perch on that isn't a giveaway.

\column

## 8 - Ackyn Selebon's shop

1. The party enter the shop across from the gate; Nuberroca uncovered, the rest in travel gear. The merchant recognises the emblem on Nuberroca's tabard — *are you the Ontharr ones?* — and slots into helping them at once. He is worried they cut it close: the group they came for arrived a few days ago, are refusing to hire guards or carts locally — *which is peculiar if not impossible* — and have been packing for two days. Best guess: they roll out today or tomorrow, from an inn at the end of the street on the left — where the party has just seen the palanquin disappear.
2. The merchant does not go out on the road himself, but offers his name as reference to the caravan's other merchants and can supply gear and horses. The caravan is bound north to **Waterdeep**. The frame: several merchants pool for a run, each with 3-8 guards, total 30-40 for the group. Two roads north: the direct route through *The Trollclaws*, or a longer detour east via *The Fields of the Dead*, then back north through the Trade Way.
3. Nuberroca cashes in a greataxe, a scimitar and a broadsword at half-price; Amidamaru sells a crossbow and its bolts, keeping the quiver. Amidamaru asks the merchant to arrange a letter to Westgate — direct messenger would be almost three hundred gold, a hand-off across trade caravans four or five with no guarantees. He takes the caravan option, four gold, and marks the letter sent.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 14}}
\page

## 9 - Contracting on

1. Outside, the party spread out to shop themselves in. Nyx counts five merchants working the crowd: **Lai Angesstun**, a dwarf — oil-and-perfume merchant from Amn, short-handed and agitated, wanted to be on the road already; **Edhelri Lewel**, an elf-woman trading in exotic woods; and **Noohar Serelim**, a second elf — standing quiet next to a wagon of carved wood, travelling with his mute brother **Selvek**.
2. Each hire lands on a demonstration. **Kaelthar** carries a flexing demonstration for Lai. **Nuberroca** opens Edhelri with a polite bow (*good afternoon, dear elf, we come to offer ourselves as soldiers of fortune*), and **Amidamaru** drives a stagecraft display alongside — shadow-blade in his hands and a wizard's blade-song through the air, Nuberroca hopping in as stage-hand. **Mialee** arrives at the tail of the negotiation and puts an arrow clean through an illusion-target thirty feet out for Noohar.
3. When the dust settles, Lai has hired the largest slice of the party — Kaelthar as bodyguard at 10 gold a week, Nyx as sergeant at 8, and Eguzkilore as guard at 5. Edhelri contracts Mialee as her bodyguard at 10 and Nuberroca as her sergeant at 8. Noohar takes Amidamaru as his bodyguard at 10, with the standing condition that Amidamaru protects him *and his brother*.
4. Wages per-week, paid week-in-arrears, plus food and shelter on the road and paid nights when the caravan stops in a village with a bed. **Sinsong** travels as a paying passenger rather than on contract.

\column

## 10 - Trail preparation
_~11:30, 6 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The larger caravan carts are almost finished loading; the suspected cultist has three carts readied but their palanquin is nowhere to be seen. Even so, the cultists stay apart from the rest of the caravan — their porters stay attached to their own group rather than joining the shared pool, walk-in guards looking for work are turned away, and no one from their contingent mixes with the other merchants.
2. When the wheels turn: ten carts on the road — five with merchants, two with travellers riding along, three with the suspected cultist contingent — protected by 30-35 warriors between guards, sergeants and bodyguards.
3. On the merchant's warning — no re-supply on the road, no smith to re-shaft a broken blade — the party lay in trail-stock. Nyx picks up a fresh longbow-string, Mialee a handful of arrows and a couple of spare strings, Eguzkilore nine long-shaft arrows. Kaelthar takes a spare longsword against the day this one takes damage. Prices per usual — one gold per twenty arrows, fifteen gold per longsword.
4. Nyx breaks out ten Goodberries as lunch — one each, enough to hold hunger through the afternoon so no one has to walk back across the city.
5. With most of an hour still to fill, the party breaks for a short rest and a quiet meal — most already at full strength, but all settle in for the road ahead.
6. At around half-past one the carts begin to move. Sinsong is nowhere to be seen; the caravan heads north without him.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 14}}

\page

# Session 15 {{float:"right" 2025-06-29}}
{wide}

## 1 - Before the road
_~13:30, 6 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Before the wheels turn, Amidamaru begs a loan around the party for spell-scribing supplies: three platinum from Eguzkilore, a scatter of coin from Nyx, ten gold from Mialee — enough for a scroll's worth of paper and ink at twenty-five gold the sheet.
2. He goes hunting the supplies through Baldur's Gate on his own and hits a wall — no shop within reach carries the mage-grade paper he needs. After a while he cuts his losses and returns to the group without the goods.
3. Nuberroca sets out for a small healing potion — the only strength his budget stretches to at fifty gold, when a medium bottle would run him two hundred. Amidamaru quietly buys one on his behalf and lodges the tab — when the trip is done, he will collect the coin.
4. The party takes a beat to think about disguise — this is an infiltration, the sensible ones should change something about how they look. Nyx picks up a spare cloak and a hood off the merchant's supply; Mialee takes a small cape. Amidamaru already keeps his elf-face illusion. Nuberroca goes the other way — chest out, the Order of the Gauntlet emblem on plain view, watching every face for the flinch of recognition he saw at the north gate.
5. Amidamaru shares that half an hour ago Munin, his owl familiar, caught a hooded figure and eight riders leaving the cultist inn on horseback, taking the north road at speed. He sends Munin up to trail them, watching through the owl's eyes as far as the bond carries; once the owl outruns his reach no new order will follow. The last command he leaves is *watch*. By roll-out the owl is back with him.

\column

## 2 - Caravan assembly

1. Ten carts assemble in the yard by the Black Dragon Gate. Five carry the merchants — the three who hired the party plus the ale-seller who did not; two are traveller carts riding along; the last three sit at the front of the line, the group the party suspects for cultists.
2. **Lai Angesstun**'s cart is first up front, the gold-dwarf and his caravan-veteran guard **Eldkin** paired to it; behind them ride Nyx as his sergeant and Eguzkilore under her, with Eldkin's own remark that the road-experience should have made her the sergeant instead.
3. **Edhelri Lewel**'s two wagons of wood take Mialee at the bodyguard's spot and Nuberroca as her sergeant.
4. **Noohar Serelim** and his brother **Selvek** ride the third wagon with Amidamaru minding both of them.
5. The last of the four is **Beyd** the half-elf, his cart stacked entire with barrels of ale for the road; he travels with one guard, **Sulesdeg**, close to seven and a half feet tall — shorter than the goliath but taller than any other man on the road.
6. Nuberroca and Amidamaru, nearest the cultist wagons in the yard, take the closest look; neither can match a face from the cave to any of the guards or drivers.
7. The party gets shown the road on a Sword Coast map — Baldur's Gate up the Trade Way through the Trollclaws to Waterdeep, the direct line rather than the longer detour. Fifteen miles a day, six days of travel and one of rest — two months in all. Nuberroca sees the catch plain enough — there is a stretch full of troll caverns and the merchants think the sensible thing is to walk the road straight through the middle of it. The merchants have counted their coin: the detour would add nearly a month, and guards are cheaper than lost weeks.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 15}}
\page

## 3 - On the road
_~14:30, 6 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The ten carts roll out of Baldur's Gate a couple of hours past midday, well later than the merchants had wanted. The formation settles by itself — the three cultist wagons at the head with their own drivers and guards, the three party-merchant carts in the middle, the ale wagon and the two traveller carts trailing. The sun is up strong for the season; it is autumn but the afternoon is hot, and the land opens out into flat country with sparse woodland — good sightlines and almost no shade.
2. Nuberroca makes for the traveller cart — a family, three children and their parents, moving north because Baldur's Gate has soured for them — a bad run of luck in the city, corruption thick enough to drive them out, and Waterdeep is where they mean to start again. He stands full-height alongside the seated father, puts his chest on show and rolls the pecs one at a time, pushing a muscle-tensing sound through his teeth — his name is Nuberroca, and if they need help on the road he is here to serve them. The family's two mounted guards ride up beside the cart without drawing steel; the father thanks him and, in the same breath, hopes not to need him on the road.
3. The family's story does not quite settle Nuberroca — a run of bad luck reads to him like a cover, and he wants to know what a supposedly-civilian family is really hauling. He fakes a stumble into the cartside and listens: for the ring of metal in the wood, for anything shifting inside. Nothing metallic, only voices asking whether he is alright. Mialee walks over and pulls him back to their own line.
4. Nyx puts the question in a lull of the ride — has anyone here fought trolls before? — and gets nothing useful; her own knowledge is rumour only, that they are big and ugly. Amidamaru suggests Mialee try her charm on the other bodyguards, on the reasoning that they will have seen more of the road than the party has.
5. Nyx murmurs a small blessing over her own eyes as she pushes forward through the caravan, then studies each of the three cultist wagons in turn from a middling distance — she is looking for a face she remembers from the cave. Nothing lands; the wagons are all tarpaulined tight, and Lai's own oil-and-perfume cart looks no different from the outside. She moves on to the head of the caravan and keeps her watch there.
6. Mialee walks over to Beyd's cart to work him for information alongside a deal on the beer. A full barrel — the man carries barrels, not small kegs — runs fifty jars of ale; Beyd would sell her one for forty-seven silver, three under market. Mialee closes the deal, then keeps him talking: he runs this road often, there is always a bandit or a monster somewhere, but a torch usually settles the trolls quickly enough. Then she puts the question about his guard, so tall — the man's real name is **Sulesdeg**, but everyone on the road calls him *The Pole* because he stands tall as a lodge pole.
7. Sometime in the ride the wages come clear from the merchants — sixty gold for a bodyguard at journey's end, forty-eight for a sergeant, thirty for a guard: six tendays' rate on a two-month trip, and only if the job is done clean.
8. Riding on Noohar's cart, Amidamaru asks the brothers whether either of them knows a merchant on the road who might carry magic-grade paper and ink. Selvek meets the question with hand gestures Amidamaru cannot follow. Noohar answers plain for his brother, catching himself mid-sentence — his brother is mute, he cannot speak — then reaches for the answer proper: the caravan people he knows carry stranger cargo than paper, and he cannot help. He does know Beyd from other trips, and knows Eldkin too — has seen her fight bandits, and once a troll.
9. Amidamaru calls Munin down onto his shoulder for the brothers to see and pledges the owl to them as a second pair of eyes on the road — in a fight the bird will come to them and watch over them as he would. To Selvek and Noohar he lays it out — with the owl beside him, he stands as two.
10. Amidamaru walks a slow *detect magic* through the caravan as a ritual, standing off close to each cart in turn — no arcane user among the guards or the merchants. He drops the plan out loud: at each halt he will walk the caravan again, twice a day if the stops hold long enough.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 15}}
\page

## 4 - First halt
_~17:30, 6 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. After three hours on the road the carts start braking to a stop for the first short rest. The three groups pull apart at the halt the way they had on the road — the three cultist merchants form up alone to the front, Lai, Edhelri and Noohar together in the middle, and Beyd walks a barrel over to the family and the second traveller cart to work the fire for a sale.
2. The meals divide by employer. Lai serves rice and beans to Kaelthar, Nyx and Eguzkilore — the same food he takes himself, and Eldkin knows him well enough for a skinflint to sneak dried meat from her own bag into the pot for a bit of flavour. Edhelri feeds Mialee and Nuberroca better — puffed wheat, cured meat, some vegetables, meal worth serving to a guest. Around the fires the party watches for anything out of order and comes up empty.
3. Beyd rolls his barrel through the party's fire before the halt breaks up — ten copper the jar, though the same beer runs two coppers back in the city. Nuberroca turns him down flat — beer dulls the senses; maybe when the watch is done — and gets up to walk it off — a refusal that surprises Mialee at the fire. Amidamaru takes a jar, on a two-copper deposit for the mug, and hands it to one of the brothers when he comes back to the cart.


## 5 - Night camp
_~18:30, 6 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The caravan pushes another two hours after the halt, aiming to close out the day early — eight in the evening by the time the light goes soft and the guards start lighting the wagon lamps. Amidamaru keeps Munin low overhead through the dusk — high in daylight, low in the dark. Two riders cross the road going the other way at a trot; brief nods around; Amidamaru shouts after them asking for paper and ink, and they ride on giving him strange looks.
2. The wheels stop for the night off the road. This stretch of the Trade Way has no villages or inns; tonight, they sleep in the open. Carts settle into the three groups they had held all day; harness comes off, and Eldkin walks the horses down for the day's sweat, rubbing them clean. Nyx and Nuberroca are told to keep eyes on the animals; Nyx passes the job on to Eguzkilore and Eldkin and slips into the trees to hunt a rabbit for Eldkin's fire, angling for a little goodwill. No game to be had in the dark.
3. Eguzkilore, needing only half a night's trance, offers to take the last four hours of the watch straight through — a shift worth a lot of gratitude from the rest of the guards.

\column

## 6 - Spear and pebble
_~21:00, 6 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. With the camp settled and the guards patrolling, Nuberroca crosses to the cultist carts spear in hand — pauses ten feet off the first, brings the point up to the tarp; a guard rises off the ground and shouts him back — away from the merchandise. He moves on to the second cart and repeats the pass; the guard tracks him with his eyes and does not intervene, but the message is clear enough.
2. Amidamaru, watching Nuberroca work cart to cart, drops ten copper into a passing guard's hand with a pebble in the other — carry this to the goliath. The guard takes the coin and the errand. The pebble reads unremarkable in the palm; touched to the ear, a whispered voice comes out of it — an artificer's small craft. To Nuberroca the whisper carries Amidamaru's warning: the front of the caravan already knows him, stop drawing attention.
3. Nuberroca hears it and walks back to his post — then shouts *Look out! Defend yourselves!* across the camp to test the caravan. Every guard down the line comes up with blades or crossbows out; the front carts move exactly the same as the rest. Nuberroca declares it a false alarm — a flash of light in the sky — and waves off the half that keeps staring with a small regal flick of the wrist, all swagger and grin.
4. Over dinner Amidamaru works his way around the rest of the party in turn — a hand on a shoulder, a pebble pressed to a pocket, an ear leaned in for the whisper: they are all being watched, everyone should keep their distance from Nuberroca specifically. Nuberroca's own pendant — a glow Amidamaru had strung on it — quietly goes dark on his chest.

## 7 - The night watch

1. Amidamaru drops a spell of *invisibility* on Munin and sends the owl out to skim the cultist carts at eavesdropping range — whatever the owl's fine hearing catches, Amidamaru will catch too. Half an hour of listening turns up nothing worth carrying back.
2. Nyx sets a silent *alarm* over the far end of the middle cultist cart — a twenty-foot square, mental rather than audible, meant to catch anyone stepping in or out of it in the night. She lays it from over thirty feet off, so the cultists cannot see the working, and folds her whispered casting into what looks like a conversation with a nearby ally.
3. She wakes about an hour or two into her sleep to the alarm — mental, silent to the camp. When she lifts her head to look, it is only one of the caravan's own guards leaning on the cart mid-patrol. She lies back down.
4. The rest of the night passes without further incident — a first uneventful night of what is to be a two-month road.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 15}}

\page

# Session 16 {{float:"right" 2025-07-13}}
{wide}

## 1 - A quiet day
_~08:00, 7 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The caravan rolls out under a clear sky and travels a full day on the road: open plains, sparse copses of trees, no travellers on the road and no trouble in sight.
2. The rest of the day passes without incident. Nightfall camp goes down at another of the road's stopping points; **Eguzkilore** volunteers for the last four hours of the watch on the strength of his elven trance, banking a little more goodwill with the caravan's guards, and the second night on the road ends as quietly as the first.
3. At breakfast the next morning each merchant serves his own guards from his own stores: **Lai**'s porridge is cheap and hot; **Edhelri** serves better porridge with fruit; **Noohar** tops his with a little honey for flavour.
4. The caravan is two days out of Baldur's Gate; the country is open plains, sparse copses of trees, and the low tumulus-shaped hills on the horizon that mark the outer edge of the Trollclaws — five days off at their pace.
5. Over porridge the caravan guards make small talk with the party: everyone shares the same wish for a quiet two months of road, because when something happens they have to work, and none of them like working when it means risking their necks.

\column

## 2 - Amidamaru's boar
_~11:00, 8 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Amidamaru keeps Munin overhead all morning, careful to stay within a hundred feet of the owl — beyond that tether the telepathic contact and the sight through his eyes both drop. He keeps the bird close.
2. Four hours out from camp, Munin catches a boar off the road, roughly five hundred feet away. Amidamaru relays it to Nuberroca low, not to the whole group, so the caravan does not tie the message to the bird.
3. Nuberroca announces to Edhelri that he is going to fetch food, gets waved off without protest, and sprints out with a crossbow. A twenty-minute chase brings him inside a hundred and fifty feet; he snaps off a long shot he does not expect to land, and instead sinks a bolt into the boar's haunch.
4. The boar turns rather than flees. Nuberroca is already laughing as it charges; he plants the spear butt into the ground and the boar drives itself onto the shaft. He lifts the carcass clear on the shaft over his head.
5. He smears himself in the boar's blood on the walk back — no river within a day and a half — and rejoins the caravan half an hour later, still dripping, shouting to anyone who will listen that lunch is on him.
6. Told the Chionthar is two days and a half behind and the Winding Water still five days off — and that no one recommends bathing in the second one — Nuberroca cleans the blood off with wet grass and bark instead. A man of the outdoors, the group agrees.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 16}}
\page

## 3 - The midday halt
_~14:00, 8 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Two in the afternoon, the caravan pulls off the road to eat. It is forty in all — thirty-odd guards, the merchants, the family, the two travelling carts — and the fires collapse into a single circle around the boar.
2. **Beyd** volunteers to cook the roast, claiming a caravaneer's practice with spits. Nuberroca donates one of his four spears — puts it through the carcass end to end and hands it over, drawing a mild rebuke about gutting the boar first.
3. The spit is jury-rigged between two forked sticks. Beyd asks Nuberroca to turn it — the goliath has the strength, Beyd does not — and while Nuberroca turns, Beyd bastes the roast with a splash of his own beer.
4. While the boar cooks Amidamaru slips onto his own cart with Munin on his shoulder, casts *invisibility* on the owl, and sends him through the tarps of the three cultist wagons in turn. Every one holds the same nailed-shut crates and no one inside.
5. Meals divide by employer at the plates as they did before. Beyd works his fire, selling beer at a silver a jar; he gives Nuberroca two jars free on the boar and Nuberroca drinks both on the spot.
6. The travelling family comes to the fire. Nuberroca waves them in warmly. Mialee joins them to make conversation. A gnome — travelling separately from the family though he shares their cart — sits apart, working the guards for a dice game.
7. The cultists come to the fire last. Their guard — the same one who had turned Nuberroca off the cultist wagons at an earlier camp — draws his knife to cut a slice without a word. Nuberroca catches the wrist, lifts the man near clear of the ground, and tells him he needs to ask permission first.
8. The cultist folds — they are merchants bound for Waterdeep, their guards had assumed the roast was for sharing — and Nuberroca steps back with a warning: the soldiers had best be well fed for whatever lies ahead. They take a slice and go.
9. The gnome, **Radecere Perethun**, asks Mialee's group for a game. She sits down opposite him. Five dice each, highest scores; the gnome opens with four fives and a six, exceptional, and Mialee comes over the top with four sixes and a five.
10. He asks for double-or-nothing and loses again. He asks for one more, four silver in the pot; he throws three sixes, a five and a four, near-unbeatable, and Mialee still lands one point over him. Radecere pays out seven silver total, retreats to his cart, and eats his slice of boar alone.
11. Mialee remarks afterwards that she had planned to cheat and never needed to. Amidamaru — watching from the far side — assumed she had cheated the whole time regardless.

\column

## 4 - The evening ride
_~19:00, 8 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Four hours further, well into the evening, the caravan is closing on the small tumulus-like hills that mark the outer edge of troll country — still five days out. Beyd's horses stay poorly kept as before, freed to graze only when he remembers, mostly ignored while he sells beer.
2. Munin's cover slips along the ride: he is a night bird flying under a hard sun, and one of the caravan guards asks Amidamaru how he keeps the owl awake. Amidamaru admits it openly — the owl is his familiar, he hides him only when he is working.
3. What the guards do talk about, over and over, is the trolls: not every caravan on this road makes it out, and the fear runs the full length of the line.
4. Camp goes down for the night in one of the pre-established stopping points that dot the road — a cleared and reused spot with good sightlines. Eguzkilore offers a full four-hour shift on the strength of his elven trance; Nuberroca takes another.
5. The party debates whether to move on the cultists tonight. Amidamaru presses for information — they still know almost nothing. Nuberroca offers to grab one and beat it out of him. Mialee counters that a missing man means questions the next morning. Eguzkilore's proposal wins the room — wait for troll country, when a disappearance can be blamed on the road — and Nuberroca stands down, agreeing to turn in.
6. The night passes without incident.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 16}}
\page

## 5 - The men in the road
_~08:00, 9 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Wake, breakfast — no boar left, back to the standard fare. Mialee slips a small illusion into Nuberroca's plate, a smiley worked into the porridge. Nuberroca spots the trick, grins the same grin back at her, and tells her she is a good one.
2. Beyd works his fire again and pitches beer as morning hydration under a hard sun. No takers today — the only regular wine-drinker in the party, Nyx, is not thirsty.
3. About three and a half hours down the road, closing on the day's usual break, Munin from overhead catches a knot of figures standing across the road ahead — perhaps four hundred feet off, in a small stand of trees.
4. Amidamaru signals the caravan to halt. The lead driver pulls up on a single guard's word, and murmurs run down the line asking why.
5. The tortle advances up the road with two guards behind him. Nuberroca peels off into the tree line to the west without any pretence at stealth — a nine-foot blue giant, in his own words, invisible only to the willing.
6. Eguzkilore falls in behind Amidamaru. Mialee stays with Edhelri on the wagon. Kaelthar, arriving late to the scene, joins Mialee at the rear.
7. At speaking distance the party makes out five men. The one in the middle wears richer clothes and carries himself as the leader; at his belt hangs a strange cylinder of metal with a wooden butt, the like of which none of them have ever seen.
8. The leader greets them and asks who speaks for the caravan. Amidamaru steps forward: no formal spokesperson, but they can help. The leader turns it around — the caravan will enter the fields of the dead in three or four days, and it will need protection.

\column

## 6 - The negotiation
_~11:30, 9 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Amidamaru points to the caravan guards behind him — this caravan has hired protection already. Ignoring that, the leader names the price: two hundred gold across the caravan, until the Trollclaws. Nuberroca, still sitting on a tree stump like a king on his throne, laughs at the word *trolls* — he has faced a dragon and been unimpressed.
2. Amidamaru sends a guard back to the merchants with a counter-offer at fifty gold, and reads the situation quietly to him — fifty-fifty these are bandits, closer to eighty-twenty. On the strength of Nuberroca's dragon boast, the leader drops his own price to a hundred and fifty.
3. Amidamaru sends Munin toward the sound of muffled laughter he keeps hearing in the trees on the far side of the meeting. The leader spots the owl instantly — *why is an owl flying by day?* — and his four crossbowmen come up with weapons on Munin.
4. Amidamaru makes Munin vanish in the instant before the first bolt looses; the bolt whistles through the empty air where the owl had been, and the four crossbowmen stand there gaping and reloading. The leader takes it for wizardry: *"¿qué te crees, que nunca hemos visto un mago?"*.
5. Eguzkilore's eyes on the trees find the shape of a bandit behind every trunk for thirty yards — six or seven of them crouched in the trees to the north and west, more figures than the five in the road. He readies a shot for the first hostile move.
6. Nuberroca stays on the stump, hears the crossbows around him cocking one after another, and calls it out loud and clear: the crossbows are aimed — let the fight begin. He sets himself ready to answer any strike on him with a killing fury.
7. Kaelthar takes hold of his sword-hilt but keeps it sheathed. The leader ups the price back to two hundred on the party's posture, and gives them a moment to accept the offer or turn around — hand never leaving the cylinder at his belt.
8. Amidamaru offers the last number the merchants would carry — fifty gold, one small stipend, take it or leave it. The leader calls fifty pieces an insult. Nuberroca stands, shouts back to have it their way, and steps behind the cover of a tree with Eguzkilore, roaring into a full fury as the fight opens.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 16}}
\page

## 7 - The ambush

1. The leader gives the word to his crew — at them. The first bolt from the tree line comes for Nuberroca, chips bark off the trunk, misses.
2. Nuberroca's readied crossbow answers before the shooter's next breath — bolt through the neck, the bandit falls out of the tree dead. Eguzkilore, who had readied his own shot on the same man, sees him drop and holds his arrow for the leader instead.
3. Eleven more bolts arc at Nuberroca from the treeline in the next volley. Four bite, but between his rage and the cover of the trunk barely a scratch bleeds through — a blue glow settles over him and the scratches close as they open.
4. One of Amidamaru's two escort guards takes a bolt at the first volley, foams blood at the mouth and falls dead in seconds — the caravan's first loss.
5. A second volley cracks against Amidamaru — twelve bolts, most turned in mid-flight by a curtain of blue light around the tortoise's shell. Two get through: one lodges at the base of his neck, another sinks in at the temple. He is left on his feet, bleeding badly from the throat and the skull, and drops flat behind his cover.
6. Two of the mercenary captains bring up the strange metal cylinders — a thunder-crack cuts through the trees, and Nuberroca catches one of the shots square: a piece of metal buried under his collarbone.
7. A blue glow settles over Nuberroca — the flesh closes around the metal ball where it stays lodged.
8. Mialee, still hidden with the wagons, casts a cloud of fog across the eastern half of the field — the archers on the far right are blinded to the caravan and vice versa, and Mialee slips into her own fog invisible to the shooters.
9. Amidamaru, prone under his cover, calls up a *chromatic orb* of acid from a diamond in his palm and hurls it at one of the mercenary captains. The orb splashes hard: the captain's clothes begin to melt off him, and he claws at himself and screams.
10. Nuberroca steps out of a wisp of cloud on the far side of the battlefield and puts himself behind the screen of bandits guarding the leader, driving a spear thrust through one who staggers but holds.
11. Kaelthar runs from the caravan into the fight and catches a bolt in one shoulder on the way in. He tries breathing fire through the fog toward the left-flank archers; the cone catches three of them but they shake off most of it, and the sheltering tree behind them catches fire.
12. Amidamaru, prone in his cover, casts a slumber spell across the tight cluster of bandits around the leader. Two go down where they stood — a bandit and the leader himself, the same man who had opened the negotiation.
13. Eguzkilore drops one of the exposed archers with an arrow — a clean shot; the bandit falls dead.
14. Nuberroca leaps at another of the mercenary captains through the fog's edge — a lance strike opens the man up, then the polearm's butt hammers into his neck and the captain crumples to the ground barely alive. Nuberroca shouts Amidamaru's name and legs it back to cover, dropping flat behind his tree; a blue glow settles over him and his wounds close.
15. Mialee moves through her own fog unseen — the bandits on the left cannot find her. She reaches one of them on their flank and cuts him down with a scimitar in the back.
16. Amidamaru calls another *chromatic orb* out of his diamond — this one fire — and it splashes on a bandit further up the tree line. The bandit ignites and collapses into a pile of ash; Kaelthar and the two bandits beside him are left staring at what remains.
17. The dragonborn, isolated on the fog's edge, is ringed by four bandits — heavy strikes bite through his guard and he holds only briefly before two more blows put him unconscious.
18. Eguzkilore fires at the enemies fighting Kaelthar despite the odds of hitting his friend, the shot lands clean and a bandit drops dead.
19. ***[EPIC]*** Nuberroca closes on the ring around the paladin, drops his spear for the halberd, and swings — a reckless cut that takes the first bandit at the ribs, drives clean through his spine and buries in the second. The first falls in two pieces; the second sinks to his knees and dies. Nuberroca calls back to the party to regroup — the ambushers are running — and what is left of them breaks and scatters into the woods.

## 8 - After the ambush
_~12:00, 9 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Kaelthar is bleeding out on his back. Eguzkilore pulls a healing potion from his own pack, tips it into Kaelthar's mouth, and Kaelthar opens his eyes barely alive — Eguzkilore over him, Amidamaru behind, Nuberroca off to his left; and behind them the caravan guards standing over two of their own dead on the ground beside a fallen bandit.
2. Two caravan guards have fallen in the fight; the party accounted for most of the ambushers themselves, with the guards' bolts contributing at range once they closed on the fight.
3. Nuberroca goes to the captain he had knocked out with his polearm butt — the man is dying on the ground — and stabilises him with his own healer's kit, then binds his wrists with cord out of his pack.
4. Amidamaru ties off the two men still asleep from the same spell — one captain, one common bandit — and the party ends the fight with three prisoners: the leader, a captain and one bandit.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 16}}

\page

# Session 17 {{float:"right" 2025-07-20}}
{wide}

## 1 - Looting the dead
_~12:30, 9 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The party works through the dead alongside the caravan guards while the merchants dig two graves in the road — the bandits to a shared shallow pit, the two fallen guards to their own deeper mound with piled stones against the wolves. The plentiful crossbow bolts scattered around are gathered back into the quivers.
2. The two captains and the lead bandit are stripped first: pooled coin and a scimitar. The strange metal cylinders are claimed by Amidamaru, one broken, one whole, along with a horn of black powder and a bag of small iron pellets.
3. The rest of the bandit dead yield some more coins, one of them foreign, some weapons and two bottles of hard drink. Coin gets divided among the party, and Eguzkilore pockets one of the rum bottles for Nuberroca.
4. Mialee tries to repair the crushed pistol with her magic. She manages to straighten the tube and glue the wood but not re-assemble a mechanism she does not understand. She also rounds a lead ball back to shape, giving Amidamaru one usable round.
5. **Edhelri** waits at the head of the line and the merchants are visibly tired of the wait: an hour is turning into two.

\column

## 2 - The lodged bullet

1. Nyx focuses on Nuberroca's collarbone wound — it isn't magic, it's a projectile, and something is still lodged inside.
2. Amidamaru wakes the leader of the bandits and presses the intact pistol against his eyes, finger on the trigger, asking how it works. The captain, calm at first, tries to lie — he tells Amidamaru to grip it by the metal part and aim with the wood, the wooden butt sold as the barrel. Amidamaru, an artificer who has spent the better part of an hour on the weapon, calls the bluff and brings the muzzle closer to the captain's head.
3. Pressed on the road ahead, the captain names the Fields of the Dead — an old massive battlefield north of Baldur's Gate. Do not make fire at night, do not call attention: the dead there do not rest as well as they should. Amidamaru's read on the man matches: terrified, straight-answering, telling truth.
4. Asked for the weapons, the leader spurts lies. Amidamaru pulls the trigger, but the pistol is unloaded and nothing happens; the captain breathes back into himself, the pistol is no longer a threat.
5. Nyx steps up at Amidamaru's shoulder with an arrow nocked, and the pressure changes hands. The captain says they were hired months ago by merchants from a city he cannot name, paid in these curious weapons; the powder is for winter fires, he offers — the lie a giveaway of how little he intends to explain, and Amidamaru's own time of study is not enough to reconstruct the loading from combat memory alone. Their camp is west along a bearing he points out, and empty of anything worth taking — they sleep in the woods and only take rooms at taverns when the coin is fresh.
6. Nuberroca crosses to the captain who shot him and asks him plainly *"¿cómo se saca esta bola demoníaca que me has tirado?"* — the man answers honestly: they've dug bullets out a few times, and the men mostly die of fevers in the days after. Nuberroca tells him back, low and level, that if he dies of it the captain dies first — the man goes white.
7. ***[EPIC]*** Nyx pours vodka along the dagger to clean it; Kaelthar leans in and talks her through it, enough to guide her hand but not to steady the cut. Nuberroca drops into a full rage to ride out the pain. Nyx digs, does not sever an artery, and finally levers the pellet out; blood is sheeting out of the shoulder by the time she has it in her palm.
8. Mialee wraps the wound with strips torn from the dead bandits' clothing while a blue glow staunches the bleeding. Kaelthar uses a measure of his healing touch, but the wound itself is still open when they are done.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 17}}
\page

## 3 - The prisoners

1. Amidamaru opens the argument to the group: leave the three bound to a tree and let the road settle them. Nuberroca refuses and takes them for himself — properly fed they can pull their weight, and he has already promised the wounded captain a matched death if he tries anything. He walks them to Edhelri and posts them; she will not have three bandits riding on a wagon and says so, and they walk beside, tied.
2. Before the caravan re-forms, Mialee crosses to Edhelri and asks the names of the two guards who fell in the ambush — **Rand** from her own group and **Lews** from Noohar's. She then hands over five gold pieces, for their families alongside the salaries they were owed. The coin passes down the line hand-to-hand between the guards, in a way that tells the party they look after their own.
3. Later on the ride, Mialee presses a separate case with Edhelri: two guards lost, the surviving bandits could be persuaded to fill the gaps. Edhelri warms to the idea on the strength of the pitch, but Nuberroca — pulled into the conversation as the merchant's sergeant — stonewalls: the men wanted to rob them, not join them.
4. Nuberroca settles the argument with a truce: one week bound, and if they are still with the caravan out of troll country they can walk with pay. Mialee accepts — the point in her head was persuading Nuberroca, and she got it.

\column

## 4 - The forced march
_~14:00, 9 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The party lays the dead in the ground and forms up — an hour and a half lost between the interrogation and the burials — and forces the march to make the next camping place before nightfall. Meals are eaten cold on the wagons, dry rations. When the wounded captain begins to fall behind, Nuberroca lifts him onto his own shoulder rather than let him drop.
2. Amidamaru works Munin at height over the road for the afternoon and evening. Among the guards Eguzkilore is now openly welcome for taking full four-hour watches on his elven trance. Meanwhile they find quiet ways to make the prisoners' walk unpleasant — a stumble here, a polearm haft as if by accident there.
3. At camp the wagons wheel into the usual circle; **Sulesdeg** *"the Pole"*, Beyd's bodyguard, comes across without ceremony, sets a jar of beer in front of each of the party members, quietly thanks them, and reminds them the jars are returned in the morning. Mialee hands hers to Nuberroca.
4. Nyx sets a warding rite on **Noohar**'s cart — the perfumes on his wagon make it the sweetest-smelling of the round. The night passes without incident.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 17}}
\page

## 5 - Fever
_~08:00, 10 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. At breakfast Amidamaru sends Munin over to Nuberroca — he does not want the suspected cultists to see him tending the goliath himself — and reads the wound through the owl's eyes. The bandage is soaked, and though a healing spell closes the skin the pus is welling under the seam. The flesh is red and hot, and the fever is coming in.
2. Four hours of road later Nuberroca is sweating hard and staggering; Edhelri, gentle about it, asks him to walk apart from the line in case the fever is catching.
3. The party rides on and passes into the **Fields of the Dead**. Munin brings back the picture of a country of grass and low tumuli, no trees to speak of, and — more troubling — the occasional carcass of a boar or a stag long dead and untouched by carrion birds.
4. Over lunch Amidamaru pours a stronger healing spell into the shoulder — the pain and the redness ease, but the fever holds; magic has closed the wound and the infection is inside the seam. Kaelthar readies the paladin's sense for the undead and holds it prepared for when the need arises.
5. Amidamaru rides forward to the head of the caravan and pulls the guards of the two cultist wagons into conversation. They admit — with more assessment than warmth — that the party carries the fighting, and one of them notes with mild fascination that the blue giant looks in rough shape.
6. Behind him a small commotion on the caravan — a stumble and a landing — and when Amidamaru turns he sees Nuberroca on the road, unconscious under the fever. Delirious, the goliath prays aloud through his teeth: *"Madre Árbol, mi carne está podrida por la sombra, pero mi espíritu no cederá. Enséñame qué raíz debo arrancar para expulsar esta enfermedad"*. **Beyd** rides across from his own wagon and presses a small cup into Nuberroca's mouth: a sweet, warm wine from his homeland, used for the fevers there. It takes some of the pain off, and the goliath is helped into the wagon under a blanket for the last leg of the day.

\column

## 6 - Lights in the tumuli
_~19:30, 10 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The caravan pulls off the road for supper at the day's stopping-place — inside the Fields now, the road serpentines around the mounds and visibility off the shoulder is poor. Nuberroca is fed inside his wagon; the party eats around the fire.
2. Nyx catches a smear of light between two mounds off to the west — nine points, moving low and erratic, sometimes crossing under the ground and out again. She leaves the fire and works out to a rock at half-distance, hidden. Nyx's mental message reaches Amidamaru with the count and confirmation; he calls Munin back onto his shoulder to see through the owl's eyes too.
3. Kaelthar activates the paladin's sense for the undead and moves closer; a moment later his sense flickers on and off — between four and ten undead, moving beneath the earth.
4. Amidamaru calls out to the merchants, asking what the lights are. **Eldkin** answers in their stead — a caravan guard who has run this route many times — and admits the fault: distracted by the bandits and Nuberroca's collapse, the caravan forgot to keep fires down. Her stories: few travellers survive the lights, and none if they are already noticed.
5. Amidamaru turns himself invisible and walks west past Nyx, dropping a small orb of fire into a stand of dead branches at range — two of the nine lights peel out of formation and drift toward the burn. Four pebbles lit by artificer's magic and thrown further west draw off two more — four wisps drawn off in all, five left to fight.
6. Kaelthar puts a ward against the wicked on himself as a precaution, standing at the end of the party's line — the last thing he sees on the way in is a wisp materialising at his own side.
7. ***[EPIC]*** A crack of light against Kaelthar's flank drops him to one knee, his teeth close on his own scream and it comes out as a bellow. The other lights, all of them, wheel to the noise. Combat is on.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 17}}
\page

## 7 - The will-o-wisps

1. Nyx puts an arrow through the closest wisp at long range — the shaft passes through and drops to the ground but the light flickers; they're solid enough, just hard to hurt. Mialee mirrors the shot at a second and marks a hit — Echo, her bat familiar, can use its echolocation to find the invisible ones.
2. Amidamaru drops his shield in the dirt, hums his wizard's war-song to steady the blade, and closes with the rapier — a thunder-charm left ringing on the edge for any wisp that moves once the strike lands.
3. Kaelthar, surrounded and unable to see his attackers, pulls out of the ring with careful footing, spends his healing touch to close his own wounds, and falls back as far as his feet allow. The wisps' pattern reads: appear at the shoulder, strike, blink out. Each landed hit cracks hard with electricity; each miss unspools into the earth. All of them concentrate on Kaelthar — his first scream told them where the living were.
4. Nuberroca — watching from the wagon — remembers older wars: his tribe fought these heralds of the undead armies before, and the shamans used things that emitted light to end them, sometimes blessing the warriors' weapons for more damage. He pours holy water along the edge of his halberd, saved for a real cut. Nyx and Eguzkilore hold their arrows for the moment a wisp materialises, striking as it appears, and Eguzkilore steadies his aim, each shot a little surer. Mialee stays hidden, striking from the shadows.
5. Kaelthar goes down on a critical hit — one strike does it. The wisps, sensing the unconscious body, gather over him to feed. Amidamaru closes on the fallen paladin and pours a healing spell into him, hauling him back to his feet. Nuberroca lays a warband's vigour on Amidamaru so the mage can hold his ground at Kaelthar's shoulder.
6. ***[EPIC]*** Nuberroca lines the halberd against a wisp harrying Kaelthar and swings; the strike lands, one drop of holy water dripping down the shaft finds the wisp, and it moans out — a low keening like a soul in torment. A later swing puts the water more squarely, and this second wisp shrieks in pain, the light of it flaring before dimming. Nuberroca has found the weapon.
7. Kaelthar drops again — a glancing hit finding him barely healed. Nuberroca reaches him with the crook of the halberd, drags him back into range, and Amidamaru's second healing spell goes into the paladin as he passes.

\column

8. Kaelthar drops a third time under another critical strike from an invisible wisp — Amidamaru has nothing left to cast. Mialee, hidden and reading the field through Echo, uses an unseen hand to feed one of her potions into the paladin's mouth; Kaelthar comes back to consciousness on the ground. Nuberroca lays a warband's vigour on him this time for extra vitality, and a red glow comes across his wounds, partially closing them.
9. Amidamaru is knocked flat by a critical strike from an invisible wisp.
10. ***[EPIC]*** As the wisp turns to feed on the tortle's body, Munin dives from above and takes the blast that would have killed him — the owl throws itself between the wisp and its master and dissolves under the light. A moment later, Nuberroca uses one of Amidamaru's own healing potions to bring him back.
11. The last wisps drop in short succession. Mialee's sneak-strike from the shadows catches one and its light begins to dim; Nyx sinks the last of her Dread Ambusher psychic bite into an arrow, and the final wisp winks out with a moan across the tumuli. Eguzkilore lifts a follow-up shot on the final wisp but the target is already dead. The party stands in the silent dark, most of them wounded and one — Kaelthar — unconscious on the ground again, stabilised only by Nuberroca's healer's hands.


## 8 - After the wisps

1. Nuberroca lifts Kaelthar onto his shoulder and carries him back into the light of the wagons. The guards close on the party with torches; Amidamaru waves them back and asks them to douse the light — don't attract any more of them — the party can see in the dark well enough to bring Kaelthar in.
2. Nuberroca has faced these things before with his tribe, and he can now tell the others: the lights feed on the unconscious. That is why they crowded around Kaelthar; that is why Munin's death bought Amidamaru his life — the familiar would have interposed itself.
3. By the fire that night the ordeal settles into them — the wisps' cold light burned into memory, the appear-strike-blink cadence learned in bone and hand. Footwork sharpens, strikes fall cleaner, an enemy's next move comes faster in the read. What the tumuli tried to take from them, they carry out the other side as strength.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 17}}

\page

# Session 18 {{float:"right" 2025-08-10}}
{wide}

## 1 - Silent darkness
_~20:00, 10 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The lights have not come back. Kaelthar is stable on the ground, unconscious with the field dressing just barely holding him, and Nyx puts one of her berries into his mouth — the paladin stirs awake, still groggy. The camp fires are out and so is the mood, merchants and guards on cold dry rations, the caravan subdued around the ring.
2. Amidamaru crosses to the suspected cultist carts to speak with the guard who had come to the wisp fight and finds he is in fact a driver. Bloodied, he sits near them, and another driver presses a handkerchief into his hands for his wounds.
3. When Amidamaru asks what he would rather see done with the captured bandits — dead or free — the driver answers it is none of his business while they are not with the caravan, and he would not condone killing unarmed men; Amidamaru and Nyx read him as lying to keep up a civil front — the driver would rather see them dead.
4. Having whispered the plan to Nuberroca earlier, Amidamaru summons a small illusion of a wisp near the goliath. Nuberroca sells the panic, and **Sulesdeg** comes tearing in with a sword nearly the size of the goliath's — the blade cuts through the fake wisp and buries itself in the ground, and the guards raise their crossbows around the ring.
5. As the dust settles, Amidamaru overhears one of the cultists mutters that they should not trust the blue giant — the muttering guard's face registers as faintly familiar. Claws out, he rounds on them: how could they speak that way of the people saving their skins day after day? They had not fought the bandits or the wisps; when the trolls came, they would beg on their knees to be saved, and then they would see who saved them. He throws the handkerchief back in the guard's face.
6. Nuberroca steps up aggressively, and some cultist aim their crossbows at him. Amidamaru holds the goliath back — only uncultured and intolerant people — and when he presses that they had not even fought the bandits, one guard admits he had been there, pinning the familiarity.
7. The party settles for sleep; Eguzkilore takes the first four hours on his elven trance, which lets the rest of the party sleep uninterrupted and earns him some goodwill with the caravan's watch.

\column

## 2 - Exotic feathers
_~08:30, 11 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The night passes without incident. Nuberroca's fever lifts on the long rest. Amidamaru wakes earlier than the rest to burn incense and re-cast the ritual that calls **Munin** — the owl the wisps killed the night before. The caravan drivers keep well clear of what may or may not be hallucinogenic. Party sets out shortly after.
2. Perhaps an hour on the road, a bird crosses the sky against their line of march — long tail, red chest, blue and yellow wings, nothing anyone at the caravan has seen before and plainly the most beautiful bird any of them have ever seen in their lives. Sulesdeg raises his crossbow and takes the bird down with two bolts. The exotic bird is lugged into his wagon in a bloody heap.
3. A second bird — this one green — crosses at greater range some time later. Sulesdeg tries again but the shot is too long; the bolt goes wide. A third time, a flock of small birds boils up from behind a rise ahead and streams away over the wagons. Nyx takes it as a warning — when things fall out of the ordinary in this world, there is always a reason, and the reason is rarely a good one. She asks **Lai** whether the caravan can spare the time to check; he warns that stopping costs them their next camping-place. She goes forward alone anyway, and Amidamaru sends Munin overhead to shadow her.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 18}}
\page

## 3 - The bird merchant
_~11:00, 11 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Cresting the rise, Nyx sees an overturned wagon defended by a handful of guards and one man in richer clothing — arrows still coming from a scatter of goblinoids around them, and a bugbear closing in to hammer the wagon between crossbow volleys. She hides and reads the field; there are several of them, and the shapes are hobgoblins, bugbears, and goblins in mixed cover. Amidamaru and Eguzkilore catch up on her steps and drop into cover.
2. Nyx opens with a shot to distract the pressure on the wagon — the arrow lands, and she calls out for the goblinoids to stop attacking and run, because a serious contingent is on its way and they are in trouble. The answer the goblinoids give is to turn on the party; a bugbear closes on Amidamaru, grabs him and drags him toward the rest without apparent effort.
3. Amidamaru sings up the shadow-blade and takes the bugbear on the spot — the strike lands, leaving a thunder reverberation waiting to crack on the creature. Nyx swaps her focus from a dying hobgoblin to a fresh target across the field, and the arrows keep finding shoulders and throats. From deeper in the goblinoid line, a heavier hobgoblin barks orders.
4. The party allies come up over the rise — a few of the caravan guards and the suspected cultists put arrows across the field at the bugbear nearest to Amidamaru, drawing blood but not dropping it.
5. Nyx takes a poisoned bolt to the shoulder and holds it — she can feel the poison burning across her skin — keeping the mark on her target through the pain. Kaelthar manages to join the fight; still too far for a javelin, so he uses his crossbow while closing in.
6. ***[EPIC]*** When the hobgoblin captain finally turns to run, Eguzkilore drives through a second volley of shots and puts three arrows into his running back — the fourth catches him as he falls. The rest of the hobgoblins fall back in disciplined formation, covering each other in leapfrog — a trained, heavily-militarised line, nothing like the goblins' panic — and the party holds fire as they clear.

\column

## 4 - Oyn Evenmor

1. Three battered guards and the man in the fine coat come out from under the wagon: **Oyn Evenmor**, an exotic-bird merchant bound for Waterdeep, who bows and thanks the party for his life. Nyx introduces the party by name; Nuberroca and the rest catch up. Oyn admits he should have hired the Company of the Boar when they offered at the last tavern and did not, and he has lost most of his cargo — the cages sit open with dead birds inside or empty on the road.
2. Amidamaru magically mends some cages and casts a healing spell into each of the three guards, one after another. Oyn presses three coins of gold into Nyx's hand and closes her fingers over them — she reads it as light payment for the man's obvious wealth, but does not press.
3. ***[EPIC]*** Oyn thinks to ask after his birds. Nuberroca answers, straight-faced, that one of their own caravan has already caught one, and points at Sulesdeg. Sulesdeg produces the plucked, bloodied bird from the wagon on demand. Oyn's face goes rabid, then collapses; he sinks halfway to the ground before recovering, and rises telling the guard he is the worst person he has ever met, with the most twisted humour he has ever seen, undeserving of the air he breathes — that was the most valuable bird he carried. He turns his back and tells his own guards to hurry the repair.
4. Amidamaru buys two broken metal birdcages off Oyn — filigree wire, good for tinkering. He also asks a guard whether they have crossed anything strange on the road behind them: no, only the usual traffic, no cloaked strangers, and about four or five groups making toward Waterdeep.
5. Eguzkilore searches the hobgoblin captain — a longbow and a half-plate that the heroes mend and keep. The party recovers some arrows and lifts several poisoned shafts from the hobgoblins, then search the bodies for valuables, retrieving some silver coins.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 18}}
\page

## 5 - The wayside tavern
_~20:00, 11 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. As evening comes on, the caravan reaches a wayside inn on the road — **The Wayside Rest**, an undefended roadhouse with one tavern-keeper, **Olver**, who greets the party and offers rooms, food, and repair. Nyx pays a gold for a shared upstairs room with Mialee, then buys a round of beer for the six. The others share rooms, except Amidamaru who will sleep outside in his shell against the autumn cold.
2. A second party is already at the tavern — a table of four humans, warily eating: a barbarian in furs, a rogue in finer dress, a paladin with the trappings of a religious order, and a warlock at the back. Their leader climbs up on his own table and announces the company to the assembled merchants as **Jorath Vexundar**, famed leader of the *Company of the Boar* and slayer of villains without number; the paladin **Kaldan Voss**, who felled the lich of the Fields of Death; the warlock **Thorvin Caldorath**, who alone brought down a mountain giant with his magic; and the barbarian **Khorvalis**, the strongest of all time — a hire-on-the-spot pitch.
3. Nyx climbs onto Nuberroca's shoulders and answers back with her own, naming herself **Nyx Tukog** and the party the *Company of the Roe deer*, listing the wisps and the bandits and adding, small, that she had not even wet herself over the trolls — and gets no applause, since everyone already knew her.
4. At a separate table sit three wealthy travelers: a knight in armour, a mage in strange robes, and a nobleman. Kaelthar asks after the blazon he saw on their wagon; the noble brushes him off and, once Kaelthar is far enough away, whispers something in the knight's ear that the paladin cannot catch.
5. Nyx presses Jorath on the trolls — the Company of the Boar is credited with a good count of them — and Jorath prices the answer at one piece of gold. Nyx pays; Jorath draws her close and whispers the trick, then reveals a pendant against his chest with a very large tusk.
6. Nuberroca wanders across to the Company of the Boar's table and challenges Khorvalis to a pre-dinner brawl — *"¿te gustaría que nos pegáramos unas hostias?"*. Khorvalis declines — he reserves his strength for real enemies. When the trolls come up in Nyx's question and Khorvalis reckons them bigger than Nuberroca, the goliath invokes his people's gift and enlarges himself in answer. Khorvalis stays where he is — he does not use dirty tricks like magic to look stronger or to frighten his companions. Sulesdeg's beer falls over from the shock of the size-shift.

\column

## 6 - The chest in the cart
_~22:00, 11 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Mialee has been laying a plan through the dinner: she casts a silent image of herself at her table, and Amidamaru turns the elf invisible over the copy. The double stays at the table doing the small motions of a conversation while the rogue, unseen, slips out to the yard where the wagons are parked. Olver, at the door, sees the door open and close and mutters about the strong winds.
2. Nyx catches enough of the nobles' conversation at the other table to know they are in a hurry to Waterdeep — their cargo is beginning to spoil. The nobles rise and climb to their private room upstairs; Nuberroca, back in his normal size, follows and is pushed out at the door by the knight — through the closed door he catches talk of plebeians who could never amount to much, and the words *cursed mutants*.
3. Mialee reaches the cultist wagon and works her lockpicks on a chest inside, taking long enough that the illusory copy of herself at the table blinks out, causing a stir in the room, though she is still invisible on the way back and returns to her chair without being seen. What she found she keeps to herself, simply saying that there are people around and this is no safe place to talk.
4. As the party turns in for the night, a sudden heavy rain breaks over the roof and does not let up — Amidamaru keeps to his shell outside through the full of it.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 18}}

\page

# Session 19 {{float:"right" 2025-08-19}}
{wide}

## 1 - The chalice revealed
_~08:00, 12 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Morning in **The Wayside Rest**, **Olver**'s inn. The heavy rain that broke over the roof the night before is still coming down. The party gather in the privacy of their upstairs room and Mialee shows what she lifted the previous night. A **golden chalice**, its interior crusted with old blood, from the chest she had picked aboard the cultist wagon. Along with it a bundle of other valuables of the same cut — plate, buckle-work, a short knife with chased hilt — read by the party as loot that could as easily have come out of **Greenest** weeks before.
2. The chalice is treated as confirmation of what the party has long suspected about the three merchant wagons at the front of the caravan: cultists in the convoy, from the same operation that burned Greenest. The question in the room shifts from *are they* to *how do we move on it before Waterdeep*.

## 2 - A matter of horses

1. By mid-morning the common room has thinned, and **Lord Eldrin Kaldur** crosses it to the long table where the lead merchants are working through their own accounts. His knight **Jaxen Korrith**, who doubles as the cart's driver, stands a pace behind him in half-armour; his wizard **Thalor Cheston** keeps apart at the hearth, warming his hands.
2. The nobleman's pitch is brief: he and his are bound for Waterdeep, their cargo cannot wait on an escort of their own, and he would travel in company to the city rather than risk the open road alone. The merchants weigh it and agree. A travel-share is settled and a handshake is given.
3. The business of his horses is the longer conversation. Lord Kaldur does not conceal his contempt for the beasts — useless, hollow-flanked, half-dead on the yoke, and sure to founder before Waterdeep without a change. He will buy fresh horses from any merchant at the table who will sell, and he will not haggle over the price.
4. The merchants exchange looks and refuse: they need every animal they have for the days ahead. Lord Kaldur receives the refusal with ill grace and blames the gods for his luck.
5. From their own corner of the room the party watch the display over the dregs of breakfast. Nuberroca, who still carries the murmured *cursed mutants* that came through the closed door at him the night before, leans back on his bench and remarks loud enough to carry that between him and the lord, he is still working out which of them is the mutant.

\column

## 3 - Fired at the inn

1. At the common-room tables, the **Company of the Boar** — **Jorath Vexundar**, **Kaldan Voss**, **Thorvin Caldorath**, and **Khorvalis** — close a hire with the merchants. The pitch wins out: four seasoned sellswords with trolls on the road not many days ahead. The rate the Company names is near the top of what the caravan's purse can bear; the merchants cannot stretch it to cover two guard contingents at once.
2. The merchants trim the roll on the spot. Amidamaru, Mialee, Nuberroca, and Kaelthar are paid out what they are owed to the day and let go; Nyx and Eguzkilore are kept on. The four released are welcome to keep travelling with the caravan — safer in company than alone, and the merchants are not above the trade — but without pay from that point.
3. The party take the decision badly and say so to the merchants' faces: this is a mistake, the Company of the Boar will not do the work that the party have done, and the merchants will come to see it before Waterdeep. The warning is heard and the merchants do not change their minds.
4. Nuberroca carries the point across to the Company's table and presses Khorvalis again for the duel he had tried for the night before — if the merchants mean to pay the Company's four over the party's six, then let the two companies' strongest settle on the common-room floor what the merchants have not settled in coin. Khorvalis declines the same way he had the night before: he reserves his strength for real enemies, and he will not brawl in a tavern for the amusement of merchants.
5. Lord Kaldur picks Kaelthar up on the spot as his own personal guard the moment the paladin is cut loose. The arrangement is private and settled by a short exchange at the noble's table.
6. Nuberroca, unwilling to walk the road for nothing, makes his own arrangement before the caravan sets out — a modest family travelling farther back in the line, **Darla** and her husband **Gus the humble** and their quiet friend **Anton**, take him on as guard for the price of a share of their food. He will ride by their wagon from that point.
7. Amidamaru finds Thorvin Caldorath at a corner table and closes a transaction long in the making: a flask of magical ink from the warlock's own supply, for a hefty sum of gold. **Munin** rides on his shoulder through the exchange, dry and preening after a long night under the rain. The ink is standard-priced for its kind. The scribing work that has waited on it since the first days of the caravan can now begin.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 19}}
\page

## 4 - North in the rain
_~12:00, 12 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The caravan rolls out of the inn into the rain later on 12 Uktar. The Company of the Boar ride at the merchants' flanks under their new contract; Lord Kaldur's cart rolls with them under the travel-share settled that morning, Kaelthar walking close by the noble's wagon as his hired guard. The three bound captives still walk tied at the back of the line.
2. Mialee works the gaps between the carts unseen through the day, listening in where the cultist drivers think themselves alone. She catches a line among them that will not leave her head — the cargo must reach **Naerytar** — spoken as a place rather than a person. She brings it back to the party at the next halt.
3. The name reaches Nyx, who, pressed on it, strains to place it and comes up with the barest flicker: she has read it somewhere in Greenest, perhaps a castle, nothing more. The party set it aside as something to ask after on the road to Waterdeep.
4. The ride out from the inn gives the party the full measure of why the beasts are in the state they are in.
