From his wagon-seat Lord Kaldur drives the pace from the back, calling the lash down on the horses' flanks through Jaxen Korrith's hand whenever they slow in the mud; no slackening at the climbs, no stop at the verges for grass, no breath at the water-crossings. Within hours the yoke-sores on both animals are weeping fresh under the harness, and the near horse is dragging its off-side hoof. The party watch it from down the line and keep their peace.
5. By the first halt Lord Kaldur is at the merchants' side again with the same offer and the same complaint he aired at the inn; the answer is the same. Amidamaru walks over to the noble's team while the argument carries, lays hands on each animal in turn and draws the worst of the wounds closed, then gathers grass by the handful from the verge and feeds them where their driver has not, and takes a few long minutes to comb the mud and tangles out of their manes. He will come back to them more than once on the road ahead.
6. The rest of the day carries on in the same ordinary flow of caravan travel in autumn rain: wheel-ruts filling, the midday meal on the wagons, watches cycled, the four unpaid keeping their own counsel at the back of the line. The caravan makes camp when the light begins to go, and the rain has not stopped once since the inn.

\column

## 5 - A soaked day
_~07:00, 13 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Camp strikes with everything already heavy — bedding, cloaks, boots, saddlebags soaked through the night under rain that has not slackened. Nothing dried, nothing warm, no fire to be had on the open road.
2. The ride is worse than yesterday's. Wheel-ruts have gone to channels of running water, the horses blow muddy clouds at every step, the carts slip sideways on the climbs and come near-lost at two of the water-crossings. Cloaks hang like lead on shoulders by midday; boots fill at every patch of flooded road; and the rain is still falling.
3. Amidamaru moves up the line to tend Lord Kaldur's team again at each halt — healing, feeding, combing out their coats as he can; the noble's demands on the horses have not softened, and without the artificer's hands the near beast would have gone down by now.

## 6 - Stones for bedding
_~21:00, 13 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The caravan reaches the night's camping place as the last of the light goes — the Trade Way has brought them to the northern edge of the **Fields of the Dead**, with the **Trollclaws** ahead. The rain is still falling. The ground is a pond.
2. Nyx hits on the idea that will save them a night on the saturated earth: she gathers a pile of flat stones and lays them under her sleeping mat as a dry platform. The others copy the trick for their own beds along the roadside. The camp is in small clusters on both sides of the road: the party together on one verge, the Company of the Boar and the three suspected cultist wagons on the other.
3. Kaelthar sleeps apart on the far side, close by Lord Kaldur's cart — the paladin keeps his watch where his new sworn duty lies. Nuberroca sleeps nearer the back, by the family's wagon.
4. Watches are set and the party go down one by one. The rain is still falling as the last of them finds sleep.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 19}}

\page

# Session 20 {{float:"right" 2025-08-24}}
{wide}

## 1 - Waking in the fungus
_~06:00, 14 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Dawn comes thin behind a dense fog and the party wakes to the *pop, pop, pop* of caps still bursting out of the ground. The stone layer Nyx proposed the night before has kept their immediate radius clear; beyond that, everyone else in the caravan sits pinned in whatever small patch they overnighted in, wolves audible somewhere in the fog.
2. Each cap shrieks when it is touched or set swinging and vents a small cloud of black spores; the spores force anyone caught in the cloud to feel the fungus's own agony. No merchant will set a foot into the field.
3. Amidamaru, arms crossed, mutters that this is what the **Company of the Boar** are being paid for and can handle themselves — he is essentially paying to be on this caravan, not the other way around. Eguzkilore joins Nyx by the wagon and stays close; one of them asks **Eldkin**, the dwarven caravan veteran, for a shovel — she leans out of her wagon-bed with great care not to touch anything and tosses one across.
4. Amidamaru whispers a magical message across the road to **Thorvin** of the Company — *"¿Qué vais a hacer con los hongos? ¿necesitais ayuda?"* — and waits for the reply, but none comes. After a quick study of the fungus, he sends a second message: *"El fuego podría limpiarlas más rápido"*, and turns to the rest to say the caps are venomous but passable if the skin is protected.

## 2 - Clearing the road

1. Amidamaru sends Munin out to the far edge of his telepathic tether — a hundred feet of road — and from the air the owl can see what the fog has been hiding: the field of caps runs on another considerable stretch past that point, up to a small rise where the fog itself breaks and the caps appear to run out.
2. From there Munin starts burning a cart-wide corridor outward, breathing out a cone of flame with every wing-beat; the fire kills the caps cleanly and each burst quiets a shriek, but the dying caps release their spores all the same and Munin catches hundreds of little pulses of pain through the clouds; Amidamaru feels the owl's small chest tighten through the bond.

\column

3. Nyx, meanwhile — a spare cloth from her kit tied over her nose and mouth against the spores — walks the road from the caravan side out toward the clearing's near edge, a hundred feet of careful, measured steps that thread between caps, with only a spore-puff or two set off along the way. When she reaches the cleared part of the road she turns and starts hammering back with the shovel toward the caravan, letting the spores settle before continuing.
4. **Mialee** pulls a torch from the wagon, dips it in a smear of the oil she keeps, and pushes it forward with her invisible mage-hand along the mid-line of the road, burning a narrow path ten feet wide back toward the party.
5. Amidamaru sends Munin on a second pass — finish the far end, then clean what he can out of the middle on the way back. The owl manages the far end, but on the return flight he can go no further; a few strides from where Nyx is working, he picks her out instead of flying on to Amidamaru and lands on her shoulder — an owl who had never let her touch him before, now clinging to her as a little ball, utterly wretched.
6. The caravan's own start trying it and mostly break. **Eldkin** picks up a torch, gets a few paces in, and curls into a ball on the ground, weeping — the spore-pain overwhelms her — and several others do the same. The Company of the Boar clear their whole side without a break, and their leader **Jorath** crosses the road to check on the party, kneels by Eldkin and gives her a swallow from a small canteen that steadies her.
7. **Lai**, seeing Eldkin starting to come around, picks the torch up himself, turns to Eguzkilore with a pointed look — *are you going to help or what?* — and Eguzkilore takes the torch and goes forward into the clearing to work it alongside Mialee's mage-hand for the final stretch.
8. When the two groups meet in the middle and the fog lifts, the applause runs mostly to the Company of the Boar. Amidamaru calls out over it — *he* had set the first fire. But the caravan's eyes stay where they were: the Company had worked their whole patch in plain sight, start to finish. Of the party, only Nyx had been visibly in it — shovel in hand at the far edge of the clearing — while the rest stood idle, until Eguzkilore picked up a torch at the very end.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 20}}
\page

## 3 - The dropped amulet
_~19:00, 14 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The road holds no more surprises. That evening, at supper, Amidamaru signals Mialee that it is time to act, sits close to the first of the men they believe rides for the cult, and spreads his papers, diary sheets, and the dragon-shaped amulet in plain view before getting up to fetch a fresh pencil from a wagon.
2. Mialee, at prudent distance, flicks her invisible mage-hand and blows the papers up into the air across the cultist's group as if a gust had lifted them. The suspected men gather the sheets, glance at them but do not read, and one picks up the amulet — which speaks in a small careful voice: *"Amidamaru, tú eres el elegido, nos veremos pronto"*.
3. When Amidamaru returns the men are all watching him. One states carefully that his papers had fallen and his dragon amulet had spoken a message aloud. Amidamaru fakes a startle and asks what it said; the man simply looks away — the read is guarded, not quite hostile. He and Mialee compare notes by silent message: everyone reacted the same, no one broke, the plan had done nothing.
4. Watches split as usual: Eguzkilore takes the first, the Company of the Boar take the second and third — the caravan's regard for them keeps climbing.

\column

## 4 - The Trollclaws
_~08:00, 15 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The day passes without event. The caravan runs its usual rhythm: wake-up and breakfast on the roadside, three hours march, a long midday halt for lunch, three more hours of road in the afternoon, another halt, and the final two hours push before camping for the night. At each stop Amidamaru works his way down the line to **Lord Kaldur**'s cart — the noble's horses are limping and raw again from his ill treatment — and lays hands on them one at a time; by now it has settled into the routine.
2. The next morning the landscape thins, the burial mounds run out, and the party can begin to smell the marshes ahead — where the road climbs into low hills. Eldkin comes up alongside Nyx and, in a low voice, tells her they are entering troll country; similar warnings ripple through the other groups. The Company of the Boar loosen weapons in their sheaths for easier draw. Nyx asks Eldkin how often the trolls attack — almost never, only once worth mentioning across all her trips, plus one lone creature met by accident — and tells her she will scout ahead.
3. Amidamaru sends Munin high to watch the woods — from the air the vegetation here is visibly lusher than anything the caravan has crossed since the Fields of the Dead — and tells **Nuberroca** it is game country: if he wants meat for the caravan, this is the place.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 20}}
\page

## 5 - Ordning
_~11:00, 16 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Nuberroca peels off to hunt. Munin drifts near him; both spot the same thing at once — a troll sat in a marsh almost two hundred feet off, happily eating a fish. Nuberroca rages — a mix of excitement and caution after so many days of boredom — and starts his approach, trying for stealth despite his size and his almost total inexperience at it. Munin returns to Amidamaru fluttering and pecking at his clothes, unable to explain through their bond what he has seen.
2. He closes to a rock about a hundred feet out from the troll's back. At that distance the troll catches his scent on the marsh wind, finishes its fish, and comes for him at a run, shouting the giant word for *food*.
3. Nuberroca throws a ration underhand, grows to match the troll's height and slightly overtake it, and calls out in giant: *"Creature, friend or foe?"*. The troll pulls up, takes the ration in a single bite, and replies a single word: *"Ordning?"* — something that translates roughly as *hierarchy*.
4. Nuberroca answers *"Me, king. Me, chief."* and gives it another ration. The troll pushes him experimentally; Nuberroca holds ground, calls out *"strong, strong, friends, we want to pass, no fight"* and tosses more rations. He flexes and puts a hand on the troll's shoulder — *"strong"* — the troll pushes his hand off without effort. He asks about the troll's chief; the troll only echoes *"chief"*.
5. ***[EPIC]*** By the time the rest of the party arrives at a prudent distance in cover, Amidamaru has reached out magically to ask what is going on, and Nuberroca whispers a reply back: *"todo controlado, estoy haciendo amigos"*. Nuberroca offers a final ration, says *"goodbye, friend"*; the troll takes it, gives him a puzzled look, and mutters *"Ordning"* before walking away. Nuberroca — running low on rations — returns to the caravan grinning, still giant-sized, and scares half the wagons stiff.

## 6 - Jorath's offer

1. On the march after, Jorath comes up alongside Nuberroca — the story travelled the moment he arrived doubled in size — and offers him a place in the Company of the Boar: they are always ready to take on another hand.
2. Nuberroca declines — he is already in another company, riding with this caravan only for the journey, and only for pay. Jorath drops the pitch and pulls back to the head of his group, a little embarrassed at the rebuff.

\column

## 7 - Dinner and the cult
_~20:00, 16 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Before turning in, Amidamaru pulls Nyx and Mialee aside — the plan on the table is to snatch, torture, and squeeze information out, with Nuberroca as the interrogator. Mialee approves, she is not going to oppose the plan. Amidamaru is careful about which specific cultist to target, he has grown fond of Paco.
2. Options are canvassed. Mialee proposes false troll-tracks to frame a snatch; Amidamaru worries about the audience Jorath's group now provides — that *company of the golden-fish* of theirs means more witnesses if the party is seen. The window is Eguzkilore's watch, though for some odd reason the caravan does not adore Eguzkilore the way they adore the Company of the Boar for burning a few caps.
3. Mialee turns to Nyx and asks whether she has an alternative plan. Nyx puts a floor on the risk: snatching someone roughly and interrogating them aggressively could mark a before-and-after in the party's standing with the caravan, and set off a hell of a mess right where they stood. The party's history of interrogation is bad — only the dwarf of Greenest ever got a cultist to talk, and never gently. They settle on waiting for a distraction — a troll attack, if it comes — to move under cover of chaos.
4. Nyx works the geography backwards: if the suspected cultists are riding this caravan all the way to Waterdeep, then **Naerytar** must sit near Waterdeep itself. Mialee adds that it is probably the stop just before the city, or a short detour off the road a day short of it.
5. Amidamaru walks over to **Selvek** the mute brother and, under the cover of small talk, sends him a magical whisper asking whether he knows where Naerytar is. Selvek's reply comes back as a thought: no, he does not.
6. Watches split as before: first Eguzkilore, then two and three for the Company of the Boar.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 20}}
\page

## 8 - The trolls come
_~06:00, 17 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Screaming from a guard next to Nuberroca wakes the camp: a troll has yanked him from his bedroll and, in one bite, tears off half his neck, blood spattering the couple Nuberroca had been sleeping beside.
2. Nuberroca scans the camp — the Company of the Boar is nowhere to be seen. The party stands alongside the merchants and whichever guards are still awake.
3. Nuberroca is on the troll before it can release the guard. He moves to shield the couple, lets the fury take him, and drives his polearm into it, calling out *"Ordning"* on the strike. He swings recklessly, and again on the next beat; the troll goes down under him, wounded but still moving.
4. The goliath yells for the rest of the camp to fall back — these things are fast and strong. He demands aloud where the merchants who fired them and the Company who took the contract have gone. *"Que se los coman vivos"*, he says when told they must all be on the other side of the camp.
5. Amidamaru opens the party's magical answer, summoning a blade of shadow at his hand and closing on the troll Nuberroca has just dropped, striking with a resonant blow that hums after the swing. A camp sergeant follows up with a lance-thrust while the troll is sprawled, finishing it off in a chorus of pain.
6. Behind the party, the first troll — thought dead on the ground — hauls itself back up on half-regenerated limbs, grabs the sergeant by the back, and bites deep into his neck.
7. Fresh trolls come in from the woods. On the pack's first charge, Nuberroca sweeps his polearm into a charging troll at full stride and knocks it flat before it can close.
8. ***[EPIC]*** Eguzkilore drops back a step, pours a flask of oil over the arrows in his quiver, sinks his torch upright in the ground, and starts firing arrows through the flame so each shaft carries a small tongue of fire into the oncoming pack.
9. Mialee — knowing trolls burn but not much else — throws up a phantom bonfire near the incoming trolls, betting on their brute wit to route them around it; two of the trolls take it as real and hesitate around the illusion, one shrugs it off.
10. Amidamaru weaves the sword-song into his movement and hurls a chromatic orb of fire into a wounded troll — the ball bursts in its chest and starts melting the slime off it. Munin, high overhead, breathes out another cone of fire over three of the trolls together, dropping one and burning the rest badly.
11. Kaelthar joins the fight — a pale light wrapping him, a dead run at the nearest troll on the ground — and strikes down into it hard, then again on the backswing. Between swings he lets loose his fire breath — one troll drops, the other stays up but hurt. A moment later a bite tears into him, a troll's claw slips past his guard and the pale light around him winks out.
12. Nyx picks target after target with the longbow, settling into a rhythm — one shot, step, pick again — and chews through the badly-wounded trolls one by one; early in the fight she puts one on the ground with a particularly devastating opening shot. Nuberroca lays a hand on Amidamaru's shoulder; something fierce passes between them and Amidamaru steadies. Mialee slips an arrow in from the flank where the troll cannot see her. Eguzkilore follows up with a shot straight into a downed troll to make sure it does not get up.
13. With most of the pack down, Nuberroca grows huge again mid-swing and roars *"ORDNING"* at the last standing trolls. The last troll to reach Amidamaru chips pieces off his shell with claws but breaks nothing he cannot heal. When the last of them is down, Munin lands and torches one with a final cone of fire; Amidamaru salutes — *he plays like a dragon*.

## 9 - Aftermath

1. Four of the defenders lie dead and three are wounded — a battered sergeant, another guard bleeding badly, and **Paco**, mostly untouched. Two of the fallen are guards the party had marked as cultists. The merchants and civilians are all alive — the guards took the bill.
2. The Company of the Boar is confirmed gone — not among the dead, not anywhere in the camp — and four of the caravan's horses are missing with them.
3. Amidamaru lays a healing on the wounded; Paco politely refuses, telling him he is holding up. Meanwhile Mialee moves to search the bodies of the guards they had marked as cultists, finding some spare coin but nothing useful.
4. By the fire, Amidamaru and Nuberroca argue over the credit — Amidamaru claims every troll kill; Nuberroca insists he speared each one and never saw them get back up.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 20}}

\page

# Session 21 {{float:"right" 2025-09-07}}
{wide}

## 1 - Lost horses
_~06:30, 17 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The fight is barely over and Amidamaru opens with the one thought that will drive the morning — making the *"company of the golden fish"* pay for abandoning them. Nyx, half an eye still on the camp, slips off to read the ground for horse-tracks while the rest keep arguing. Eguzkilore reasons through it: the Company of the Boar had only two directions open to them, forward or back; had they taken the road north, they would already be ahead of the caravan and bound to be caught sooner or later — so they must have fled south, back the way everyone came.
2. The count is laid out — four horses missing: two guard mounts, two draft animals, one from a cultist-suspected merchant's wagon and another from **Beyd** the beer-trader's cart. Amidamaru checks after **Noohar** — the merchant who initially hired him — only to find out he has no escort.
3. Amidamaru turns the merchant-group himself — pulls them into a loose half-circle and makes his case: with four horses short, someone will have to drop a cart; the ones who promised to protect them have run; those still here fought and bled without a plate of food between them. Nuberroca draws his lance behind Amidamaru and declares that they will be ready if things come to it.
4. The merchants chew on it and settle: nobody drops a cart. Beyd's cart ties onto another cart and pulls with one horse; the three suspected-cultist merchants will do the same with their own cart. **Eldkin** is asked how far the troll country runs — three more days to leave it, roughly ten to the main Trade Way. Nobody is willing to peel off six horses to go hunt the four stolen ones; priority is Waterdeep.
5. Nyx returns to say the tracks go north, no older than four hours; running them down would need horses they don't have and leave the caravan unguarded. The decision stands: continue north together.

\column

## 2 - New wages

1. **Lai** opens with promotions. Nyx moves from sergeant to bodyguard and Eguzkilore to sergeant; **Edhelri** takes Mialee back as bodyguard with a hefty raise, after the slight the merchant had done her. **Noohar** hires Amidamaru as bodyguard again.
2. Nuberroca stands apart. He turns to the merchant group and demands gold, flat-out, as the price for the lives he has already saved — making clear the commoner family he had been camping beside stays protected either way. The merchants mutter, pool a coin each, and come up with four pieces together; Nuberroca pockets them, says *"he hablado"*, and walks off.
3. Camp strikes — graves for the four dead soldiers, pyres for the trolls, two hours before the wagons move again.

## 3 - Shapes in the sky
_~16:00, 17 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Afternoon on the road, Amidamaru keeps **Munin** circling high and gets back a report of four shapes a long way off, wheeling in a circle like vultures but wrong for the size. He pings Nyx with a magical whisper in her ear and sends her forward with Munin flying ahead.
2. Nyx and Munin close to the brush edge; Nyx fails to recognize the shapes, but Amidamaru is sure they are not vultures. The shapes have already spotted them and are turning in. Munin tucks into a bush ready to vanish if danger closes in; Nuberroca lets the fury take him and rushes forward through the brush.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 21}}
\page

## 4 - The perytons dive

1. The lead bird comes down the sky like a peregrine falcon — talons first, beak after — and takes Nyx on the way through. Only up close does the shape resolve: a parasitic predator Nyx has heard of in a dark story, something that feeds on hearts.
2. Kaelthar slips to a tree, draws a shortbow, and lays an arrow through the first bird's feathers from a long distance off. The arrow lands but the plumage soaks most of the damage.
3. Nyx pins her hunter's mark on the diving bird, using her powers to put a black-glowing arrow through its chest, leaving it staggered. Amidamaru shrugs the shield off his arm so he can weave his sword-song, draws a **Shadow Blade** out of thin air, and chops with it and his off-hand dagger. Mialee throws up a bank of fog behind Nuberroca and ducks out of sight.
4. ***[EPIC]*** Nuberroca runs headlong into the fog as if fleeing, pivots inside it, and vanishes into a jaunt that sets him well up in the air, polearm first, to spear the lead bird. He misses, drops the lance in the air and throws everything into a grapple instead, wrestling the bird mid-air by the wings as it falls. The two of them hit the bushes together — the bird takes the worst of it, Nuberroca dulls the pain through his rage. He drags it still-grappled into the fog where it can only fight blind.
5. A second peryton dives in on Nyx: beak and claws tearing at her. Amidamaru casts a spell to distract the creature, but the hit lands anyway and Nyx goes down unconscious. Her last shout to the field is that these things *need hearts*; nobody knows what for. A beat later Amidamaru throws himself bodily onto her, shell up, as cover.
6. Mialee ducks and darts to Nuberroca's shoulder in the fog, guided by sound and his own call-out. She appears to see where he can only hear and cuts the grappled bird twice with her twin scimitars. The bird is a mess of plumage but still breathing. Eguzkilore stays at range, puts two arrows into the peryton on Kaelthar's side of the field, then catches his breath and shifts into position behind the fog before the birds can retaliate.
7. Amidamaru, almost on instinct, pops one of his share of Nyx's berries between her lips — she comes around dizzy and weak. On the next swing he puts the Shadow Blade through the nearest bird with a deafening boom of thunder.

\column

## 5 - A rogue griffon

1. Above the fight, over the camp's own shouting, a far larger pair of wings rolls in from the south — a gigantic griffon, muzzled and with mittens on its claws, coming down on the lead peryton in a stooping dive. The griffon can hook the bird but the muzzle keeps it from doing damage; a flurry of plumes rises off the pair.
2. ***[EPIC]*** As the griffon pulls up, a figure comes off its back — Sinsong, choosing the moment to let go — and falls through the air gracefully. A dozen feet from the ground angelic wings sprout from his shoulders and a ring of pale celestial figures spreads out around him, attacking anything within a few paces. He glides low so the ring sweeps the three birds together.
3. The celestial guardians wound two of the perytons; the three of them turn and bolt straight up, avoiding the magical creatures. Nuberroca pulls a rope from his pack and ties his grappled bird at beak and wings — down and out of the fight — then tries to grow huge again, but he is too exhausted to manage it.
4. Two of the fleeing birds catch Nyx by the shoulders and lift her into the air, bound for the sky; she twists and shakes but fails to escape.
5. Kaelthar's draconic wings beat open and he magically teleports up to reach the two carrying birds in the air — he lets fly a cone of dragon-fire that takes both perytons and the unconscious Nyx in the same arc; both birds are severely hurt but hold on.
6. ***[EPIC]*** Amidamaru pulls a diamond chip from his belt and arcs a small sphere of fire upward: it bursts on the first fleeing bird and consumes it on the spot, then jumps onto the second turning it into a ball of burning feathers. Nyx falls limp from the dying bird's grasp as the sphere bursts across her face.
7. ***[EPIC]*** Eguzkilore has one chance and throws everything into the catch — he vaults into the trajectory, getting his arms under Nyx just before she hits the ground.
8. Sinsong quickly summons a bond of magical aid on the three of them and Nyx is on her feet with an open chest but her heart untouched, the open wound partly closing under a soft red glow.
9. Nuberroca walks over to the bird he had tied, draws his lance, and puts it through the heart; then coils the rope, picks up the lance, and he is done.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 21}}
\page

## 6 - An unwanted pendant

1. The griffon is loose now — it works the muzzle off its beak and then tears the mittens off its claws, and once free it is nervous and will not stay still. Sinsong moves to introduce it as **Targutai**, a gift from a merchant he met on his detour; the griffon can be handled if nobody startles it.
2. With the griffon loose and nervous and Nuberroca walking straight toward it — fresh from boasting that lethal damage is necessary — Sinsong fears for the beast and speaks a word of command on him: *flee*. The barbarian dashes a long way north before his own protest can land.
3. Nuberroca turns back moments later; on the way in he sees Sinsong gesturing a second cast and holds himself ready. The cleric whispers a sleep-spell to pin him before he can retaliate; Nuberroca teleports clear of its area before the words land.
4. From that safe distance Nuberroca throws the stone Sinsong had once given him back at the dwarf's face and walks away.
5. Sinsong fills the gap of the lost days over the quiet that follows: he drifted away from the party in Baldur's Gate and didn't find them afterwards. He was lost in the city for three days, then bartered a griffon from a merchant he befriended in order to reach them again. Along the way he passed a lone cart with an unusual bird on the merchant's shoulder, a group of trolls, and some burial-mound pyres. Amidamaru reads him as hiding something but no more than Sinsong always does.

## 7 - A wolf called Fenrir
_~21:00, 17 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The caravan beds down early. Sinsong fits the mittens and muzzle back onto Targutai, with Nuberroca helping gently when asked; they tie the griffon at a tree well away from the camp horses. Nyx, badly cut across the chest, rests for the whole time.
2. Kaelthar summons his bonded mount out of nothing in the shape of a giant wolf — **Fenrir**, wits no sharper than a regular wolf's, but fine for pulling a cart or startling anyone who comes close. Amidamaru takes first watch with Eguzkilore; Nuberroca and Mialee take second.
3. Amidamaru explains the pair of new animals to the caravan when the questions come — the griffon will be kept far from the horses and Fenrir is indeed a very docile wolf.

\column

## 8 - Moving north
_~08:00, 18 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. The caravan rolls out at dawn. Sinsong holds the griffon under control with effort and a word of command ready if the bond slips, feeding him one of Nyx's berries to steady him before departing.
2. Along the way, Nyx finds prints that are at least a couple days old. Sinsong mounts the griffon and strikes north to scout for the stolen horses while the caravan rolls on. The road through the end of troll country is light on sign.
3. Night falls with Sinsong still in the field. He casts a *sending* spell over to Nyx — tracks of two or three horses heading north, it is dark and there is no fire. The next day he searches on and finds nothing more, then turns back; it takes him until the following night to reach the caravan.
4. At the camp reunion, Sinsong admits that the *command* and the *sleep* spells had been to keep Nuberroca off a griffon he was sure the goliath meant to kill; when he presses on Nuberroca's loyalty, Nuberroca tells him he has no more talismans worshipping any false god. Sinsong tries to return the stone as if dropped by accident; Nuberroca refuses to even look at it.
5. The remaining days run quiet — Targutai pulling with the carts, Fenrir alongside, with the caravan edging out of troll country.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 21}}
\page

## 9 - Broken crates
_~16:30, 23 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Mid-column, Targutai turns on the draft horse harnessed next to him and takes a bite out of its neck; the horse screams, tears the harness, and bolts. The griffon snaps the lines on his side and goes after it at low altitude.
2. Sinsong, with the chaos exploding around him, throws a sleep-spell at Targutai to stop the rampage, but the griffon twists free of the weave even after Amidamaru's attempt to distract the beast. Sinsong uses his magic on Targutai again — a command of *come to me* — and the griffon only just gives in, halting in mid-chase. Nyx and Eguzkilore break off to run down the fleeing horse.
3. ***[EPIC]*** Nuberroca jaunts into the air above the suspected-cultist cart that Targutai had torn loose from, and lets himself fall into its cargo bed. Amidamaru starts coaxing the split harness back together with a word of mending as the shouting goes up around them. Before the guards wrestle Nuberroca off the wagon, he cracks one of the crates with a slam of his shoulder; the wood splits enough to show a glint of something metallic within. He plays the whole thing off with a thin apology for how clumsy he is, but no-one is convinced.
4. The suspected merchants' guards draw crossbows on him in seconds; Nuberroca stands up and tells them to fire if they have the stones. **Paco** — the one Amidamaru has always said is likeable — is the first to lower his crossbow and ask Nuberroca to keep away from their carts, promising no quarrel if the barbarian does the same. Amidamaru and Mialee together, playing to the merchants' nerves, talk the standoff down.
5. Nyx and Eguzkilore come back with the wounded horse and a bloody scrap of cloth cut from the brush where they caught it; by the time they arrive the cart is together again under Amidamaru's hands. The tortle studies the cloth and recognises it as **Khorvalis**'s; Nyx tells them that she also saw dried blood and a torn horse-hoof in the brush, and thinks the trolls got at least one of the stragglers.
6. Sinsong flies Nyx up on the griffon to search west of the road for signs of the stragglers. Meanwhile, Amidamaru uses his magical whisper to ask Nuberroca, and the barbarian replies along the same magical thread — jewels and coin in the crates. Overhead, Nyx finds Khorvalis's severed hand and his axe at a wider patch of churned ground; Sinsong *sends* the find back to Amidamaru — the pattern reads *trolls*.
7. Flying back towards the caravan, Sinsong floats breaking the carts open now. Nyx cuts him off: better to let the carts reach their stop so the party can find **Naerytar** at the end of the trail.

## 10 - The painted stone
_~12:00, 27 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. After four quiet days, during which Sinsong keeps Targutai in hand with constant supervision and the command of return held in reserve, they are finally approaching the main Trade Way road.
2. When they glimpse the intersection, they notice a large piece of stone sitting in the middle of it, painted with something they cannot make out — and they are sure it is moving.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 21}}

\page

# Session 22 {{float:"right" 2025-09-21}}
{wide}

## 1 - A head in the road
_~12:00, 27 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. Nyx rides ahead of the carts to settle what the painted thing at the crossroads actually is, and at close range it resolves not as a stone at all but as a man's head — a human buried up to the neck in the middle of the roadway, the word *"Oathbreaker"* scrawled across his forehead in red paint, unconscious but still breathing.
2. The caravan to a man wants to leave him where he lies — the man surely deserved whatever got him there — but the party overrules them, and Nuberroca sets to the digging himself, slow work with his hands and the butt of his lance. Amidamaru waits at the side with water and healing, and when the hole is deep enough and the first touch lands the man comes round with mud still thick in his beard.
3. As the dirt comes off his shoulders a tattoo surfaces on bare skin — a harp within a crescent moon, which Nyx reads at a glance and keeps to herself for now. Clothes come out of one of the packs; the man is dressed and settled in the back of a trailing wagon.
4. He gives his name as **Carlon Amoffel** and tells, first for the merchants' benefit, a broken-engagement story — the oath he broke was a promise to marry a woman whose father and brothers all turned out to be bandits, and who had expected him, once the marriage was sealed, to come into the trade with them. When he refused them — the oath and the trade both — the brothers tracked him down on the road and planted him here in answer. The merchants mostly nod and look relieved.
5. Off to one side of the halt, away from the circle around Amoffel, Sinsong is at **Targutai**'s flank. The griffon is hitched into one of the carts in place of a missing horse, and the arrangement has been getting worse by the day — Targutai would sooner eat the horse next to him than pull alongside it, and the only thing keeping him in the traces is Sinsong's steady handling, a hand on the harness and every trick he has for a difficult beast. When that falls short he lays a word of command on him — *hold* — and the halt passes without blood on the harness, barely.
6. At the next rest stop Nyx draws Amoffel aside with Mialee at her shoulder, and they identify as Harpers themselves — at which the cover falls away. He had been riding infiltrated with a caravan a few days ahead of this one — a merchant convoy with cultists hidden among the merchants — and when the cultists made him, they fed the merchants the story that he was passing intel to bandits; the merchants, unwilling to kill him outright, left him buried in the road to let providence decide his fate.
7. The hunt is the same one — the stolen loot moving north — and the name **Leosin** is already in his mouth before anyone offers it. He names **Vauser** too, a half-orc Harper contact in Waterdeep: muscular, red-haired, a scar running across his face, reachable at *The Broken Jar* when the party gets that far.
8. Amoffel takes a place in one of the back wagons. The party briefs him of their situation, the three carts at the front of the caravan are the cultists, and the loot has been identified. Then they ask for his help against them, he agrees, and from here he rides with the caravan as a third Harper on the trail.
9. The following days go by without any remarkable event as the caravan progresses through the Trade Way.

## 2 - Dragonspear Castle
_16:00, 30 Uktar 1489 DR_

1. By the afternoon of the last day of Uktar the road turns west around a long shallow rise, and the ruins come into view — a hulk of blackened stone standing in a sea of grass, towers broken down, walls staved in, a low mist clinging to the ground around the foundations even under the midday sun.
2. **Eldkin** rides up out of the caravan's middle to Nyx's side, as if the sight of the stones has pulled her forward out of the line, and names the place quietly, for Nyx alone to hear — **Dragonspear Castle**. A dwarven fortress once, she says, raised by one of her own.
3. She tells Nyx what she will. It had been built by a dwarf called **Daeros Dragonspear**, long before her grandfathers were born, with an artefact weapon set at its heart that gave the castle its name. For a time it stood as a hold of her people on the High Moor — her kin in the halls and dwarven runes cut deep into the stone above the gate.
4. Then it fell — to fiends, she says. They came up from somewhere under the stones. Her kin bled for the hold of the place twice over, and in the end the castle was ruined and nothing the dwarves had set in it was ever recovered.
5. Today it sits as the party sees it — mist on the ground that does not clear, no smoke from any hearth, no voice of any watch — and the merchants steer the carts a touch wider as they pass. Eldkin does not look back as the road carries on, and Nyx does not press her for more.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 22}}
\page

## 3 - The golden stag

1. Later that day the road rises past a stretch of open ground, and late sunlight falls on the hillside where a herd of deer grazes in the grass. Among them stands a stag whose coat catches the light like beaten gold, antlers like platinum. The caravan halts at the sight.
2. Half the guards reach straight for their bows — that hide would pay a season, meat for a week, a trophy worth a year of road — the other half hold back, hands raised: a creature coloured like that is not game, it is omen, and bringing it down will bring worse down behind it.
3. As the archers start running toward the hill, Nyx throws a cloud of fog across the slope, dense and low; the hunters lose line of sight and the stag within it, and in the thick of the mist they scatter in different directions after a glimpse that is no longer there.
4. Behind the mist the party divides. Nyx has caught the stag's trail at the far side of the fog and goes after it; Mialee and Nuberroca go with her. Sinsong, Eguzkilore, and Amidamaru stay with the carts to settle the hunters when they come back empty-handed.
5. The chase runs harder than any deer should, and the three of them follow at a long pace through the brush. Somewhere in the ground where the trail turns in on itself Nuberroca loses the track and falls behind; Nyx and Mialee come on alone at last to a moss-grown, ivy-draped ruin in a wooded hollow — a ring of fallen stones, roofless, the trees crowded close against the walls.
6. The stag is already standing there when they arrive, light still on his coat though the sun has fallen below the trees. He turns at the stones and speaks — Sylvan, carrying across the clearing. Nyx takes it in as a Fey-speaker. Mialee hears it through a spell for the understanding of tongues.
7. Behind the shape of the stag both of them feel the hand of **Silvanus** — god of wild places, of what grows and keeps watch. Neither of them speaks the name aloud. Both of them lower their bows.
8. ***[EPIC]*** The stag confirms they are on the right track, and leaves the prophecy on them: ***"Follow the river of gold until you reach the castle in the sky. Your path will be filled with hardship and blood. Not all will survive..."***
9. When the voice goes silent a longbow is lying at their feet — the **Forest Longbow**, a deer head carved into the grip, its antlers forming the bow itself. The stag fades into the trees between one breath and the next. Nyx, reverent, takes up the bow.
10. In the silence of the clearing the two of them stand a long moment with the prophecy still in the air between them. Then the last of the daylight goes out of the trees and they turn back the way they came, to find Nuberroca on the back-trail and the caravan still halted on the road.

\column

## 4 - Diverse rites
_~11:00, Feast of the Moon 1489 DR_

1. The Feast of the Moon falls between the last day of Uktar and the first of Nightal — a day set apart from the months, and from the road. By mid-morning the carts have drawn up to a roadside tavern still within sight of Dragonspear Castle to the east, and halt there for the full day.
2. The tavern halt is the worst yet for Targutai. The stable fills with the caravan's horses one after another, and the griffon — lashed off to a corner of the yard well out of range of them — strains and screams every time one is led past. Sinsong is at his side through the long morning, hand on his neck and voice low; when the handling will not hold he lays a slumber spell on him and the beast goes down for a span, and wakes angrier each time.
3. The hours otherwise pass easily enough: horses rested, harness mended, bread and salt-pork taken under the awning. By late afternoon the sun hangs low to the west and the first bite of winter has come in on the air; as dusk drops the lamps come alight in the common room, and the caravan folk begin to set themselves for the three rites of the day.
4. Eldkin keeps her own rite out in the tavern yard, apart from the warm room, at a small fire of her own under the open sky — a dwarven Commemoration of the Fallen. She has set a flat stone before her, and as each name goes into the fire she tips a measure of ale onto it for the dead to drink.
5. Nyx comes out and sits with her, and the names Eldkin speaks under her breath are no longer only her private grief. Daeros Dragonspear she names first, raiser of the castle on the High Moor. Then the names of her kin who held the hold in the two sieges and did not come out of it — a dozen names and more, one after another, until the ale is gone and the small fire between them has run down to coals.
6. Inside the tavern the main fire belongs to Shiallia: a feast built at the hearth, warm cider in hands, bread broken and passed from table to table, caravan voices and the tavern's own folk rising together in old road-songs across the room.
7. In one corner a short Lliira observance has gone up as well — a weapon laid flat on the ground under a scatter of flowers, chanted over quietly.
8. The rest of the party take their places at the long table and the hearth, warming hands and passing the cider, but stay outside the Ritual of Remembrance; the stories told that night belong to other travellers.
9. Long after the second cask has been broached the common room thins and the fire settles, and the party find their pallets along the wall. Out in the yard Eldkin stays with her coals until they are properly dark; Nyx stays with her, and does not leave her alone.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 22}}
\page

## 5 - Heroes of Mansken
_~13:00, 1 Nightal 1489 DR_

1. The first day of Nightal opens cold and grey, and the caravan is slow to leave — the Feast night has run its wake, and the merchants have agreed to take a midday meal at the tavern before the road. The common room fills with caravaners and tavern-locals over bowls of stew and warm bread, and Amidamaru stands up at the long table and asks for a word.
2. He tells the locals the long road behind the caravan: the fungus-field with its black spores that pinned the carts until the way was cut open; the lone troll Nuberroca talked down in the marshes of the Trollclaws with a handful of rations; the night the other trolls came at the camp out of the dark and the pyres on both sides the next morning — and here his voice turns cold with hate at the **Company of the Boar**, paid in coin to guard that very camp and gone from it before the first blade fell, taking four horses and the caravan's trust with them. He closes on yesterday — the golden stag at the ruined stones and the Sylvan voice on the wind — and names each of the party in their turn by what they did.
3. When the story runs to its edge, Amidamaru names the party itself for the first time — ***"The Heroes of Mansken"*** — and the name lands outside their own mouths at last. The caravan folk pound the table with their cups; the tavern-locals follow because the caravan has; someone starts a song before the bowls are cleared.
4. Before the carts roll out into the afternoon, Amidamaru pulls a square of paper from his pack and spreads it on the table in front of him. The locals lean in. In a careful hand he writes down the shape of what he has just told — the long road, the trolls fought, the forest-god met at the end — and one by one each of the party puts their mark under the banner of *"The Heroes of Mansken"*. He pins the paper to the beam above the hearth, so that the next traveller to stop here will have a name to put to the tale.

\column

## 6 - Sinsong flies north
_~16:00, 1 Nightal 1489 DR_

1. By mid-afternoon the carts have rolled out from the tavern and the caravan has settled back into the road, the mood still high after the festival.
2. It does not last. Three hours out of the tavern Targutai breaks the line on the carthorse beside him again — this time to the throat, a tear that would have killed the beast outright but for Amidamaru's hand the moment after. The merchants will not have it any longer; cries go up along the carts to cut the griffon loose and leave him in the ditch for the crows.
3. Sinsong stops it before it goes further. He steps between the merchants and the harness and says what he has been thinking all day: Targutai cannot be held in traces another hour, and he will not leave him to be killed on the road. He will take the griffon up and go north instead — the Company of the Boar is days ahead of them on the Trade Way, but a griffon can cut that distance fast.
4. He frees Targutai from the harness and talks him down one last time; the griffon settles the moment he is out of the line of horses, farther from his preferred prey. Sinsong packs for several days on the wing — rations enough to last him until he can hunt his own, a bedroll, water-skins and his stones, every last thing he carries on the road — and straps the saddle to the griffon's back. Nyx offers to come. He refuses her — this is his to do, and the caravan still has need of a scout.
5. He swings up into the saddle and Targutai goes. One long run of the claws on the wet verge, one great beat of the wings, and the griffon is rising over the treeline with Sinsong black against the grey sky. The party watch him climb north along the line of the Trade Way until he is a shape no bigger than a bird. The road carries on, and the rest of them with it, under the promise to meet again soon.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 22}}
\page

## 7 - No room at the inn
_~13:00, 11 Nightal 1489 DR_

1. The first tenday of Nightal ran cold and wet through deepening winter — rain on and off since the second day, no stretch of dry road for more than a morning at a time, the merchants' cloaks never quite drying between halts. Nothing on the road worth the telling.
2. By midday of the eleventh the caravan draws up to a large roadside inn standing alone on the grey land, smoke from its chimney the only thing moving in the rain. Two women are already outside under the awning, drenched and out of options — twin sisters **Arietta** and **Zelina Innevar**, bound for Waterdeep themselves and turned away from the door.
3. Eguzkilore draws alongside them with a general greeting, and the sisters pick him up with a cordial flirtation; Nyx joins the circle and Amidamaru stands with them under the awning. Names are exchanged, and the shared destination of Waterdeep.
4. Nuberroca stops at the awning long enough for the sisters to catch his eye before Mialee pulls him through the door. The common room is empty but for four men at the one lit table — three swordsmen with blades at their hips and the build of men who use them, and at their head a noble in rich clothes and an elegant red cap, plainly the one carrying the voice. On the table between them sits a strange **lamp** that emits a soft blue light.
5. **Barlon** the innkeeper bows to the newcomers, polite and apologetic: the noble has paid for the *whole* inn — the common room and every room upstairs. When Nuberroca asks about the stables, one of the guards at the table calls over, loud enough to be heard across the room: his master's horses are high-bred, and will not rest anywhere near the filthy animals from the caravan. They have had to rent out the whole stable too, he says, to keep the two apart.
6. The four at the table laugh at the goliath and drop the next jibe at his expense, then settle back to their hands of cards around the blue lamp.
7. Nuberroca stands his ground with Mialee at his side; Barlon moves between them pleading, while the four at the table watch pointedly bored and ready.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 22}}
