# Session 23 {{float:"right" 2025-09-28}}
{wide}

## 1 - At Barlon's door
_~13:30, 11 Nightal 1489 DR_

1. The party stands at a tavern under heavy rain; two beautiful women huddle under the eaves while the common room is reserved for a private party. Nuberroca and Mialee are inside talking with **Barlon**, the innkeeper, while a noble and his three guards sit at a table near the hearth, a curious lamp on the tabletop giving off a soft blue glow.
2. Barlon apologises: the rooms, the common room, and the stables are all paid for, nothing to offer, he can only ask them to leave. The noble and his retinue snicker at the caravan's misfortune.
3. Nuberroca refuses the eviction with a flop onto the nearest bench, lying comfortably on it and closing his eyes. He warns Barlon the noble's party has yet to be called to heel for the insults: *"bastante que no les parto el cuello a esos cuatro monos que tienes ahí insultándonos"*.
4. One of the bodyguards approaches Barlon and Nuberroca to order the innkeeper to vacate the room. Mialee uses the covering distraction. An unseen hand slips into the standing bodyguard's pouch and lifts it clean. Nobody at the table marks the loss.
5. Nyx, Amidamaru, and Eguzkilore approach the two women under the eaves, the halfling breaks the ice and the women introduce themselves — **Arietta Innevar** silent on the left, **Zelina Innevar** doing the talking.
6. The sisters are bound for Waterdeep, turned away like the party and left under the eaves with no better place to go. Eguzkilore keeps the conversation shallow — a lie when a lie is safer, the truth otherwise — and pulls them a few steps from the door.
7. The guard commands Barlon to clear the room again; the tavernkeep rounds back on Nuberroca to beg him out, pushing weakly against a mountain that will not move. The guard enforces the eviction kicking the bench under Nuberroca: *"animal, fuera"*.
8. Amidamaru picks the argument up from the door — a bodyguard's job is not to call other races animals, that is racism and bad raising. The guard rebuffs him — proper respect goes to people who follow the rules, not to animals.

\column

## 2 - Three windows

1. With the eviction complete, Nuberroca swallows the kick and turns to the door. Amidamaru is clear — Barlon is not the one at fault, and there are other ways to settle a thing like this.
2. Nyx pries the sisters for what they know: two or three hours out in the rain since it started, and a half-caught title for their rude host — a Lord, with a northern-sounding name they could not quite pin.
3. Not knowing they are already paid for, Nyx scouts the stables: plenty of room, and five horses standing — one more than the noble's party.
4. Amidamaru casts *Invisibility* on Munin and sends him up to the windows to find where the noble sleeps and where his belongings are. Three rooms in use upstairs — two lit, one dark — each with lodgers' belongings. Only the easternmost has someone inside: a hooded figure in rich robes reading on the bed. The other two sit empty.
5. Mialee opens the climb with a grappling hook off her thieves' kit — the wall is bare and difficult but she manages. She goes in through the dark window first. The take is quiet work: a well-worn armour and a handful of silver she *almost* leaves.
6. She tries to cross to the second window and falls — catches the rope on the way down, reaches the ground without serious injury, and goes unspotted. She climbs back up for the other empty lit room and finds a similar haul: ten pieces of gold worth taking, armour she leaves this time.
7. Outside, Nuberroca works a window from below like a child baiting a cat — calling out hello to the little mice in their burrow — and nobody indoors stays composed about it.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 23}}
\page

## 3 - The duel breaks
_~14:30, 11 Nightal 1489 DR_

1. One of the guards comes out, draws his longsword, and warns Nuberroca off — *"mide tus palabras, escoria"*. Nuberroca lets the fury take him, settles his polearm on the ground, and tells the guard to kneel and ask forgiveness or come get him.
2. ***[EPIC]*** Nuberroca comes down on the guard recklessly and lands the first strike with devastating force, toppling the man flat on the ground. His second strike comes down on the guard, he turns the blade to avoid a killing blow and the man goes down unconscious instead. He grins over at Nyx — *"non-lethal damage, did you see?"*
3. He vanishes into a cloud and reappears behind the standing guard, waving — *"cucú"*. **Lord Zelan**, the noble, immediately says *"oh, so we can use magic... perfect!"* and the two remaining guards close around the goliath.
4. While holding the blue lamp, the noble speaks a line the party hears but cannot understand. Nuberroca feels an odd pull against the back of his mind but manages to hold it off.
5. One of the mage-guards cuts under his polearm with his sword, flashes behind him, and lays two shortsword hits across his back. Nyx cannot land either of her thrusts with the sword — the noble's clothing seems to hide armour underneath. She marks him as her quarry and holds her line. Eguzkilore prepares his bow but holds the arrow, hesitant to shoot, while asking the ladies to move further apart.
6. ***[EPIC]*** The noble utters words without moving his mouth, and Nuberroca starts shrieking. The barbarian sees the ground under him open — the people of his tribe rise out of it as undead, skeletons and zombies with the faces of his companions and friends tearing at his skin and flesh. He drops his polearm, screams names the party does not recognise, and tries to tear things off himself that are not there.
7. Eguzkilore, hesitant to act, keeps his arrow notched but doesn't let it fly. Nuberroca teleports in a last attempt to escape the hands of his phantasmal friends, but the hands that are not there finish what they started and he drops face-down and bleeding.
8. Nyx is at his side a step later. She kneels, pops one of her berries between his lips, and he comes around just enough to breathe, a blue glow starts closing the worst of his wounds. The noble calls his guards off — point made, no more to prove — picks up his lamp, and the four of them carry their fallen man back inside.

\column

## 4 - After the fight

1. Nyx opens the tavern door and asks Barlon for a few minutes of his time. The tavernkeep comes out with food and clean cloths for Nuberroca's cuts, the apology still on his face.
2. Amidamaru asks him about the *Company of the Boar* and describes the three riders: the leader in fine clothes with a large tusk pendant hung at his chest; the warlock in hood and robes; and the paladin in religious trappings. Barlon has not heard the name, but when Amidamaru describes the warlock the tavernkeep hedges — a man of that look might match one of the current guests, though he could not say for certain.
3. Nuberroca looks Barlon in the eye and tells him flat: *"la fuerza no basta contra las sombras"*. Barlon does not know what to make of it. The barbarian explains that he is afraid of necrotic magic — leaves what he actually saw out of it — and Barlon simply nods.
4. Watching the goliath worried for his friends, Nyx leans close: it was wizard tricks, nothing real about his village. Nuberroca cannot take the reassurance clean — the vision had the faces of his tribe as undead, and the doubt that it was a true seeing stays in him. Nyx asks whether he said anything about his tribe aloud in the fight; he answers no.
5. Nuberroca asks whether there is a way to verify. Nyx recalls Sinsong once reached a man in Waterdeep with a magical message; perhaps he can carry one to the tribe tonight.
6. Mialee joins them from the back of the tavern, finds Nuberroca half-wrecked on the cart, and reports what she had been doing while they were being broken — rooms searched, nothing more interesting than armour and small coin. Amidamaru floats the quieter half of the report: one of the upper guests, a hooded figure reading in his room, may be the Company of the Boar mage from the description.
7. Nuberroca thanks Nyx in front of everyone — the only one who stepped in to help was her — then catches Amidamaru's eye: he had tumbled one without killing, was that enough? Amidamaru brushes his belt with his artificer magic, lifting a soft light out of it: *"te lo has ganado"*. Nuberroca walks it over to Barlon to show it off like a child.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 23}}
\page

## 5 - Supper by the carts
_~17:30, 11 Nightal 1489 DR_

1. The caravan spreads out beside the road for the night: the next tavern is two days further on and was under construction last time they passed, so the merchants will rest here through tomorrow before pushing on. The two sisters move their belongings into the stable — Barlon's apology never became a bed — though they stay out with the merchants through the afternoon.
2. Nuberroca narrates the duel for the party: he had opened with honour, four closed on him together, and the noble invented he had used magic. He wants Eguzkilore to answer for keeping his bow silent — he had shouted an order to shoot the one casting on him. Eguzkilore says the shout was confusing and he was hesitating, so he kept the arrow for next time.
3. The party puts the pieces together on the enemy. The noble himself carried the lamp and spoke the mind-breaking words; the hooded reader upstairs matches the Boar's warlock from the description. Eguzkilore and Nyx would wait for the morning; Amidamaru wants the lamp tonight, with the noble asleep, using Mialee's unseen hand. The night plan wins.
4. Through the afternoon the two sisters move along the camp, trading life-stories with anyone who will share. Nyx opens up about her nomad tribe; Eguzkilore stays as cautious as he had been at the door. Mialee keeps a careful distance — a greeting, no more — and tells the party afterwards that the two women do not sit right with her.
5. Amidamaru asks the sisters about the Boar's three riders, Zelina confirms they came through fast on the main road a while back. He offers the party as paid protection the rest of the way to Waterdeep for a few coins; the sisters say they are poor as rats, all their money spent on reaching this far, and ask instead if the party could *lend* them something. Nuberroca cuts in to help — the sisters could sell their services. Nyx elbows him; Nuberroca, oblivious, points at his loincloth and clarifies that he means mending work for his torn clothes.
6. By sundown the party splits. Nuberroca beds down with the caravan family he had been camped beside; the other four stay up to prepare the night move. Before sleep Nuberroca has Sinsong send a magical message to his village to ask whether they are safe.

\column

## 6 - A little blue man
_~01:30, 12 Nightal 1489 DR_

1. Mialee releases Echo for the climb; Nyx weaves a spell over the four of them, a stealth-emanation that leaves no mark on the ground. Amidamaru moves in with Mialee, Nyx and Eguzkilore behind him; Nuberroca stays with the caravan to guard the family he had been camping beside.
2. The tortle works a *Sleep* spell through the window at Lord Zelan; the noble shrugs it off and is up out of his bed to look around, finds nothing, and settles again on his own in time.
3. ***[EPIC]*** Mialee's unseen hand works the window latch open from outside with her thieves' tools — not a sound carries. The hand reaches in across the room and lifts the blue lamp off the table; she brings it down and gives it over to Amidamaru.
4. The lamp is a small lantern of four glass panes, and inside it an old wizened fellow in a sage's robe, blue-skinned, no larger than a bat and wide-eyed as if the world had just arrived. Amidamaru asks his name in Common, then has Nyx ask it in Sylvan; the mouth opens as if screaming but no sound comes. Mialee catches a whisper near her ear, barely at the edge of a word she could pick out.
5. Mialee says the whole thing is giving her a bad feeling. Amidamaru hesitates — whatever this is, letting it loose beats leaving it in the hands of the man who broke Nuberroca. Mialee sends Echo across to the other rooms and a moment later whispers to Amidamaru: the robed figure from the other room has got up.
6. ***[EPIC]*** Amidamaru opens the lantern door, sets it on the ground and steps back — *"eres libre, muchachito"*. The little blue sage lifts both hands and sends a small point of blue light up along the air; it hangs above them for a half-second and bursts into a sphere of cold. Four of them are caught in it; Nyx and Mialee dodge the worst, Amidamaru and Eguzkilore are engulfed. The sage climbs out of his lamp and goes back in through the second-storey window as the guards begin to stir.
7. They melt back under Nyx's cover. A heartbeat later the guards sweep lanterns in cones of light across the ground; a voice sounds in the night — *"who goes there?"* — but the stealth spell holds and the beams find nothing. Mialee wipes the frost off with her magical trick.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 23}}
\page

8. Back in the safety of the shadows, Nyx turns on Amidamaru: why the lamp? — he had not wanted the man who broke Nuberroca to keep whatever this was. The puzzle sits between them: imprisoned in the lamp, and then back in it by his own will. Mialee presses — was the lamp his cell or his house? Did it even have a bolt? Perhaps they had freed a man who had not asked to be freed. Amidamaru takes the lesson; Nyx grants she would have done the same. Eguzkilore wonders what the little sage was doing all that time with his mouth open — perhaps something inside the lamp as well.

\column

## 7 - Village's reply
_~09:00, 12 Nightal 1489 DR_

1. Barlon tells Mialee at breakfast what she already feared — Lord Zelan's party has paid for two more days, two hundred gold up front. The caravan plans to rest the horses another day and leave at midday. The sisters have asked to travel with them and the merchants have made room.
2. Mialee had tried one further thing at breakfast — a sent whisper to the stranger upstairs, with her comprehension ready for whatever he answered, asking whether he was there against his will. No reply came back, and she took the silence as his answer: not kidnapped, not for them to free.
3. Nuberroca steps away from the group to hear the reply Sinsong had received during the night. The village holds — losses as usual, no breaking — but the undead attacks have grown heavier and more frequent, and lately bald men with facial tattoos in red cloaks walk among the undead at the attacks.
4. He pulls the party in and tells them the whole of it, then remembers that the elegant cap on the noble's head is red. Nuberroca puts the pieces together out loud — bald, red-clothed, cruel with the mind — and asks the party whether this is their fight.
5. Nyx recalls a name she once heard — red mages out of a place called Thay, dangerous and not kind people — but the information is not enough to make the noble one of them. Amidamaru, Nyx and Mialee all push the same way against a second fight — not everyone bald is cruel, not everything red is bad, and the caravan on the road is a larger obligation than the lesson.
6. Nuberroca holds his anger, says he will not drag the party into another fight, not after yesterday. He wants to grow strong enough to answer this kind of magic the next time it comes for him or his.
7. Mialee tells the goliath the lamp story — he had slept through the heist. He asks whether the little sage is held captive; Nyx is not sure, the lamp may be his cell or his house, and he had chosen to go back in. The barbarian floats taking the lamp if its tenant wants to stay. Mialee cuts the thought: the sage had not asked to be freed.
8. Amidamaru lays out the darker read — the sage was the source of the cold he threw at them, and perhaps also of the magic that broke Nuberroca at the duel; best to keep him as far away as possible. Nuberroca lands on the inference: so you are saying the lamp-man was the one who attacked me?
9. The party eats, settles, and spends the rest of the day resting and chatting with the caravan and the two sisters. The next day, at noon, they roll out.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 23}}

\page

# Session 24 {{float:"right" 2025-10-05}}
{wide}

## 1 - Rolling out
_~12:00, 13 Nightal 1489 DR_

1. The caravan breaks camp at noon after two days lying over in sight of **Barlon**'s tavern — tents struck wet, cold provisions stowed back on the carts, the beasts walked into their harnesses under a low Nightal cloud that has been spitting rain off and on since dawn. **Lord Zelan** crosses the yard for his lunch at the common room with his guards in a loose knot around him; they pass the convoy still jesting at its expense, with the tiny blue sage back inside his lamp. The hooded figure glimpsed upstairs has never come down in all this time — not for meat, not for water, not for a breath of fresh air.
2. The sisters **Arietta** and **Zelina** come with the convoy at the last of the mustering — the merchants made room for them on one of the carts when the tavern had refused them a bed. Arietta rides silent on the left side of the cart with her eyes on the road ahead and a blanket drawn to her chin; Zelina sits beside her and does the talking for them both — a quiet word for the driver who had taken them aboard, a nod for the party across the gap.
3. The convoy rolls out in its usual order: the three cultist carts at the head, the merchants' wagons through the middle, and two traveller carts at the tail — one of them **Darla** and **Gus**'s cart, Nuberroca's self-assigned post since the Trollclaws. The goliath takes his place at the near wheel without a word and stays there as the line stretches out onto the Trade Way, the tavern shrinking at his back.
4. Sinsong is away ahead of the caravan on his own trail; he had cut north off the main road days earlier after the **Company of the Boar**, intending to catch them before Waterdeep. The party does not expect him back on the main road before they reach the city themselves.

\column

## 2 - Spilled cargo
_~13:00, 15 Nightal 1489 DR_

1. Two days' march after leaving Barlon's tavern, the convoy reaches **The Way Inn** — the Trade Way's hardened mid-stretch waypoint, a walled compound on the road's western side atop a grassy plateau. Three bridges lead up to gatehouses the size of small keeps, each armed with catapults; the inn stands stone and tile-roofed inside the walls under a lookout tower. The gates are shut and the compound closed to travellers while the renovation works are under way, so the convoy pulls off the road under the walls for a lunch halt — food out of the wagons, beasts unhitched to graze.
2. As one of the cultist carts rolls off the road into its halt position, a wheel bumps over a stone and jolts a crate loose from its lashings; the crate falls and cracks open on the ground, and the lid comes up long enough to show a spill of wrapped jewellery — items of worked gold padded in wool — before one of the cultists sweeps a cloak across the whole crate in a single motion and closes the spill back out of sight.
3. Mialee is across the gap first — she steps in from the nearest cart with her hands open at her hips, offering to help settle the lashings. The cultists wave her off hard: one plants a palm on the cloak atop the crate, the other puts a shoulder between her and it, and the refusal lands twice over, hands and voice — *we have it, we have it, step back*. Amidamaru closes from the other side a heartbeat later, kinder-voiced and open-handed, and meets the same wall — the two cultists shift to face him without lifting their weight from the crate, and the second *no* lands harder than the first.
4. Nuberroca drifts over a stride later on the same pretence — a loose strap on his own cart, he says, wanting a word on how the tie-downs had failed. Two more cultists step out from the next cart with their hands on their belts to make the count on their side even, and the words come at him before his hand gets within reach of the cloak — *they have it, they do not need help here*. The goliath holds the eye of the lead cultist driver for a long heartbeat before he gives ground.
5. With the party backed off the cultists tie the cloak down over the whole crate and lash the broken lid under it so no second glance can find the join. They stay posted around the cart through the rest of the halt, and when the convoy hitches up and rolls on by afternoon the lead driver's eyes do not leave the party's carts until the Way Inn's walls fall behind them.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 24}}
\page

## 3 - The long road north

1. The road climbs further north bordering the Misty Forest and, in the distance, they can glimpse the mountains near the Delimbiyr river. The path is clear and the weather bearable, and they advance without incident for several days.
2. Across those mornings the two sisters work the carts one by one — a greeting, a question, a drawn-out thread of family and road and where-are-you-from with everyone who answers them. By the fourth day they have been through every merchant, every guard, every traveller who will sit with them.
3. Nuberroca spends the leg riding ahead of the three cultist carts and dropping back behind them in turns, making a point of being where they can see him. He finds the cult drivers' eyes on him more than once. One of them — the lead cultist driver — holds the look longer than the others and does not break it.
4. At each night's camp the convoy rings up tight — merchant and traveller carts settling within a few paces of each other — and the party sits down to dice, bread and small beer with the merchants and the gnome Radecere, always looking for gambling; the sisters sit on the edge of these fires and listen.

## 4 - Into the pass
_~13:40, 21 Nightal 1489 DR_

1. The mountain pass of the Delimbiyr river is a treacherous one, a thin snaking gravel path with steep cliffs on one side and the roaring waters far below on the other. The convoy threads it single-file, the drivers setting the brakes a notch tighter and the guards closing up to the wheels.
2. On the thinner stretch of the pass the cart where Nuberroca is riding lurches into a lean; a rear wheel is loose on its axle and the whole cart tips toward the drop. Ahead on the pass, the lead cultist driver has turned in his seat to watch over his shoulder.
3. ***[EPIC]*** The goliath jumps with feline reflexes and sets his back under the frame of the wagon, holding it there with his own weight and the strength of his arms while **Anton**, who was walking in front of the cart, pulls the horses to help take the cart to safety.
4. The near guards rush to hold the axle with the barbarian, and between them they walk the cart back off the shoulder and down the pass to the wide cleared ground beyond, where the rest of the caravan has drawn up to wait out the single-file stretch.
5. With the cart secured, and Darla comforted by Gus, Amidamaru goes under the axle. The nails that pin the wheel into its socket are not there — not sheared, not broken, pulled clean out of the wood by someone who had the time to work them.
6. The tortle straightens from the wheel and tells the party his conclusion on the findings: the sabotage is deliberate, and the wagon had been in sight of the caravan all night.

\column

## 5 - First blows

1. Kaelthar does not wait for the party to settle a plan. The cultists are a few places up the row and he reads the sabotage as their work — he summons his draconic wings and takes to the air across the caravan, flying over toward the cultist wagons.
2. Over the last cultist cart he pulls the wings and drops onto it with all his weight — the fall meant as a strike. The landing goes heavy and ugly, crashing across the frame rather than onto the bed, but the wood opens under him where he lands.
3. The cultists around the broken wagon have their blades and crossbows drawn before Kaelthar is back on his feet. They close in around the paladin, weapons up but held, shouting curses and foul names at the dragonborn.
4. ***[EPIC]*** Nuberroca goes in behind Kaelthar on foot. He crosses the gap between the carts in a rush, striking the first cultist guard who turns to meet him, and takes the man's head clean from his shoulders in a single stroke. The swing was meant to stop the man, not to end him — but a goliath's weight behind a halberd's blade was more than the cultist's slight frame could hold.
5. With the head on the stones the rest of the cultists forget their restraint at last. Blades come at Kaelthar and bolts fly at Nuberroca, their voices turning to steel.
6. Across the length of the convoy the caravan's guards raise crossbows at the goliath and the dragonborn. They have seen a fellow cart guard cut down without reason and they side with the innocent dead: the first shafts come in low, aimed for their legs rather than their chests, a wounding stop and not a kill. Two shafts find Nuberroca's thigh — and a blue glow comes up over the punctures, closing them shut almost as fast as they open.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 24}}
\page

## 6 - Fire in the cloud

1. Mialee sees the shafts flying and makes up her mind. She lays a thick fog across the three cultist carts — guards and party alike lose their lines of sight through the cloud — breaking the crossbowmen's aim and covering the beheaded body where it has fallen.
2. The caravan's shooters hold their fire. The crossbows stay ready across the length of the convoy, but no bolt flies into the cloud for fear of finding a friend in it.
3. ***[EPIC]*** Inside the fog Kaelthar draws breath and empties a cone of dragonfire across the two shapes that have closed on him. The fog flashes red from the inside for an instant, and the screaming comes a moment behind — the two of them go down burning on the stones and the cloud thins around them for a heartbeat before it closes back.
4. Amidamaru has not crossed into the fight. He holds ready at the fringe, watching for a chance to stop it before more people die. Eguzkilore holds beside him, out of Mialee's cloud: the caravan guards are the ones he has stood watches with across the trip, and he is not going to put himself on the wrong side of them by an unconfirmed suspicion.
5. ***[EPIC]*** Mialee closes on the nearest cultist through the fog while her ethereal hand searches the broken cart. The hand lifts the lid on a crate under the cover — chalices, religious icons, bags of small gems and jewellery stripped from statues, the cargo the party has been chasing since Greenest. Her blade finds the cultist in the same breath; he drops to the stones senseless but alive.
6. Through the cloud Mialee sees Kaelthar ringed by blades and crosses toward him. She closes on a cultist at the paladin's flank; they exchange blows in the fog, and she drops him senseless to the stones — but his blade had found her on the way down. The hit is enough to pull her focus from the cloud, and the fog thins and dissipates on the wind.

\column

## 7 - The caravan turns

1. With the fog gone the caravan takes in the sight: three dead by the broken wagon — two still smouldering, one beheaded — and two more down unconscious nearby. From the bed of a wagon at the front the lead cultist driver throws his own version of the fight across the convoy — the party are robbers, they have come to rob the caravan, they have killed his companions — and the crossbowmen set the next shafts for killing shots as some of the caravan guards close to the carts with their swords out.
2. **Sulesdeg** and **Eldkin** come across at the two biggest shapes in the fray — the goliath and the dragonborn — with hands open for the grab rather than weapons drawn, iron cuffs ready at their belts. Eldkin reaches for Kaelthar beside the broken wagon; Sulesdeg closes on Nuberroca with his hands empty.
3. Sulesdeg closes the last step into Nuberroca's reach aiming for a grapple — and the goliath moves first. The polearm comes up in a single swing and drives through Sulesdeg's shoulder before his hands can close; the two of them hold a long heartbeat at the end of the shaft, and then Sulesdeg draws his massive sword and comes in on Nuberroca swinging, but the strokes pulled short of killing blows — a blue glow appears over Nuberroca's cuts, partially closing them. Eldkin and the rest of the caravan guards drop the grapple and pull swords with him.

## 8 - The leader's bolt

1. Meanwhile, across the broken wagon the cultist still on his feet fights Kaelthar; his blade comes over him in a single pass and the paladin goes down in the dirt beside the frame.
2. Mialee closes on the remaining cultist on Kaelthar and drops him with a swift move — her third strike of the day, soft enough to leave him breathing. The only cultist still on his feet is the lead cultist driver, firing his crossbow from the bed of a wagon at the head of the caravan.
3. Amidamaru breaks from his place at the fringe and lays a healing spell into Kaelthar's chest — the paladin's wounds close enough to keep the breath in him.
4. The leader's bolt finds Nuberroca while the goliath is fighting Sulesdeg; the shaft lands hard in his side, and he stays on his feet with the bleed opening on him.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 24}}

\page

# Session 25 {{float:"right" 2025-10-26}}
{wide}

## 1 - The fight continues
_~14:00, 21 Nightal 1489 DR_

1. The mountain pass fight is still raging. Six cultists are down — three dead, three dropped by Mialee's blades under her fog, now thinned. Kaelthar is on the ground; Amidamaru's healing has kept the breath in him. Nuberroca holds **Sulesdeg** off behind the broken wagon, a bolt in his bleeding side.
2. Nuberroca blurs to mist and reappears behind the near cart for cover. Sulesdeg — wounded while trying to arrest him unarmed — now has his sword out and is swinging to kill; the lead cultist works the surviving caravan guards against the party, shouting that these adventurers are robbers who have murdered his companions. A second crossbow bolt catches Nuberroca at the lip of his cover.
3. Amidamaru reads Sulesdeg coming through to finish Kaelthar on the ground and steps across to catch the captain's wrist — a hand on the arm, *"please, bear with me,"* no blow. Sulesdeg pushes through regardless; he throws himself across Kaelthar, pins him down, and snaps a pair of irons on his wrists. Kaelthar tries to answer with a tail-strike from under the knee but the angle is wrong and it goes nowhere.
4. Mialee has climbed up on one of the carts. She throws down a convincing-looking parchment — an illusion of a mandate sealed with the **Order of the Gauntlet's mark** — and calls out that they are the ***Heroes of Mansken***, sent by **Ontharr Frume** on a sanctioned mission against the raiders of Greenest and Greenfields. Her unseen hand works the lid of the nearest crate to show the plunder, but the lid comes up on rations. The lead cultist takes the opening — *"they're liars, they're deceiving you with illusions"* — and the caravan guards hold, undecided.
5. **Eldkin** advances on Nuberroca with a pair of irons in her hand. Nuberroca swings as she approaches but misses. The dwarf then drops the shackles, draws her spear, and charges the barbarian.
6. Amidamaru takes the lead cultist to the ground in a grapple. Mialee follows and sets a dagger to his throat, telling him to confess with a look hard enough to make him flinch, afraid. Her unseen hand lifts the lid of another crate while she speaks — jewellery, chalices, religious icons in precious metal, some of them still bloodied.
7. On the other flank Nyx goes to **Lai** and tells him to trust her, she is going to help calm the fight. She crosses behind Eldkin, tells her to stop — they are going to solve all this — and grapples her, Nuberroca flanking from the other side. The barbarian then unspools the rope out of his pack and binds the dwarf while Nyx murmurs that it will not be long, soft words close by her ear.

\column

## 2 - Chests and pendant

1. The fight ends with the lead cultist on the ground under Mialee's dagger and Eldkin bound in Nuberroca's rope beside him. Sulesdeg finally comes off Kaelthar, lifts him up to unlock the irons on his wrists, pushes past him without a word, and goes to Lai to swear quietly at him about what has just happened. On the way past, Amidamaru closes on him and lays a hand on his back — a soft light passes through Sulesdeg, the wound in his shoulder closes under it, and the captain walks on as furious as before.
2. Amidamaru goes through the dead cultists' pockets. There is nothing of note on them beyond their arms, except that one of them is wearing a fine golden dragon pendant. He pockets it.
3. **Beyd**'s instruction is that the party should prove the goods are what they say. Sulesdeg sets his sword into the lid of one of the crates and splits it open; what spills is what the party has been saying — chalices, religious icons in precious metal, bags of gems prised from temple statues, a few pieces smeared with old blood. The four lead merchants — **Edhelri**, **Noohar**, Lai, and Beyd — pull apart from the rest and talk it over.
4. Beyd's first proposal, overheard, is to bury the cultists on the road and share the plunder among the surviving merchants and guards. Amidamaru cuts in — the gold has to go back to the villages it was taken from, through the Order of the Gauntlet; Nyx steps up behind him with the same line — they have tracked these cultists all the way from Greenest and will not see the loot broken up at the roadside.
5. ***[EPIC]*** As Amidamaru's words carry — *the Order of the Gauntlet* — Nuberroca stands tall and lets his frame grow to giant size behind him; he hauls the furs off his shoulders and shows the great fist blazoned on the breastplate underneath.
6. Nuberroca rounds on the merchants — the Company of the Boar they had hired for protection bolted the night the trolls came, the party and the caravan guards paid for it, and now these same merchants want to carve up stolen gold between them. Mialee opens the door a crack to gifting the humble family a coin or two, but no more than that.
7. Lai sides with the party against the carve-up — pack the crates into two carts, abandon the third for parts, run the whole load to Waterdeep, and let the city's justice resolve the ownership.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 25}}
\page

## 3 - Papers of the dead

1. Searching the wagons for papers Amidamaru turns up a note that names the destination as ***Carnath***, a roadhouse along the Trade Way past Waterdeep. Mialee remembers *Castle Naerytar* as destination, though no one in the party can place where it is. Amidamaru also prises three or four rubies and small stones from the loot for his own scroll work.
2. The practical work takes the rest of the daylight. Amidamaru patches the broken family wagon out of parts cannibalised from the one being abandoned. Nuberroca brings the humble family forward — **Gus** and **Darla**, with **Anton** travelling with them — and moves their wagon to third position so he can keep eyes on both the family and the cultists at once.
3. **Paco** — the cultist guard Amidamaru had grown fond of, scorched during the fight — is laid out apart from the others at the tortle's insistence. Amidamaru closes his eyes and covers him with a blanket. Nuberroca — still large from the breastplate moment — heaps the other two cultist dead in a pile and sets the surviving suspect propped against one of them, *"we'll talk later, criminal"*.

## 4 - A buried cultist
_~16:00, 21 Nightal 1489 DR_

1. The party splits into two groups to work on the four live captives. Nuberroca, Mialee, and Eguzkilore take the apparent leader — the one Mialee had on the ground — out past the carts; Amidamaru, Kaelthar, and Nyx take the other three to the opposite side.
2. Eguzkilore digs a pit and the three of them put the lead cultist in it up to the neck, blindfolded with a strip torn off his own shirt. Nuberroca lies down face to face with him and asks whether he would rather speak now and die with honour or die like the rat he is. The cultist's answer comes level: *"Tiamat's kingdom will come soon, and my sacrifices will not be in vain. I have no fear of death"*.
3. Nuberroca lets the fury take him to scare the prisoner — but the cultist holds against fear and intimidation both; he answers only with the dogma of his cult — the world will be a better place under the dragons. Nuberroca plants the lance a few inches from the cultist's face: *"you are this close to your goddess, right now"*. The cultist does not break.
4. ***[EPIC]*** Mialee sets a small wooden chest down on the ground beside the cultist's head and conjures an illusion out of it — four scorpions, clambering over the rim and walking toward his bound-eyed face. Nyx, as the illusion carries them in, lays a sound of her own under it — the dry rasp of claws on gravel, so close the cultist feels it on his cheek. The cultist breaks. The caravan's destination was always to cross Waterdeep and go on a little further to Carnath Roadhouse; the goods were for the Lady Dragon, whose colour he gives as black.

## 5 - The pendant trick

1. On the other side of the camp Amidamaru hangs the cultists' dragon pendant around his own neck, slaps the prisoners awake, and casts a charm across all three of them at once; two shake it off, the third — the sergeant — settles into looking at Amidamaru like a friend. Amidamaru leans into the role, pendant forward, and asks the sergeant who he is supposed to meet at Carnath Roadhouse.
2. The sergeant has nothing to give him. He is not a cultist at all — a mercenary hired out of Baldur's Gate to run with the caravan as far as Waterdeep, no questions asked about who paid him or what the cargo was. He has watched his companions die, he does not want to go the same way, and he asks only to be released and allowed to walk to the next town on his own feet. The sense of him sits sincere, so the party lets him go.
3. Amidamaru turns to the second — a true believer. Pendant forward, the tortle presses him on the cult's work and who waits at Carnath; the cultist answers with doctrine — Tiamat's rule will be better than any king's, the gold is needed for her return, and the party have killed without trial and are no cleaner than the cult. Nyx steps up at Amidamaru's shoulder — if the mediators lie, if a dragon-ruler would enslave rather than care — but between the two of them they get only more doctrine. *"I'd rather die than betray my own,"* the cultist says at the end, and Amidamaru leaves him tied against the cart.
4. Nyx takes the last of the three aside and asks him in a low voice what he can offer for his life. His answers come back the same as his companion's — the goods are for the Lady Dragon, colour black. Kaelthar, close enough to listen in, asks the dragon's name and the cultist reluctantly answers: **Rezmir**; he then asks whether they are guarding sacred eggs for the lady, and the cultist tells him no — he would consider that an enormous honour. Nyx slaps the cultist once across the face and leaves it there.
5. At dusk, with the interrogations wrapped and the prisoners tied behind the carts, the three dead cultists burn under Kaelthar's dragon-breath — the two in the pile and Paco where he had been laid apart — so nothing comes back out of the mountain at night. Amidamaru shapes a small glowing stone to mark Paco's resting place.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 25}}
\page

## 6 - Throats cut
_~19:00, 21 Nightal 1489 DR_

1. The caravan agrees, with some reluctance, to stay with the party; they can tell from the way the merchants look at them now that something has shifted. The convoy is too far behind to reach the next town before dark and makes a cold camp off the road halfway in. The watch is organised badly — Amidamaru first, Nyx second, Eguzkilore for the long end, Nuberroca, Kaelthar, and Mialee not actually on the rotation at all because the decanting of hours did not get worked through properly.
2. The party wakes to find the cultists tied behind the carts dead in their bindings — throats cut, cleanly done, only one of the three left alive. When the survivor is woken he panics at the sight of his dead companions; Mialee backhands him demanding what he saw and the man swears, shaking, that he knows nothing. Nuberroca puts his experience with corpses on the bodies and determines the death at about five hours earlier — Nyx's watch, by the counting-back.
3. Nyx walks the ground around the cart as the camp wakes. She can read the trace of someone who came up on the carts well before her patrol passed, and the patrol tracks that walked across those tracks afterwards — her own, going by — but the ground is bare stone and the trail does not carry beyond that. Kaelthar casts his sense out for any undead or celestial lingering nearby and finds nothing. Nobody saw anything on any of the watches.
4. Mialee goes around the camp while people are breaking fast, looking for blood on anyone's clothes. She finds a smear on the sole of Eguzkilore's shoe — a single mark, nowhere else on him. He claims he must have stepped in it while looking at the body. The sense of him sits uncertain, but she lets it rest.

\column

## 7 - Daggerford
_~13:00, 22 Nightal 1489 DR_

1. The caravan makes Daggerford four hours after breakfast, where the merchants settle to lose the day in town and set out again the next morning. Sulesdeg and a knot of guards stay with the parked carts. Nuberroca has the surviving cultist tied against his chest facing outwards all the way in — like a baby in a sling, so the prisoner has to look at everything Nuberroca looks at — mouth gagged at Mialee's insistence. Mialee stays close while Nuberroca carries him into the tavern and drinks beer in his face, drool falling on him: *"you thought death was the worst thing that could happen to you"*. Every eye in the place is on them.
2. The errands move quickly. Eguzkilore buys arrows at a stall but otherwise sticks close to the carts; Amidamaru fills Mialee's order for incense and takes a few extra for his own work; Nuberroca asks after rope and Nyx hands him hers off her belt — she has plenty. Amidamaru also asks after a smith in town for a commission he intends to settle.
3. Nyx passes Eguzkilore the magical longbow she has carried since the golden stag crossed their path and takes his ordinary longbow in exchange; neither of them makes a fuss about it, the trade is just quietly done over a drink.
4. The three bandits Nuberroca had kept walking tied behind the caravan since the roadside ambush weeks back — the leader, his captain, and one of their men, the band that had come at the convoy with the strange metal pistols — are cut loose and left in Daggerford.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 25}}
\page

## 8 - The elf at the gate
_~09:00, 23 Nightal 1489 DR_

1. The next morning the caravan goes out light. The noble with his mage and his guard are stopping in Daggerford for their own business; the mercenary hired by the cult has walked off before the caravan rolled out; and the two sisters — **Arietta** and **Zelina** — are gone. Nobody saw them leave and nobody remembers when they last saw them. Nuberroca jokes that it might have been the sisters who did the cultist in the night.
2. There are also two new faces in the caravan where the departures leave gaps. A small gnome woman with knives sewn into the folds of her clothes who moves fluidly and watches the party — Nuberroca particularly, with his strapped prisoner — with open attention. And a man dressed in black, hooded, who speaks to no one and travels alone.
3. As the carts are rolling out an elf comes down the road toward them at a hard gallop. He is tattooed on the face and carries more knives than the party can count — not drawn, kept — and asks the Daggerford traffic quickly as he rides, person by person, about a dwarf. He stops at Lai and Eldkin: *"I'm looking for a dwarf like you. He has a mark here — a tree tattooed on the nape of his neck"*. He turns to the great blue goliath with the human bound to his chest and hesitates.
4. Nuberroca lifts the strapped prisoner by way of answer. The elf takes one look, says flatly that the thing bound to the goliath's chest is a human and the goliath himself the strangest thing on the road, then names what he is after — *"I'm looking for a dwarf. He thinks he's a god. He calls himself Sinsong"*. Nuberroca's grin changes.
5. The barbarian rolls up a sleeve — Sinsong healed the scar across his arm. The elf fires back that Sinsong probably put it there in the first place, because he is a bastard. Nuberroca invites him to ride on with the caravan: last they saw of the dwarf he was on the back of a chicken, so he will likely turn up. The elf wheels his horse away — if the party finds him, tell him **Elodin** is looking for him — and rides out at the gallop he came in on, north, the same road the caravan is about to take; the convoy rolls out behind him.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 25}}

\page

# Session 26 {{float:"right" 2025-11-02}}
{wide}

## 1 - Guards on the road
_~10:00, 23 Nightal 1489 DR_

1. The caravan rolls north out of Daggerford on the tail of **Elodin's** visit — the elf gone ahead at the gallop, the merchants still shaken by the threat he carried for Sinsong, and the two sisters **Arietta** and **Zelina** gone in the night without any word [S25:8.1-4]. Nuberroca still has the surviving cultist strapped to his chest, mouth gagged [S25:7.1]. **Jamna** the small gnome and the hooded man who speaks to no one travel in the gaps left by the departures [S25:8.2]. "Nyx" rides with the party as she always has [input:GM].
2. A tight-knit company of guards intercepts the convoy on the road [recap:Nub:1-2]. They move with discipline — a sergeant reshuffling them like puppets across the field, calling positions as fast as the fight unfolds [recap:Nub:3][input:Sergio:1]. The party puts the whole unit down.
3. Two of them live long enough to be questioned. Their story comes back ordinary and thin: they are paid-up guards whose coin had stopped coming, who turned to the road to make up what was owed them [recap:Nub:4-6][input:Sergio:1]. The party leaves them where they fell.

## 2 - Sinsong out of the sky

1. Later in the day **Targutai** cuts down out of the sky overhead and Sinsong slides off the saddle to the road — back with the party after the long stretch away in the north [GM][input:Richy]. He arrives on the wing rather than along the road; Elodin has ridden ahead on the same trail, naming him by name, and the dwarf has taken the sky [input:Richy].
2. Amidamaru does not let him draw breath. The first thing he asks — before Sinsong is even fully settled — is everything the dwarf learned up north about the **Company of the Boar** [WA Pablo 2025-11-02 14:54]. Sinsong's answer comes back clipped and short, half the thread held back behind his teeth [GM].
3. Eguzkilore already has a card to play: someone in Daggerford had told him the Boar were in the city, ahead of the caravan on the same road [input:Richy]. The ranger lays it down; Sinsong takes it flat-faced and keeps whatever he had been going to say to himself.

\column

## 3 - A griffon flies off

1. The road rolls on through Nightal's deepening cold; the next day's travel grinds out behind the convoy without anything worth the breath of a retelling [GM].
2. One morning Targutai goes wild in the harness-line — jaws on a carthorse's neck, hard enough to open skin, then another horse, then another [GM][player:Pablo]. Amidamaru snaps a quick working over the nearest wounded horse and the beast vanishes from sight, out of reach of the griffon's eye; the trick holds, and that horse walks the rest of the morning unseen and unharmed [player:Pablo].
3. Sinsong puts himself between the griffon and the carts and talks him down, barely holding him before he can turn on another mount [player:Pablo]. Once the griffon has settled, Amidamaru works along the harness-line tending the wounded horses, while the merchants give the party dark looks the rest of the day [player:Pablo].
4. Later in the stretch the griffon is quiet for a span, and then without warning takes to the air and does not come back [GM].

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 26}}
\page

## 4 - Bones in the breakfast
_~08:00, 25 Nightal 1489 DR_

1. On the morning of the second camp beyond Daggerford Nuberroca sits with a bowl of porridge in his lap [recap:Nub:9-11][input:Sergio:2]. Jamna steps across, puts her dagger into the bowl, and lifts out a thin curved thorn — a twisted ring of it — that is slowly opening as the moisture of the gruel reaches it [recap:Nub:11-13][input:Sergio:2]. She says it plain: *"os han intentado asesinar"* [recap:Nub:14][input:Sergio:2].
2. The party's attention sharpens. Nyx, standing nearby, rounds on the gnome fast — speaking against her, pointed, unlike the Nyx the group knows [recap:Nub:15-16]. Jamna does not answer the charge directly; she asks only that the party meet her after nightfall, and walks away [recap:Nub:17][input:Sergio:3].
3. Later in the day Eguzkilore asks Nyx for one of her berries [input:Richy]. She says yes, then no — tells him flatly she has none on her [input:Richy]. The ranger lets it sit; Nyx without a single berry is the first odd note of the day, though the slip only lands later, in retrospect [input:Richy].

## 5 - A whisper that answers from a box
_~17:00?, 25 Nightal 1489 DR_

1. At nightfall Mialee and Sinsong walk out to Jamna's appointed spot to hear what the gnome has for them [recap:Nub:17-19][input:Sergio:3]. Jamna lays out other things — none of it touching the Nyx at the camp [recap:GM:3-9].
2. Mialee throws a whisper mind-to-mind in the middle of the exchange, addressed by name to Nyx — a simple invitation to join them [recap:Nub:19-21][input:GM][input:Richy]. The answer arrives back from somewhere behind them, not from the halfling shape across the camp: *"estoy atrapada en algún tipo de caja"* [input:Sergio:4]. The Nyx standing with the rest of the party does not react at all — she never heard the whisper [input:GM].
3. Mialee throws the same whisper to Nuberroca, telling him the halfling at the camp is not Nyx [input:Sergio:4][recap:Nub:21-22].

\column

## 6 - Darla's scream

1. Nuberroca is up and driving at Nyx before the message is halfway through [recap:Nub:21][input:Sergio:5]. The halfling turns to run; the barbarian catches her, forces her to the ground, snaps shackles on her wrists, and hauls her back to Mialee and Sinsong to be put to the question [recap:Nub:22-23][input:Sergio:5].
2. Between the two of them the false Nyx gives it up — a shapechanger in Nyx's shape — and the real Nyx whispers back through Mialee again that she is inside some kind of box, somewhere in the wagons [recap:Nub:23-27][input:Sergio:6]. The impostor opens her mouth and lets out a shrill scream meant to put fear into them; it does not land [recap:Nub:28-31][input:Sergio:6].
3. Then **Darla** — the wife from the humble family wagon Nuberroca has been riding guard on [S25:3.2] — walks up to the group and lets out a scream of her own [recap:Nub:30-32][input:Sergio:6]. A second shapechanger, come to pull the first one free [recap:Nub:32][input:Sergio:6][input:Richy]. Combat opens on both of them.
4. Nuberroca is on Darla with the halberd. **Gus**, the family's husband, throws himself between the barbarian and his wife — not knowing what she is — and the backswing catches him clean [recap:Nub:35-38][input:Sergio:7]. Gus dies on his own doorstep, accidentally this time, under Nuberroca's blade [recap:GM:2][recap:Nub:38][input:Sergio:7].
5. Sinsong throws his voice across to Nuberroca — a short murmured word — and the barbarian's shoulders drop; he slumps flat on the ground, asleep where he knelt, his grip on the shackled halfling going slack [WA Sergio 2025-11-02 23:33][input:Richy]. The false Nyx twists loose and tears away into the dark; Sinsong is up and after her, pale light flaring around him [derived:WA Sergio 2025-11-02 23:33+S27:1].
6. Eguzkilore does not move through any of it — he stays on his heels, watching, holding his hand for something stranger [recap:Nub:40-41][input:Sergio][recap:GM:11-12]. Nuberroca — when he has the breath for it — tells Sinsong to his face to stop throwing spells at him; he is through with magic landing on his own skin [recap:Nub:41-42][input:Sergio]. The scene breaks there — Nuberroca down, Gus dead, the doppelganger running into the dark [derived:S27:1].
