NOTES: Deadwinter does not exist (Claude hallucination). Seems like I made a mistake and added a day that should not be there, review thoroughly and merge in the narration. Start and end dates should be preserved.
Describe briefly end of year festivities.
Review fully, possibly Claude hallucinations everywhere
Also, possibly span until Hammer 2 to reach the start of S31 and make it match (to compensate for the removed day)

# Session 30 {{float:"right" 2025-12-07}}
{wide}

## 1 - On the north lot
_~09:00?, 30 Nightal 1489 DR_

1. Breakfast at the tavern behind them, the party walks the north quarter to the wagon-lot where **Mia** has been standing watch overnight [Td:2-3]. As they close on the fence Mia steps out behind them from a rooftop she had been crouched on, in the hostiles' blind spot [Td:4-7].
2. She reports: in the whole lot the private guards never touched their wagon through the night — no cargo in or out, no visitors — though the lot itself is loud with other wagons being hitched for the north run [Td:8-11]. There are many private guards, not city watch [Td:9-11].
3. Sinsong lays a quiet working on himself to burn off the last of Elodin's compulsion [Td:14-16]. Eguzkilore sweeps the rooftops for watchers and catches only a hooded figure moving on business of its own, not following them [Td:18-24].

## 2 - The whistle swap

1. Nuberroca turns on Sinsong and asks after the magic whistle Sinsong had passed him months ago [Td:36-55]. Nuberroca reads his face [Td:56-60]: Sinsong is lying about the whistle, flatly [Td:58-66].
2. Nuberroca produces his own whistle and demands an explanation [Td:67-70]. Sinsong confesses — he had handed Nuberroca the wrong one by mistake and now offers to trade for the real one [Td:71-82].
3. Nuberroca will not take Sinsong's word for it. He puts both whistles in his mouth and blows them together [Td:93-94]. Nothing comes of it [Td:95-100]. A nearby guard turns at the noise and starts drifting toward them [Td:102-104].
4. Nuberroca looks hard at the two whistles and recognises them — they match the ones the city guards wear round their necks [Td:106-110]. He is not amused [Td:111-113].
5. He fixes Sinsong with the warning hand he had used the time he broke the stone, and lays down the rule — no magic from Sinsong on his person again, ever, unless it is healing. *"Como me eches algún tipo de magia que no sea para curarme... me voy a enfadar mucho"* [Td:112-123]. With an inspiration already on him, Nuberroca passes it across the bond to Sinsong — *"por estar huyendo de mí"* [Td:130-137]. When the dust settles, Nuberroca carries both the Silver Whistle (the real magic one, back where it belongs) and the Signal Whistle Sinsong had tried to pass off [Td:140-150].

## 3 - At the caravaneers' guild

1. The party talks the shape of it out on the lot. The Order of the Gauntlet has no local contact here; Carlon is dead and the Harpers in Waterdeep are not organised yet [Td:203-212]. The objective is unchanged — follow the stolen wagon north and track where the Cult of the Dragon is hauling its treasure [Td:250-258]. They will hire on as escorts to the next caravan, break off at any divergence [Td:300-305, Td:494-500].
2. They push into the caravaneers' guild. An old, half-balding manager works a desk buried under papers, running sums with an impressive dexterity, barely looking at the pages [Td:306-314]. He prices the work: five gold a decade for escorts, three a decade for construction, two gold bonus on completion. Eight to nine days up to the Carnath station, then two months on the road itself — forty-five gold a head if they come back whole [Td:316-337, Td:355-357, Td:425-437].
3. Sinsong tries to talk the man up to six. The manager asks the arguments; Sinsong offers that it costs him only six per decade and the party likes money, really [Td:386-410]. The pitch does not land [Td:542-544].
4. Nuberroca steps in with braggart's weight — *"Soy el famoso Nuberroca... somos los héroes de Greenest, rebautizados en los héroes de Mansken"* [Td:409-413]. He has Sinsong flash the revivify diamond; Sinsong, flustered, produces instead a small stone Eguzkilore had given him [Td:416-419]. The fee stays at five, with a one-off bonus for Nuberroca's visible strength if they sign [Td:462-466].
5. Kaelthar arrives at the counter and is caught up in a whisper [Td:471-487]. The party commits — six escorts on the next caravan, the one the stolen wagon rides with [Td:528-533, Td:633-644].
6. On the way, Nuberroca checks with the lot's valet-keeper about the wagon they came for. He claims Anton as a hired driver, their own man; the valet replies that Anton had described a different pair of men as the pickup, so he cannot release it to them [Td:570-600]. Next caravan to carry the wagon leaves at noon, about three hours off [Td:600-604, Td:616]. Nuberroca sets up where he can keep an eye on the fence [Td:618-620].

## 4 - A crate on the run

1. As the party signs the sheets inside the office, a private guard at the doorway holds the manager's attention with an abrupt stumble and loud bangs against the wall — a diversion [Td:647-651, Td:701-712]. Outside at the wagons another guard snatches a crate from one of them and runs with a second guard at his heels, carrying as if their lives rode on it [Td:714-726].
2. Nuberroca is after them in a stride, bellowing *"¡Al ladrón!"* [Td:726-728]. The surrounding guards converge on the sound, but it is uneven — several speed up toward the chase, and a few others, who had seemed worried earlier, visibly ease their pace [Td:770-780, Td:823-828]. Sinsong peels off to the party's wagon to guard what remains; Eguzkilore runs after Nuberroca [Td:782-786]. Nuberroca enters his rage [Td:788-793].
3. The thieves zig-zag through the parked wagons and Nuberroca loses the line — the lot is their ground, not his [Td:794-820]. He breaks off and scrambles onto a rooftop to look down over the maze [Td:829-837]. From the eaves he catches them crouched under a wagon with the crate [Td:838-841].
4. Nuberroca steps across the air in a cloud and drops onto them from above [Td:842-847]. Between shouts and lost bladders the thieves let the crate go [Td:849-855].
5. Nuberroca takes the one carrying the box by the arm and tells him in a quiet voice that if he returns the crate to its wagon and goes along quietly, nothing happens to him [Td:856-864]. The thief folds — his private guards do not pay enough to die for a crate [Td:859-861]. He walks the crate back under Nuberroca's shadow. To any curious guard, this was a misunderstanding — the crate had to be moved; it had the wrong wagon in mind [Td:869-872].
6. Watching the thief return the crate, the guards who had chased hard look at the ones who had slowed — some drop their eyes, some do not [Td:872-879]. One of the straight ones steps across and tells the thief *"no deberías coger las cosas que no son tuyas"* and walks on [Td:878-881]. Nuberroca pulls that guard aside: say nothing to Anton when he arrives — the party means to ride the next caravan behind the wagon, and the pickup should not know he is watched [Td:903-911].

## 5 - A sphere and a short rest

1. The party takes the hour on the lot, waiting for the noon caravan. They buy rations at seven silver a piece [Td:928-933, Td:995-997], refill waterskins and shuffle weight between packs [Td:998-1004, Td:1069-1088].
2. Sinsong spends his rest with the smooth sphere Mialee had lifted off Jorath in Waterdeep [Td:1057-1060]. He licks it and turns it in his hand — inside the glass a cloud of mist turns no matter how he moves the sphere, always pointing in the same direction [Td:1061-1067].
3. Sinsong hands it to Eguzkilore. *"Creo que esto lo vas a necesitar tú más que yo"* [Td:1067-1068].

## 6 - The caravan heads north

1. Noon of 30 Nightal — the last day of 1489 DR [Td:600-604, Td:616, Td:1198-1199, Td:1264]. Six wagons move out onto the High Road, the party among them — six escorts, twenty-four construction workers, all under the High Road Company's banner [Td:1198-1211, Td:354-357]. The three who had seen the Greenest cave-camp close faces on the drivers and workers as they come up; none triggers a memory [Td:1226-1236]. Waterdeep at their backs is drawing in for the turn of the year — braziers kindling in the squares, shutters half-closed, families focused on the long night ahead [GM]. The caravan's departure is quiet against it — a few farewell their men, most do not [Td:1237-1241].
2. The road is paved and patrolled; the travel runs to eight or nine days up to the Carnath station [Td:426, Td:1262, Td:1272-1275]. The caravan has no leader; thirty-six people walking the same road toward the same work [Td:1292-1310].
3. The first day passes without incident. Company barrels of food and water, no cook. The party camps for the night [Td:1309-1324].

## 7 - A hand in the pocket
_~10:00?, 1 Hammer 1490 DR_

1. Second morning on the road. Nuberroca takes a turn driving one of the wagons — his hand with horses is well above the ask and he puts flourishes on the harness, swerving the small bumps on the paved road while the actual driver dozes under his hood [Td:1326-1342, Td:1349-1355].
2. Mid-morning, Nuberroca feels a hand on his pocket [Td:1380-1385]. He checks his pack — a small object has appeared he does not recognise [Td:1389-1392].
3. He pinches it in his thumb and flicks it like a chip toward Sinsong's bald head further down the wagon [Td:1393-1395]. Sinsong catches it before it lands — *"¡Qué buen culo! Si quieres algo de mí, tendrás que reconquistarme"* [Td:1395-1398].
4. Sinsong confesses the game — he had tried to put a conjured spark on Nuberroca's weapon so he would have a lantern after dark. He touches the lance; it brightens in Nuberroca's hand with a steady light [Td:1435-1446]. The glow holds for an hour, and the sun is high, so the gift mostly amuses the rest of the caravan [Td:1450-1452].

## 8 - A horse by the road

1. Further down the road the caravan passes the carcass of a horse by the left side [Td:1469-1480]. Something very large has broken it [Td:1476-1479].
2. Eguzkilore reads the ground and the body. He draws it out — a heavy stone rolling down the mountain to the right has caught the horse and its rider, flung the horse through to the water's edge; the rock went into the sea with it. The horse was ridden at the moment, and the attack is recent, within a day or two [Td:1491-1504].
3. He speaks it to the group. Sinsong wants to press on and not lose the caravan's pace [Td:1537-1540]. Nuberroca defers to Eguzkilore — he feels the alert in his gut when he sees a thing like this, but will stay on the wagon if the ranger is calm [Td:1542-1552]. Eguzkilore chooses to investigate [Td:1553-1555]. Mia and Nyx stay with the wagons [Td:1561-1562].

## 9 - The orcs come down

1. A bellow from the slope above and a boulder tumbles down the mountain at the wagons [Td:1577-1582]. Nuberroca drags the horses forward in time and the wagon lives [Td:1583-1600]; Kaelthar's wolf leaps and lands atop the stone with a flourish [Td:1600-1601].
2. An orc follows the boulder down the slope [Td:1607-1610]. Eguzkilore drops it with his bow before it reaches the road [Td:1611-1615].
3. Two ogres follow with giant javelins that look more like young pines than throwing-weapons [Td:1641-1644]. The first lays a javelin into the wagon's wood — the shaft bites but Nuberroca holds — then roars and plants a second in him across the next breath [Td:1649-1660]. Nuberroca drops the reins and climbs down to meet them.
4. Sinsong puts his back behind a wagon and reaches across the road with a word of compulsion aimed at both ogres at once — one shakes it off, the other turns and begins to retreat under Sinsong's *"huir, insensatos"* [Td:1957-1966].
5. ***[EPIC]*** Eguzkilore crouches to his waist behind a horse, strings the bow, and lets fly with everything he has in the time he has — four arrows one after another, closing on the near ogre like a hail. The creature is left bristling with shafts and limping, barely upright [Td:1935-1950 ?ord].
6. ***[EPIC]*** Nuberroca brings the halberd down twice across the same arc — the first attacker at his flank drops to the first swing, and his own god's dark fury carries into the follow-through to take the next [Td:1980-1996]. As he swings, dark-light tendrils climb his veins and the wounds across his chest close under his skin. *"De nada por creer en Sinsong"* [Td:2005-2012].
7. The distant ogre throws javelins back. The first takes Nuberroca deep across the chest and the second lands close behind [Td:2155-2165]. He is heavily wounded now.
8. Nuberroca sets down the halberd, picks up a crossbow and tries to put a bolt into the ogre at range. The first bolt catches it for a sliver; the second fumbles cold [Td:2173-2237].
9. ***[EPIC]*** Kaelthar closes the ground on his wolf, meets the last ogre and brings his javelin down with a dragon's roar — the strike opens the creature's chest like a flower, smoke rising from the dragonborn's nostrils as the ogre falls [Td:2309-2331].
10. On the other side of the wagons the guards' crossbow volleys have cut most of the oncoming orcs down before they reached the road; two guards leap on the last crawling ogre and finish him. Three guards wounded lightly, one badly, no deaths. Thirty silver among the orcs and swords too crude to keep [Td:2322-2348].

## 10 - False gods

1. The party takes the hour by the road to catch their breath. Sinsong tells Nuberroca across the camp that this time he will not heal him [Td:2369-2370]. Nuberroca steps across, taps his own chest, and the dark-light climbs his veins again — his wounds close under his own hand [Td:2372-2374].
2. Sinsong lets it go. *"No quieres que te desalbe, no quieres que te cure, no quieres que te resucite"* [Td:2374-2376]. Nuberroca does not soften it — *"No quiero falsos dioses, y si no me vas a resucitar, devuélveme lo que es mío"* [Td:2376-2378].
3. He names the cowardice as he saw it — Sinsong hid behind the wolf while two ogres came at him [Td:2378-2381]. Sinsong insists he handled both ogres with the word of compulsion [Td:2380-2381]. Nuberroca will not grant it — the second ogre could not have reached him under any circumstances; the compulsion only tipped the one that was already out of range [Td:2381-2385]. The quarrel ends with the caravan rolling on, noon of the second day, the next ten unaccounted for [Td:2397-2404].

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 30}}

\page

# Session 31 {{float:"right" 2025-12-14}}
{wide}

## 1 - A compass in the hand
_~09:00?, 2 Hammer 1490 DR_

1. Before the caravan rolls, Nyx takes a short rest to finish identifying the smooth sphere she has been turning over in her hand [Td:1-10]. The mote of light inside holds its line no matter how she tilts the glass — a compass, pointing one way and one way only [Td:14-15]. She slips it onto her person and keeps it [Td:17].
2. Nuberroca makes a point to the party before they move out: the ogres from the day before threw trunks like twigs — keep distance if more come [Td:28-31]. Kaelthar heads off the wrong way out of camp and has to be turned round, pointed north [Td:34].

## 2 - On the caravan line
_~mid-morning, 2 Hammer_

1. The caravan rides in file — four workers and two guards to each wagon, the party counting for the guards of their own [Td:122-125]. The one they suspect of hiding the stolen cult cargo sits in the middle of the file [Td:117-120]. **Ardred Briferhew**, a lean sergeant with the look of a man who gives orders by habit, rides at the head; the caravan has no formal leader but the guards defer to him [Td:177-183].
2. Ardred asks the party plain whether they mean to be known as the Greenest heroes or ride anonymous. Nyx and Eguzkilore keep their heads down; Nuberroca and Kaelthar wave the name [Td:140-170]. Those who kept quiet pass; those who did not get looked at by any drivers who had cause to look [Td:473-479].
3. Walking the line to feel out the drivers, Nyx catches the face of the man on the suspect wagon — **Dillian** [Td:186-199]. She slips to Mia under her breath that he belonged to the cultist camp back at Greenest [Td:286-295].
4. The party trades it round quietly. Nuberroca argues for *an adaptive, escalating strategy — upward in violence* [Td:306-307]; Eguzkilore and Sinsong push for watch-and-follow and let the cargo lead them [Td:303-305, Td:322-327]. Nothing is settled; the plan holds at "shadow Dillian, do not spook" for now [Td:285].
5. Ardred lets drop that at Carnath Roadhouse the party will meet **Bog Luck**, the semi-orc tavernkeeper — big as Nuberroca and known for a fist-match. The guards are already betting on who wins [Td:246 ?ord, Td:468-473].

## 3 - A berry for everyone
_~late morning, 2 Hammer_

1. Nyx walks a Goodberry round to each of the party, pressing one into each palm — a quiet handoff, the day's revival stock [Td:328-333].
2. **Craith**, one of the sergeant's guards, drifts over asking for food — he has hunger like the rest [Td:334-335]. Nyx digs a bird egg she had saved for months out of her pack and gives it to him [Td:346-348]. Craith beams and tucks it away — *"Lo guardaré para la feria"* [Td:349-351]. Nothing of it then, but Craith marks it.

## 4 - Amidamaru's quiver
_~mid-afternoon, 2 Hammer_

1. ***[EPIC]*** Riding on, Nyx realises she has a quiver on her hip that is not hers — Amidamaru's, with a sealed note tucked in among the arrows [Td:373-385]. She reads it apart from the others. The note is a goodbye [Td:389-391]: Amidamaru means to fight the cult his own way, not by following at the party's heel, and he has gone [Td:390-391]. She has been carrying it since the morning Amidamaru walked out of Waterdeep without a word [Td:388].
2. Nyx takes stock of what the quiver carries and distributes [Td:392-399]. The pearl Mia had once lent Amidamaru goes back to Mia — *"Creo que era tuya, un préstamo que le hiciste"* [Td:393-395]. Three platinum coins go to Eguzkilore — *"Pensaba que no las iba a recuperar ya"* [Td:398-402]. Two unidentified metallic spheres, each with a small button, stay with Nyx; without triggering them she cannot work out what they do [Td:415-422].
3. Nyx keeps the quiver itself. *"Quiero quedarme el de Amidamaru por si algún día vuelve para dárselo"* [Td:440-441].

## 5 - Shapes behind the trees
_~nightfall, 2 Hammer_

1. The day draws down to dusk and the party catches movement in the trees ahead — large shapes trying to hide behind trunks too narrow for them, badly [Td:487-490]. Three or four are visible; more are not [Td:490].
2. Sinsong leans in, taps Nuberroca's sword, and lays a conjured spark on it so there will be light when it is wanted [Td:502-507]. The weapon brightens — and so does Sinsong's bald head, close enough to catch the same working [Td:574-577]. The surprise is gone; the guards see what is coming [Td:524-525].
3. Four ogres charge from the fore of the trees, with orcs behind them [Td:528-529]. Ardred takes a knee, lays a crossbow bolt into the first one with a fluid gesture [Td:600-606]. Nuberroca enters his rage before the ogres cross the road [Td:530].
4. A fifth ogre jogs in from the back of the woods [Td:681-684]. Mialee throws a wall of fog across the party's flank and dashes behind it, sending Echo above the mist so she can see through the bat what she cannot see through the fog [Td:689-725].
5. Nyx keeps the near ogre marked and lays a venomed shaft into it from range [Td:618-635 ?ord]; the poison turns the giant to tear at itself [Td:997-998]. A second arrow catches it in the throat, and the thing crumples trying to pull it free [Td:1007-1014]. First kill of the fight.
6. Kaelthar closes in on **Fenrir**, his summoned wolf, and comes in behind Nuberroca. He brings a javelin down with the dragon's roar carrying through the strike — the point drives straight into the near ogre's open mouth, a critical [Td:794-824]. One round further on, a returning javelin takes Fenrir through and the wolf dissipates into smoke [Td:951-964].
7. Sinsong plants his back behind a trunk and reaches across the field with a word of command aimed at two ogres by Nuberroca — *"huir"* [Td:1133-1137]. One shakes it off; the other turns to flee [Td:1136-1139].

## 6 - The halberd and the head
_~nightfall, 2 Hammer_

1. Nuberroca sets the butt of his spear in the ground, pins it with a boot, and waits. One ogre charges him and runs itself clean through, out the other side [Td:1037-1041].
2. ***[EPIC]*** He takes the next with the halberd across the same arc — his dark fury pulling tendrils up his veins and the wounds on his chest closing under his own skin as the swing lands [Td:860-868 ?ord].
3. On the fleeing ogre, Nuberroca does not wait. A reaction catches the thing turning, and it falls with his spear buried in its back [Td:1296-1302].
4. Eguzkilore drops to a knee behind cover and sends a hail of arrows into the sixth ogre as it closes [Td:1225-1248]; the creature kneels, bristling with shafts, and begins trying to rise [Td:933-934 ?ord].
5. Across the melee, Mialee closes on a flanking ogre with her scimitars — the first strike cuts into a crit [Td:1305-1324], then she slips free of the ogre's reach in a cutting feint and vanishes behind a trunk [Td:1326-1339].
6. ***[EPIC]*** Ardred ends it. He runs the length of the road, calls the guards into position with their crossbows, and vaults the last ogre — the first stroke rocks it on its heels, the second catches it ducking and the head comes off clean onto the road [Td:1531-1540]. He wipes the blade on the ogre's hide and sheathes it in one motion [Td:1538-1540].
7. Six ogres dead on the road, no deaths among the caravan [Td:1540-1554]. Two guards badly wounded — **Jarth** cut deep, **Isaiash** lighter but hit — the rest with scrapes [Td:1546-1549]. The ogres carry only trophies from past killings: a bashed guard's helmet, a tin cup; nothing worth taking [Td:1626-1633]. Ardred claps Nuberroca on the shoulder and promises the first round of beer at Carnath Roadhouse on him [Td:1572-1577].

## 7 - Creo en ti, Sinsong
_~dusk, 2 Hammer_

1. Sinsong gathers the wounded guards close around him before the caravan moves off. He will heal, he says, but they have to raise their hands and call it out — *"Creo en ti, Sinsong"* [Td:1646-1662]. The guards, confused and bleeding, do it. Jarth and Isaiash fold in; Craith plants himself at the back [Td:1667-1672].
2. ***[EPIC]*** The light rises off Sinsong's bald head as he goes to Goku-pose and the healing lands [Td:1670-1685]. On the first pass Isaiash stabilises, Jarth bleeds to a stop; a second pass brings Jarth back further and Craith comes up whole and radiant, declaring himself a new convert on the spot [Td:1693-1706]. Craith asks whether he can swap his standing-stone for a place among Sinsong's faithful; Sinsong accepts [Td:1704-1710].
3. Mia steps in and tries to monetise the moment — the healing costs two gold [Td:1727-1731]. Craith reaches into his purse and comes up with four electrum. Sinsong refuses the coin: *"No quiero tu dinero, Craith, solo con tu adoración me sirve"* [Td:1732-1733]. Sinsong's long-cold honour notches up a point in the moment [Td:1737-1743].
4. Nuberroca hoists Sinsong by the collar like a child and sets him back down. *"Esta tarde lo has hecho bien, esta mañana no tanto. A partir de ahora te llamaré Sinsong el apósito"* [Td:1711-1721 ?ord]. Kaelthar, from a few paces back: *"El tiritas"* [Td:1722].
5. Ardred, watching, remarks it is good to have a healer along for the road and gives the order to move — they will push to reach the night camp before the light is gone entirely [Td:1746-1753].

## 8 - A sending into the dark
_~night, 2 Hammer_

1. The caravan reaches the rest zone late. Ardred lays the watches: Mialee and Sinsong first, then Isaiash and Craith, then Nuberroca and Nyx, Eguzkilore last [Td:1759-1762].
2. Before his head goes to the saddle-pack, Sinsong sends a whisper out across the road and the night — a working aimed at Amidamaru, somewhere beyond reach. *"Amidamaru, ¿dónde te has metido? Aquí se te echa en falta, te echamos de menos. Vuelve pronto"* [Td:1773-1783]. The party then sleeps.

## 9 - Dillian knows the goliath
_morning, 3 Hammer_

1. Nuberroca wakes with his veins their usual colour, the dark tracery gone [Td:1784-1786]. The caravan is already moving. He drops back along the line and asks Nyx under his breath whether the suspect driver survived the night; he did [Td:1799-1803].
2. Mia reaches Nyx by sending across the wagons — Dillian has recognised Nuberroca, pins him to the fight against Langdedrosa at Greenest. Be careful: he knows who the goliath is [Td:1894-1902]. Nyx passes the warning along [Td:1902-1903].
3. Nuberroca's answer is to lean further into it. He plants himself at the head of the caravan and begins to recite his old killings aloud — *"¿Te acuerdas de ese combate, Nyx? ¿Te acuerdas de cuántos matamos en la cueva?"* [Td:1905-1911] — mugging through the morning. Ardred watches him from a few paces behind, half-smiling, half not; Isaiash asks if he is well [Td:1913-1920].

## 10 - The moon in the sphere
_through 3 Hammer_

1. On the march, Nuberroca pulls out a small smooth sphere he had picked up off the ground after the ambush and shows it to Nyx [Td:1818-1822]. It is nothing like the compass — motes of light inside form a semicircle beside a dark edge, as if a sun or moon were caught mid-rise [Td:1836-1842, Td:1861-1868]. When held, the shape faces the holder [Td:1844-1845].
2. The party passes it hand to hand through lunch — Nyx, Sinsong, Mia all taking turns, each watching the semicircle present itself fresh to whoever holds it [Td:1857-1875, Td:1961-1971]. Dillian casts furtive looks at the party across the fire; the conversation at his side goes quiet whenever Nuberroca passes close [Td:1978-1983].
3. Dinner is boiled legumes and a half-passed strip of meat; the party watches a round of darts for coppers at the night camp [Td:1984, Td:2015-2017]. The watches run Nuberroca and Mialee first, Sinsong and Nyx second, Craith and Isaiash third, Sarek and Jarth last — Ardred, as ever, pulls none [Td:2117-2123]. Before sleeping, Sinsong lays a day-long ward of iron under the skin on Nuberroca, Kaelthar, and himself [Td:2202-2204].
4. Mia pulls the thread the party could not: the picture matches the moon's phase. Nuberroca holds the sphere up in the dusk — the semicircle inside is the moon in the sky, waning as the real moon is waning [Td:2081-2092, Td:2222-2234]. By morning the sphere shows a full disc; the sun is up [Td:2219-2229, Td:2293-2298]. A clock, by moon and sun.
5. Ardred rolls them out at 8:30 on the morning of 4 Hammer 1490 DR after a frugal breakfast; the caravan is on the road again before 9 [Td:2302-2304].

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 31}}

\page

# Session 32 {{float:"right" 2025-12-21}}
{wide}

## 1 - The fairy and the empty well
_~07:00?, 4 Hammer 1490 DR_

1. Eguzkilore steps off from the camp to relieve himself in a bush, and the piss finds a small winged thing hiding inside. The fairy climbs out drenched, cursing him in a language he does not know, and makes a sequence of gestures that are plainly obscene even without translation [Td:1-3]. He feels something go out of him — not any part of his body, something hollow where it should not be [Td:4, Td:35] — and the fairy wings itself invisible and gone [Td:5-7].
2. The curse is a slow bleed on his soul: every time he reaches for magic beyond his basic knack, there is a chance a piece of him goes with the casting. Being no spell-slinger, it will cost him nothing in practice, but it does not lift of its own accord — it is a curse, and it stays [Td:12-29].

## 2 - Backgrounds at the fire
_~07:30?, 4 Hammer_

1. Sinsong proposes that at each day's breakfast one of them share something of their past — a small ritual to know each other better, since the party has been on the road together a while and the backgrounds have barely surfaced [Td:55-61].
2. Sinsong goes first. His village was a mining town inside a cave, and his people struck a black stone deep in a dig — brilliant and heavy and taken for valuable. Within days half the village was stone itself. He has the piece and no idea where to begin looking for the cure [Td:72-85].
3. Mialee comes from Waterdeep. When they first met her she had two orphans in tow, children she had been training for a trade; she keeps up with her master there, took the recovered cart to him, and he intends to use it to raise more spies [Td:91-96].
4. Eguzkilore says plainly he is in it for fame and glory — young still, taking an *Erasmus* of sorts before he has to settle to work in the forests of his tribes [Td:101-102]. Sinsong tries to pitch him on a higher purpose; Eguzkilore declines [Td:103-107].
5. **Ardred** rides in and gives the order: *"vamos a partir"*, dawn-to-night marches, and the reminder that the arrival at Carnath is set for the 9th of Hammer, five days out [Td:110-115].

## 3 - A shower of stars
_~20:00?, 4 Hammer_

1. Eight hours of march bring them to full nightfall. The waning moon still holds more than half its disc, the sky is clear, and above them the stars are streaked — the party sees shooting stars, and most of them running the same way, west to east [Td:129-134].
2. Over the course of dinner the meteors multiply. What began as the odd flare becomes one every five or ten seconds; some burn large enough that the party can see them nearly reach the ground [Td:144-171]. Nuberroca's sun-stone — the sphere he picked up after the ogre ambush — shows a moon on its face that no longer matches the moon above, since the icon is fixed and the real moon has turned [Td:189-195, Td:1601-1608].
3. Ardred is unbothered — in these stars, the old men said, falls the iron that mixes with earth-iron to make blades more permeable to magic, and he would be pleased to see any of it strike nearby [Td:251-256]. The party is less sanguine; Mialee asks whether staying put is safe, Sinsong wants a tree to shelter under [Td:245-248 ?ord, Td:257-258].
4. Mialee, Sinsong, Eguzkilore, and Nyx take the horses to go find a shelter — a cave in the hillside, if the mountain will offer one [Td:287-298].

## 4 - A cave on the hillside
_~22:00?, 4 Hammer_

1. The first cave they find is small — twelve or thirteen could squeeze in — and they push on [Td:325-328]. Half an hour later they come on a larger one: thirty or forty people would fit, though not the wagons or the horses [Td:359-363]. The walls carry old animal-rubs; no beast lives there now, but outside the mouth there is a goat-path with real traffic on it [Td:369-371].
2. At the cave's entrance the ground has a small fresh crater, the width of a closed fist, and a stone sitting in it — porous, black, warm but no longer hot, the earth beneath it not even crystallised [Td:376-389].
3. Mialee crouches to it. With her gloves on she picks it up — a plain weight at the touch, nothing felt through the leather [Td:390-401]. Sinsong, when he sees the colour, tells her to drop it: *"se parece a la piedra de mi pueblo"* [Td:405-411]. She refuses to leave it behind and wraps it in a cloth without touching her skin to it, and stores it [Td:415-420].
4. Eguzkilore volunteers to ride back for the camp while Mialee and Sinsong hold the cave; Nyx goes with him so the whole return party keeps night-vision between them [Td:432-444].

## 5 - Spiders out of the dark
_~22:30?, 4 Hammer_

1. Mialee gathers wood and lays a fire in the cave [Td:457-466]. While it builds she walks the floor and finds a half-eaten mouse — bites smaller than the mouse itself, and more than one [Td:514-526]. Sinsong, picturing a swarm of fire-ants, makes for the mouth of the cave and starts loosing flame at the walls on his way out [Td:536-556].
2. A hairy leg comes out of a hole in the ceiling. Then another. A spider the size of a dog descends on silk, a second shows itself at a nearby hole, and a third surges from a crack in the wall [Td:574-608]. The first spider's strike catches Mialee off-balance and her bow falls from her hand; a web lashes past her and misses [Td:596-614]. A bite catches her and the venom slides off — she rolls with the strike and the fangs find little of her [Td:615-630].
3. ***[EPIC]*** Sinsong plants his feet and calls down a circle of radiant guardians around himself, holding every ally and every creature in the cave outside its reach save the spiders [Td:639-666]. The light rolls out from him and the three spiders take it in full — one dead on the ground, the other two bolting back into their holes [Td:669-686]. Sinsong finishes the dead one with a bolt of fire, to be certain of it [Td:697-700].
4. He keeps the circle up and walks the cave with the light at his heels. Small insects die as he passes; four spider corpses in all drop from the ceiling holes across the length of the cave, and from one of the gaps a slow drip of thousands upon thousands of dead ants [Td:703-734]. He spreads a pair of radiant wings and goes along the ceiling to sweep what he had not reached from the floor [Td:719-724]. By the time the circle runs out, the cave is clean of anything that bites.

## 6 - Mis argumentos narrativos
_~00:00?, 5 Hammer_

1. Eguzkilore reaches the caravan and tells Ardred they have found a cave that would shelter them all; it is half an hour by horse [Td:446-479]. Ardred shrugs — the caravaneers are not frightened and are already turning in. If Eguzkilore can convince them, go ahead. Eguzkilore rolls his best persuasion and makes a hash of it; no one lifts their head from a bedroll [Td:481-501].
2. Kaelthar takes him up on the wolf for the ride back [Td:506-508]. At the cave Mialee shows Kaelthar the swept floor and the burning spider corpses outside; Sinsong, straight-faced, tells Kaelthar that Eguzkilore has caught syphilis — he soaked a fairy from above [Td:810-814]. Eguzkilore cannot heal it: too holy for Sinsong's reach yet [Td:816-826].
3. Mialee asks Eguzkilore where the rest of the camp is. He answers with a straight face: *"Mis argumentos narrativos han sido tan magníficos que se han quedado todos durmiendo ya"* [Td:844-847]. The lie goes down without comment.
4. Later, Sinsong tries sending his voice out across the night — *"Ander, venid a la cueva. Los meteoritos están cayendo cada vez más cerca. Quizás no sobreviváis esta noche"* [Td:875-878]. No answer comes back. Eguzkilore, pointedly: *"Están sobaos. Ya te lo he dicho yo"* [Td:887-889]. The four turn in with Eguzkilore on first watch; by the small hours the meteors have thinned to nothing [Td:894-899].

## 7 - A second morning
_~08:00?, 5 Hammer_

1. Kaelthar wakes them for breakfast [Td:903]. Sinsong takes a turn at the storytelling: in his village his old master taught him brews and spells drawn from the plants that grew in their deep cave, and the master is stone now too. The magic itself, he says, comes from his belief that everyone can be made better [Td:943-947].
2. Mialee's turn. She was a child when the people of Waterdeep found her and took her in, and she means to give back to the city that made her what she is — *"un niño ladrón"* [Td:950-955]. Eguzkilore quietly cinches his pack straps in front of him and shuffles his hand to it [Td:956-957].
3. They ride down and rejoin the caravan on the road. Ardred asks how the night went — the stones fell close but not close enough to hurt; some of the workers wanted to go looking for the pieces to sell, but there was no time [Td:995-1005].

## 8 - Ardred on magic iron
_~13:00?, 5 Hammer_

1. Over the midday halt Mialee presses Ardred on the fallen stones: where would she take one to be understood? Any decent blacksmith in Waterdeep or Neverwinter, he says. The iron that falls from the stars runs stronger than the iron drawn from the earth; mixed, it comes out more permeable to magic. He cannot explain the smith's work — he only ever heard the men who did it talking shop [Td:1037-1050].

## 9 - Birds take flight
_~15:00?, 5 Hammer_

1. The afternoon's march is unnaturally quiet — none of the usual bird-noise, especially from the north. When they begin to doubt there is any life in the woods at all, a flock lifts out of a stand of trees three or four hundred feet off, and the party makes out several orcs moving on the caravan in a very bad attempt at stealth [Td:1066-1094].
2. ***[EPIC]*** Eguzkilore takes the first of them before the orc has crossed any ground — one arrow out of the drawn string, through the eye, dead on the first impact [Td:1097-1109]. His second shot into the next orc goes shallower, through the shoulder, and that one breaks into a sprint toward the party [Td:1110-1117].
3. Ardred stands back from the cart and watches. *"Claramente sois buenos combatientes, no creo que necesitéis nuestra ayuda"* — if that proves wrong he will step in, otherwise it is the party's fight [Td:1154-1157]. He does not step in.
4. Kaelthar presses forward and drops the first orc with a single stroke of the longsword, then steps through the mist to the next tree and cuts the second — the stroke lands but does not drop it [Td:1227-1270]. Sinsong flings a bolt of fire that passes Kaelthar's head and sears a nearby tree, but the winter damp will not take the flame [Td:1282-1284]. Mialee drops behind a trunk, breathing, and sinks an arrow into the second orc's back with the quiet-killing shot that is her trade — a one-stroke kill [Td:1286-1294].
5. The whole business runs some twenty seconds, no caravaner hurt [Td:1297-1298]. Ardred claps a slow applause from the cart. *"Magnífico. Me he quedado sorprendido sobre todo con la habilidad de vuestros arqueros"* [Td:1298-1301].

## 10 - Something ate the orc
_~16:00?, 5 Hammer_

1. When the party goes back to the orc bodies to recover arrows, one of them is gone. The ground where it had fallen is turned up — claw-marks, teeth-marks, a smear of blood — the orc has been torn to pieces and dragged. No tracks lead cleanly away [Td:1389-1393]. Sinsong reaches with a locate working at a thousand feet in every direction and finds nothing [Td:1403-1420].
2. Mialee, half to herself: *"Si antes se removieron las arañas que estábamos quemando y ahora se ha removido un cuerpo así de un orco, ¿nos puede estar siguiendo algún bicho?"* [Td:1399-1400 ?ord]. They press the caravan to move faster [Td:1437-1442].

## 11 - On the other body
_~16:30?, 5 Hammer_

1. At the second orc body Sinsong spots them: two creatures the size of mice, devouring the corpse at a rate no mouth that size should manage — fifteen or twenty seconds and the whole orc would be gone [Td:1452-1458]. They are not mice. The description that comes to the party is a shapeless amalgam of broken bones, raw muscle, and strips of hanging skin; each one different from the other [Td:1459-1462].
2. Sinsong throws fire at the nearer one as the first act of the fight; it reels [Td:1463-1465]. The second does not stop eating — the wounds the party puts into it close as it swallows [Td:1489-1492]. A second bolt from Sinsong and two arrows from Eguzkilore bring the first one down; it twitches twice on the ground and does not move again [Td:1493-1510].
3. Mialee reaches with a floating invisible hand to turn the dead one over, and gets a close look — a mouth lined with mismatched teeth, each tooth sprouting from a different kind of flesh. She vomits [Td:1522-1535].
4. The second creature breaks for the first body with its half-formed pincers; Kaelthar and Eguzkilore work it with arrows and it folds, muscle still twitching, into the dust [Td:1510-1549 ?ord]. Mialee pushes both carcasses off the road with the floating hand and cleans the hand [Td:1552-1556]. Eguzkilore refuses to go near either body to recover his arrows; Sinsong lays fire on the pile and they burn [Td:1559-1572]. The caravan beds down for the night without further incident; during the long rest Mialee turns another hour on the parchment she has been puzzling at and finally makes out its school — abjuration [Td:1612-1638].

## 12 - The horse that was taken
_~20:00?, 6 Hammer_

1. A day's march on, the caravan stops to camp beside something strange: a large depression in the dirt in the middle of the road, a wide dried-up blood-stain, and the horse's tackle — straps, yoke, bits — scored with bite-marks but all accounted for. The horse itself is gone [Td:1641-1646, Td:1659-1663].
2. The blood is coagulated; investigation puts the kill between one and five days back. Something tore a horse down here, pulled the carcass free of the gear, and took the body [Td:1655-1662]. Mialee, flatly: *"Estaré atento porque algo mata caballos por aquí"* [Td:1665-1666].

## 13 - Boneleechers at the fire
_~late night, 6 Hammer_

1. Round the fire they hear the same stone-on-stone they had heard before. Mialee, more awake than the rest, sees a small misshapen thing moving through the brush toward the camp — a kin to the two from the orc body — and alerts the party before climbing onto a cart to take cover [Td:1700-1713].
2. Eguzkilore puts an arrow into the nearest; it rocks the thing on its feet but does not drop it. A second arrow does [Td:1716-1731]. Sinsong tries to put a fear on the creatures and it slides straight off them; a bolt of fire does better [Td:1786-1800]. Nyx moves out of cover and takes one down in close with her blade, then plants a shaft into the one beside Nuberroca with a stroke that could not have been placed better [Td:1810-1819]. Nuberroca steps into his fury and goes for the one on him with the halberd; both swings bite only air [Td:1821-1831].
3. One of the creatures lunges at **Fenrir**, Kaelthar's summoned wolf. A single bite and the beast dissolves into smoke, and the Boneleecher falls backward in the dust — then its bones snap and recompose and it is on its feet again as if the fall had not happened [Td:1844-1857].
4. Another springs for Kaelthar on the ground; a bite catches a fold of exposed flesh and tears it [Td:1869-1876]. He feels the pull of it in him — a sickness beneath the wound that his Dragonborn constitution does not quite throw off [Td:1878-1886]. He gets up swinging; the first stroke of the longsword catches on the stones and he loses the second [Td:1898-1902]. Pinned by three of them, he takes a step through the mist to clear the ring and comes up outside their reach [Td:1904-1908].
5. Sinsong calls down his guardians a second time. The light rolls across the three creatures left standing; all three fall together. *"Limpiado"* [Td:1914-1942].
6. He crosses to Kaelthar and wraps him in an embrace. Kaelthar, patting Sinsong once on the back: *"Estoy bien, amigo gay"* [Td:1949-1952]. Kaelthar, back at the fire a minute later, catching the party's eyes: *"¡Qué joder, ya me habéis llevado a la sífilis, cabrones!"* [Td:1962-1964]. The carcasses go into one pile and Sinsong keeps laying fire on them until they are only ash [Td:1972-1996].

## 14 - El tiritas and the fairy
_~03:00?, 7 Hammer_

1. On the first watch Sinsong turns to Eguzkilore and asks about the fairy. Eguzkilore tells it plain — he was in the one place he should not have been, the fairy spoke in her language, and ever since there is a small hollow in him [Td:2015-2020]. Sinsong proposes a rite of apology on the spot, and asks Eguzkilore to beg the fairy's pardon and give a word of praise for Sinsong himself [Td:2024-2031].
2. Eguzkilore, grudging: *"Pido perdón al hada y gracias a las alabanzas de Sinsong, que vaya todo bien"* [Td:2032, Td:2041-2044]. Sinsong's bald head catches a glow, the hollow in Eguzkilore closes up, and the curse is gone [Td:2046-2051]. *"Lo que sea que te pasase ya no hace falta"* [Td:2047-2051].

## 15 - The Metagorger at dawn
_~05:20, 7 Hammer_

1. On the second watch, in the blue before sunrise, Mialee and Kaelthar hear a sound they have heard before — the hollow click of walking bone — louder now, mixed with the chittering screams of the creatures from the orc body. They move out on foot to look [Td:2082-2097].
2. ***[EPIC]*** What Mialee sees between the trees is three to six of the small bone-things fighting over each other — not quarrelling, eating. One is growing. In some fifteen seconds it has gained enough mass to crush and swallow a second and then a third; a fourth tries to flee. The thing that stands up from that meal has four legs with bone sticking through, two pincer-arms where none were before, and a new mouth large enough to use them — then it bounds after the one that fled, catches it, and tears it in half [Td:2100-2122].
3. Mialee slips back and wakes the camp [Td:2126-2130]. The Metagorger comes on at a surprising sprint, and Kaelthar meets its charge inside its reach — a stroke of the longsword takes a pincer off at the joint. The pincer goes into the thing's own mouth and the stump begins to grow back as it swallows [Td:2142-2158].
4. The first bite that comes for Kaelthar misses; the remaining pincer scrapes a weak score down his armour and finds nothing underneath [Td:2167-2171]. Sinsong, pushing up without his armour on, throws fire at the thing and lays a shimmer of warding across himself and Kaelthar in the same breath [Td:2190-2211]. Kaelthar hits it twice, the second with a divine burn under the blade [Td:2214-2224]. Mialee, caught without her bow (dropped from the fright of it), scrambles for it and plants an arrow once she has her hands on it [Td:2229-2230]. Kaelthar, driven: *"Me estoy coronando"* [Td:2258].
5. Two more arrows from Eguzkilore, another bolt from Sinsong, and Kaelthar brings the sword down a last time — the Metagorger folds, deformed, into the dust [Td:2237-2266 ?ord].
6. Sinsong lays hands on Kaelthar and the gash from the bite closes [Td:2268-2271]. A last spasm from the carcass brushes Kaelthar's armour and does not break it; Sinsong puts fire to it and the tree next to it, and the thing burns with the smell of hot chemistry rather than hot flesh [Td:2272-2280]. Mialee, flat: *"¿Cuánto falta para la taberna?"* [Td:2283-2284].

## 16 - Tracks in the mud
_~day, 7 Hammer_

1. Before the party makes a last long rest, Sinsong reaches out to feel for the cart that was stolen from them in Waterdeep. He finds it — in the caravan, with them, right there [Td:2370-2378].
2. The rain comes down hard; the horses slow, the cart-wheels catch in the mud, and the party's boots pick up a stubborn skin of it. The rain's one gift is the tracking. Line after line of small holes along the road match the little bone-things from the fire; longer, lonelier trails match the larger one. At one spot the party finds three sets of the small holes together, and nearby a different print — a half-skeletal foot the size of a tall man's — moving in a group with two of the larger prints. A third kind they have not met [Td:2393-2403].

## 17 - Carnath Roadhouse
_~12:00, 9 Hammer_

1. The last night in camp passes without attack; the heavily armoured ones sleep in their armour both nights running — none the worse for it in the morning [Td:2351-2371, Td:2409-2412]. In the morning the road brings them in sight of a large two-level building that is pointed out as Carnath [Td:2414-2417].
2. At the gate is a semi-orc nearly Nuberroca's size — **Bog Luck**, the one Ardred had told them of back on the first day of the march. The yard is already crowded with their caravan's wagons, going in one by one to be unloaded into the hall; the ground has frozen overnight and the goods will not sit out [Td:2444-2466].

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 32}}

\page

# Session 33 {{float:"right" 2026-01-04}}
{wide}

## 1 - A duel in the yard
_~13:00?, 9 Hammer 1490 DR_

1. Kaelthar connects to a yard already in motion: **Nuberroca** stripped and trading bare-handed strokes with **Bog Luck** in front of a crowd of workers [Td:1-3]. The punches are called non-lethal from the first exchange — this is a prizefight, not a kill [Td:107-108].
2. The two set in. Nuberroca opens with a swing that misses and a second that lands [Td:14-26]; Bog Luck's return runs heavier — a crit through for thirteen [Td:28-33]. A couple of exchanges on and Bog Luck is barely marked, *"fresco como una lechuga"*, while Nuberroca is already visibly tiring [Td:62-63]. The crowd outside is calling for the half-orc — *"¡Gogol! ¡Gogol!"* — and from the back of the yard Mialee is watching her bet come apart [Td:91-93].
3. Nuberroca throws himself into his fury and loses the discipline of the opening — the extra edge opens Bog Luck up but costs him the next return [Td:68-71]. By the time he goes for his next swing he is one hit from going down [Td:117-118]. He calls on a divine favour and thirty points of hurt knit closed in a single breath [Td:128-131].
4. ***[EPIC]*** Nuberroca immediately extends his hand across the ring — *"eres el ganador"* — and calls the fight: he will not use the favour to actually win a bout he does not need to win [Td:132, Td:142-143]. Bog Luck refuses the call outright, spits blood, calls what has already happened a warm-up: *"no ha estado mal el calentamiento"* [Td:133-139]. Then he returns the hand himself: *"me ha sido un placer pelear contigo, eres un buen guerrero, aunque no me ha gustado el uso que has hecho de tus habilidades mágicas"* [Td:140]. Nuberroca, back: *"no son habilidades mágicas, es el poder innato que tengo de los dioses"* [Td:141].
5. The semi-orc gets up on his own and walks off with a touch of unsteady on his step [Td:157-158]; the crowd slaps his back as he passes inside [Td:170-171]. One of the workers brings a cloth to tend him on the way; he waves it off — *"ahora no, hay cosas más importantes"* [Td:172-174].
6. Before the party disperses, Ardred's lot pay out the ride-wages: six pieces of gold each for the march from the ambush-camp to Carnath [Td:161-168].

## 2 - Nap and a message at the tavern
_~14:15, 9 Hammer_

1. Nuberroca retires straight to his room, bars the door with a chair, and sleeps [Td:178-191]. Sinsong takes a short rest at the same hour [Td:189-190]. Food is not part of the caravan's hospitality — the roadhouse serves it from the upstairs tavern at five silver a plate [Td:193-204].
2. Mialee goes upstairs with a cup of milk, picks a table near the fire, and listens for anyone from the cult. The read holds — she picks a message out of the chatter and keeps it to herself for now [Td:208-218].
3. The party have been told they have two days of half-rest before road-work begins; most of the afternoon is still theirs to spend [Td:221-224].

## 3 - A hunt and an eaten elk
_~15:30?, 9 Hammer_

1. Nuberroca proposes a hunt — meat for the kitchen and a diversion for the afternoon. Mialee goes with him; Sinsong comes running up some minutes behind, surprising everyone with a tracker's eye no one knew he had [Td:226-289]. The crowd at the roadhouse cheers them out, hoping for free meat [Td:259-272].
2. The three roll poorly for the woodcraft and spend the first stretch finding nothing [Td:272-285]. Sinsong puts them on an elk trail — hours old, not fresh [Td:287-299]. What they catch first is the smell: rot, and strong [Td:293-298]. Behind a stand of brush is the carcass itself, a bull elk, half-devoured and a few hours dead [Td:299-315].
3. Alongside the large bites that killed the beast, a few smaller missing patches look different — holes smaller than mouths that size should leave, more than one of them, spaced in a cluster that recalls the bone-things from the march up [Td:313-315]. Mialee, flat: *"qué pena que no esté Nyx aquí para descubrir qué tipo de bocado es este"* [Td:316-320].
4. The meat can still be used. Sinsong lays a working on the carcass — a glow at the bald head, a shimmer across the body — and the flies abandon it, except for the small cluster of suspect wounds where they stay interested [Td:338-363]. Nuberroca cuts the suspect flesh out and throws it aside, then opens the belly to finish the gutting [Td:366-379].
5. He wraps three hundred pounds of meat in the elk's own hide, lashes it over his shoulder with his rope, and brings it with them — speed cut to twenty-five with the load, but moving. The horns are winter-ruined and will not fetch [Td:421-440]. Mialee throws a prestidigitation across the lot of him so the party does not have to walk downwind: lavender and rosemary over the raw-meat smell [Td:436-438]. They decide to press on and find whatever made the big bites [Td:442-472] — Nuberroca pointing out to Mialee that the predator's hide might just cover what she lost on the fight [Td:461-462].

## 4 - The owlbear's lair
_~17:00?, 9 Hammer_

1. The trail runs clean for another twenty-five minutes and brings them in sight of a flat spread of ground littered with old carcasses — most devoured past the meat, down to the bones and the horns and the hoofs. A lair [Td:474-488].
2. The occupant stands up on two legs the moment it sees them — an owlbear, white, larger than the usual, roaring and already on the run [Td:530-544]. Sinsong catches up by the sound of it and finds the fight already starting [Td:546-548].
3. Kaelthar engages at the front, longsword drawn [Td:566-568]; he throws up a shield of faith around himself [Td:598], then swings in twice with the longsword and the second cut finds the beast [Td:586-612 ?ord]. Sinsong, with no time to prepare a real working, pulls an oil bottle from his pack, strikes a spark across it, and lobs the lot at a nearby thicket shouting *"¡fireball!"* The flame goes up on the thrown bottle [Td:620-631].
4. Mialee shoots once and finds the beast [Td:633-635], then slips into cover in the brush [Td:636]. Nuberroca waddles up at reduced speed with the full elk still over one shoulder [Td:642, Td:651], sets the short spear's point to the owlbear's flank for an opening strike [Td:649-653], and after a second poke settles on the spear-and-shield to wait for the beast to come at him [Td:671-676].

## 5 - Blood in the lair
_~17:30?, 9 Hammer_

1. ***[EPIC]*** The lair itself begins to come apart. From one of the piled carcasses something assembles — a shapeless amalgam of broken bone and tendon walking on four — and runs not at the party but at the owlbear itself [Td:687-694]. A second comes from the top of the lair and bites the owlbear in the arm; the owlbear screams and starts shaking the limb where the teeth went in [Td:695-699]. A third flanks around behind and lands a crit on it [Td:707-709].
2. One of the small ones peels off and sprints at Nuberroca from behind. It climbs the raised spear with a dexterity no bone-amalgam should have, pins his arm, and finds his bracer rather than the flesh under it [Td:737-748]. A larger kin — halfling-sized now, four legs — rises from the back of the lair and runs at Nuberroca first for being closest, then dashes past him for the owlbear [Td:749-754, Td:761-762].
3. Sinsong plants his feet and calls down the radiant guardians. The circle rolls out from his bald head; the small ones caught inside are struck by the light but none drop on the first pass [Td:782-815].
4. The owlbear's hide begins to show crimson bulges that swell and burst in a twenty-foot ring of fire-blood. The party is caught in it — Kaelthar's dragon-born blood takes the worst of the heat without harm; Nuberroca and Sinsong soak the rest [Td:1191-1222]. Both the shield around Kaelthar and the circle around Sinsong hold through the burn [Td:1223].
5. The large Metagorger closes with the owlbear, bites a strip out of its flank, and swallows — and the party can see the thing's wounds knit closed as it digests [Td:1245-1250]. Another one rises from the far side of the lair, this one closer to human-size [Td:891-895 ?ord], and a third halfling-sized kin tries to break through from the brush later [Td:985-991].
6. Nuberroca sheds the spear and shield to the ground and draws his halberd, going black with his fury [Td:910-912 ?ord]. One stroke light, the next hitting with a shimmer of divine light he turns radiant on the swing — the halberd takes a leg off the near Metagorger and one of the stump-legs drops to the ground [Td:1264-1273].
7. Kaelthar swings a cone of dragon-fire that catches two of the small ones in its spread; the third sits just outside the line and does not. Both the two in the cone take the full of it [Td:717-732 ?ord]. Later he brings the sword down with a divine stroke of light that cleaves a leg off a lingering Metagorger — the thing limps on but will not fall [Td:1442-1446].
8. The last of the Metagorgers takes a bite out of Sinsong before he can shift — a wound that leaves something more than the puncture under the skin, something the dwarf cannot read for himself [Td:1282-1297]. Sinsong closes the surface hurt with his domain's balm and walks the circle, passing the heal along to the others as he moves [Td:1355-1368].
9. Mialee's last arrow comes out of a thicket on the flank, catches the final creature in the neck as it leaps for Kaelthar, and the fight is over [Td:1476-1482]. The owlbear itself went down at some point in the middle of it; by the time anyone looks back it is already dead on the ground [Td:1383-1385].

## 6 - Picking the bones
_~18:30?, 9 Hammer_

1. The lair is still. Most of the owlbear's hide is torn past the point of selling and the carcass is only good for the beak; the carnomorph remains are left where they fell [Td:1484-1510].
2. Among the half-devoured kills is the body of what looks to have been one of the road-workers — gone looking for somewhere to piss and never returned. Nothing of value on him; the party has already been told that anything worth keeping goes into Bog Luck's locked box [Td:1515-1525].
3. Sinsong crosses to Nuberroca and wraps him in an embrace. The bald head glows and a round of hurt knits closed under the hands [Td:1526-1529]. Nuberroca, catching the trick, asks for a second one; the dwarf obliges, but there is no glow on the second embrace, nor on the third — the favour runs out one hug at a time [Td:1530-1542].
4. Sinsong clamps a light-cantrip on Kaelthar's armour as an improvised torch for the return. Kaelthar: *"¿que soy un gruesito?"* [Td:1598-1607]. Only on the way back does Mialee catch what no one burned: *"¿no deberías de haber quemado esos bichejos como haces siempre?"* — too late [Td:1596-1597 ?ord].

## 7 - The elk gone and the walk home
_~19:00?, 9 Hammer_

1. On the way back they pass the site of the morning elk. There is nothing left of it — not the bones, not the tendons, not the horns. The ground is dragged and bloodied, and the woods around rustle with something small or middle-sized that does not quite close with them [Td:1608-1625].
2. Nuberroca wants to look; Mialee refuses outright; Sinsong starts physically shoving him forward: *"corre, corre, puto Nuberroca"* [Td:1631-1646]. They push on and reach Carnath in another hour [Td:1647-1650].

## 8 - Sold at the tavern
_~19:30?, 9 Hammer_

1. In the tavern the owlbear beak sells quickly for eighty-five silver — twenty-one pieces each after the split, with no money-changer here to work it up into gold [Td:1660-1678].
2. The elk meat goes to the tavern cook, **Grisel Pit**: one silver a ration [Td:1696-1700], four rations kept back for the party's own dinner [Td:1718-1721], one hundred and forty-two silver for the rest — thirty-five pieces each [Td:1722-1727]. Sinsong, generous: *"es generoso Nuberroca, muchas gracias"* [Td:1729]. Sinsong offers Nuberroca one of the Sinsong-stones in return — *"por eso me gustaría compartir contigo una piedra de Sinsong"* — Nuberroca takes it and asks for a hug on top [Td:1731-1744].

## 9 - A dorm with four bunks
_~19:30?, 9 Hammer_

1. The warmer rooms are the upper ones — the animals in the pens below leak heat up through the floor [Td:1772-1773]. After some circling and door-slamming the party settle in one four-bunk dorm together [Td:1783-1823]. Sinsong sends word to Eguzkilore and Nyx to join them in the dorm when they can [Td:1855-1858].
2. Nuberroca, bored, winds up and punches the wall to test the stone. The plaster takes a dent; the stone behind does not shift [Td:1807-1818 ?ord]. Sinsong sighs and mends the plaster afterward so no one coming in notices [Td:1819-1820].

## 10 - Signals at the window
_~19:30?, 9 Hammer_

1. Settling into the bunks, Nuberroca cracks the window open and shouts a joke-alarm to the yard — *"¡cuidado, nos atacan!"* — then shuts it and announces he wants to sleep [Td:1861-1867].
2. Almost at once, the sounds come for real: running on the roof above the dorm, three or four pairs of feet; voices up there shouting *"¿dónde? ¿dónde?"*; from the yard below the metal clatter of guards drawing weapons [Td:1868-1878]. Sinsong trusts the guards to it, Nuberroca is spent and will not get up for a lie he already shouted, and Mialee has her own errands waiting [Td:1886-1898].
3. From the glass later they hear the real fight — a cry of pain, the ring of steel on something that was not armour [Td:1960-1961]. Nuberroca opens the window a second time, this time with a warning that actually means something — *"cuidado con esos bichos, si os muerden, os transformáis en ellos"* — closes the window and goes back to bed [Td:1964-1966].

## 11 - The storehouse chest
_~20:00?, 9 Hammer_

1. Mialee asks Sinsong for something that will let her move with less sound. He lays a benediction on her head — a glow at the bald, a cat's grace on her shoulders for the hour [Td:1906-1912, Td:1941-1944]. She slips out of the dorm [Td:1944-1948].
2. She lets herself into the ground-floor storehouse under cover of the yard's distraction [Td:2010-2015]. Inside is the ordinary freight of a road-crew depot — crates of tools, provisions, nothing worth lifting [Td:2017-2020]. A second door is locked; she picks it with her thieves' tools [Td:2021-2027], checks the frame for a trap (none [Td:2033-2039]), and lets it open a hand's breadth [Td:2047-2048].
3. Beyond is a stacked room of crates, chests, and sacks — the valuables Bog Luck had said he keeps apart [Td:2051-2056]. Mialee cannot pick the cult's crates out from any of the others; the cult uses the same freight boxes everyone else does [Td:2052-2056]. She picks the lock of one of the single-standing chests [Td:2079-2086] and finds, inside, coin bags and **two spheres** she cannot name — plainly magical to the hand that touches them [Td:2088-2100].
4. She takes the spheres and the coin, closes the chest, re-locks the lid [Td:2115-2118], slips back out the way she came, and watches a passing patrol climb the stair before stepping into the yard [Td:2155-2157]. She is gone close to half an hour.

## 12 - Eoman's leg
_~21:00?, 9 Hammer_

1. Crossing the yard Mialee sees **Wex** carrying one of the small bone-things on the point of his spear. He stops her: *"¿os habíais encontrado antes con estas cosas? ¿Por qué no habíais avisado?"* [Td:2163-2173]. Mialee says Ardred should have passed the word and that they themselves only ran into them that morning while hunting; Wex throws the carcass into the yard [Td:2174-2186].
2. Wex points her to the kitchen — **Eoman** was bitten and taken up to be tended [Td:2188-2191]. Mialee finds him beside Bog Luck, bleeding out, the better half of one leg severed and lying on the table [Td:2232-2261]. She tries Sinsong's grey stone first — Eoman takes it, drops it to the ground, and gives up on it — and she runs for the dwarf [Td:2201-2213 ?ord].
3. Sinsong comes down in his bedclothes, protesting the whole way [Td:2206-2225]. By the time he arrives the others at the table are stanching the hemorrhage with a tourniquet [Td:2262-2263]; Bog Luck has a hand in his pocket on what the party can see is a vial of red liquid [Td:2269]. Sinsong lays the staff over Eoman and casts; a vast roll, the hemorrhage stops in the same instant, and the muscle begins to re-form around the stump as the party watches [Td:2264-2268 ?ord].
4. Bog Luck slips the vial back into his pocket and takes Sinsong's measure. *"Está bien saber que tenemos un curandero en la venta, puede sernos muy útil"* [Td:2269]. He claps Eoman on the mended leg and sends him off to bed: *"ahora, ya estás fresco, a dormir"* [Td:2270-2273].

## 13 - A benediction for the rats
_~22:00? → 10 Hammer, ~06:30_

1. Sinsong steers Mialee and Eoman to the bar for beers [Td:2280-2290] and asks the barman what plagues the roadhouse. Grisel complains of rats in the walls — months of them, keeping him up [Td:2292-2308]. From the back of the tavern a regular shouts a crude blessing on the dwarf's generosity [Td:2275-2278 ?ord].
2. Sinsong spreads a pair of radiant wings behind him [Td:2310-2312] and walks the whole tavern — common room, kitchen, well-shaft — every pass of the circle killing the small lives inside the beams and the floors [Td:2358-2402]. Dead insects — ants, flies, little spiders, a bloom of aquatic bugs on the well's surface — rain down on the drinkers' heads and cover the water of the well [Td:2374, Td:2402-2405]. One drinker, looking up at the floating dwarf, protests to the room that *"los enanos no vuelan"* [Td:2377]; Mialee, deadpan, points at her mug and tells him *"esto es cerveza"* [Td:2384]. Grisel adjusts the price of beer by one silver *por el espectáculo* [Td:2388].
3. The party finally takes a long rest. The night rolls over without further attack, and the next morning the sun comes up at half past six on 10 Hammer. Road-work will begin the day after [Td:2425-2430].

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote Tyranny of Dragons | Session 33}}
